# Changes

zgx is on crates.io from 0.1.0 (`zgx = "0.2"`); this lists what changed for apps, newest first.

## 0.2.0 (2026-11-25)

A breaking release for Rust apps: what changes is under "Migrating from 0.1.10", each with what to write instead. No 0.1.10 example or template that loaded on 0.1.10 fails to load on it (21 examples and 10 template apps; the other five examples need a host of their own). What a Luau script may notice is listed there too.

### Highlights

- **Native modules** (zgx-module, unstable before 1.0): an app's own Rust, built apart from zgx and called from Luau.
- **Sound** (zgx-audio): a mixer with buses, fades and effects, behind an `audio` feature.
- **Text areas that hold chips**: atoms with icons, pictures and padding, as a chat composer's mentions.
- **Text and layout nearer the platform's and the browser's**: macOS text as heavy as macOS draws it, firmer text below 2x, box edges on device pixels.
- **Menus, tooltips, scrollbars and focus rings in each platform's shapes** on Windows 11 and GNOME.
- **Scrolling on macOS coasts as an `NSScrollView` does**, and scroll views copy their pixels under more of what floats over them.
- **Opaque 1x windows on a machine with no hardware GPU draw on zgx's software raster** (`ZGX_RENDERER=gpu` restores the GPU renderer).
- **Apps run from a folder or a bundle**: `zgx run` opens one, its window and look from `zgx.json`; `zgx build` writes it as a bundle, its native modules with it.
- **Streamed Markdown**: `ui.markdown` of a state keeps its blocks current in Rust.
- **Pointer lock, copies with HTML, images and an app's own types, and canvas text that wraps.**
- Not on crates.io: `zgx-fx` (effects), `zgx-video` and `zgx-dom` (the browser). Take every zgx crate from one revision of the repository to use them.

Published with it: `zgx-text`, `zgx-audio`, `zgx-module` and `zgx-module-macros`, all new, and the forks `zgx-harfrust` 0.5.4, `zgx-swash` 0.2.11 (new), `zgx-cosmic-text` 0.19.14, `zgx-taffy` 0.9.2, `zgx-mlua` 0.12.10, `zgx-vte` 0.15.4 (new), `zgx-alacritty-terminal` 0.26.8 and `zgx-arboard` 3.6.3 (new).

**Apps from crates.io get upstream winit, wgpu-hal and AccessKit**, not the copies patched in this repository; docs/releasing.md lists what that leaves out. Entries below about a patched crate reach only builds from the repository.

### Migrating from 0.1.10

#### Rust

Version requirements: `"0.2"` for every zgx crate together.

- **A value's strings, lists and maps have types of zgx's own** (zgx-script): `Value::Str` holds a `Str`, `Value::List` a `List`, and `Value::Map` a `Map` of `Field(Str, Value)`, where they held `Rc<str>`, `Rc<[Value]>` and `Rc<[(Rc<str>, Value)]>`. Their layout is stated, so code built apart from zgx can read a value where it is. Reading them is as it was (a `Str` is a `&str`, a `List` a slice, a field's `.0` and `.1`), and so are `Value::record`, `Value::from`, `field` and the rest. Code that matched a field as a pair, took an `Rc<str>` out of a value, or called `Rc`'s functions on a list changes: [docs/value-types.md](docs/value-types.md) has each case, before and after. A `Str` is not an `Rc<str>`: turning one into the other copies the text, so what keeps a value's string keeps a `Str`. zgx-script's own Luau cells are at or under what they cost before (most between 0.1% and 0.6% under).
- **A `SemanticNode` is made with `SemanticNode::new` and then assigned to** (zgx). It has a private field now, so a literal ending in `..SemanticNode::new(role, label)` no longer compiles, whichever fields it names: `let mut node = SemanticNode::new(role, label); node.disabled = true;`.
  Fourteen fields few nodes set (`text_selection`, `logical_parent`, `min`, `max`, `numeric_value`, `position_in_set`, `size_of_set`, `placeholder`, `document_selection`, `level`, `description`, `shortcut`, `active_descendant`, `table`) are in a `SemanticRare` it derefs to. Reading one, assigning one, `take()`, `clone` and `==` compile as they did. These don't:
  - moving one out, `let d = node.description`: `node.description.clone()`, or `node.description.take()` on a `mut` node;
  - a pattern naming one, `let SemanticNode { level, .. } = &node`: `let level = &node.level`;
  - two borrowed mutably at once, `(&mut node.min, &mut node.max)`: assign one, then the other.
- **`SemanticNode` and `Semantics` stay on their thread** (zgx): neither is `Send` or `Sync` now, as nodes share their rarer fields. To hand accessibility information to another thread, copy out what it needs (the role, the label's `String`, the numbers) and send that.
- **`text::rich_decorations` and `text::rich_decorations_in` take the fonts** (zgx-gpu, and zgx-text, where they are now): a last argument, `fonts: &mut FontSystem`, the one the text was shaped in: `rich_decorations(&buffer, &rich, &mut fonts)`. zgx-gpu's other text paths are where they were.
- **`SpanIcon` has `bg`, `radius` and `pad`** (zgx): a literal adds `bg: None, radius: 0., pad: 0.` for an icon with no well, as before.
- **`CanvasText` has a `width`** (zgx): a Rust canvas making its text with a struct literal adds `width: f32::INFINITY` for a line as before and `known_size: KnownSize::NONE` (`width` is a bare `f32`, so a line of text is no bigger than before).
- **`ClipboardContent` has `types`, and copies hand it whole** (zgx): a literal adds `types: Vec::new()`. It is what `View::on_copy` and `View::on_selection_copy` return. `Ui::copy_from` and `Ui::copy_selection_from` call their `write` with the `ClipboardContent` rather than `(text, html)`: `|content| content.write(platform)` puts it on a `Platform`'s clipboard as the window's copy does.
- **Enums with new variants** (zgx, zgx-desktop): a `match` naming every variant handles them, or adds a `_` arm: `InputEvent::PointerMotion` and `PointerLockChange` (a locked pointer's motion, and the lock given or let go), `NativeContent::Glass` (the system's glass, from `ui.glass`) and `GlassMaterial::Liquid`.
- **Canvas enums with new variants** (zgx): `canvas::Draw` has `Image` and `Shadow`, and `canvas::Brush` has `Radial` and `Conic`: a renderer matching every command or brush handles them (or rasterizes the canvas, as zgx-gpu does).
- **Structs with `Default` gained fields** (zgx, zgx-gpu, zgx-desktop, zgx-script): a literal of one ends with `..Default::default()`: `WindowOptions` (`renderer`, `traffic_lights`), `GpuStats` (`backdrop_copies`, `regions`), `LayerShader` (`bleed`, `reach`), `TerminalState` (`search`), `trace::Frame` (`sites`, `spare`) and zgx-script's `markdown::Options` (`html_marks`, `autolinks`).
- **`StandardItem` has a `ToggleFullScreen` variant** (zgx-desktop): a `match` naming every `StandardItem` no longer compiles until it handles `ToggleFullScreen` (or adds a `_` arm).
- **`theme::Field` and `DebugCacheStats` are `#[non_exhaustive]`** (zgx, zgx-gpu): a token can come to set another property, and the stats to count another cache, without breaking an app. A `match` on a `Field` needs a `_` arm and a pattern naming the stats' fields ends with `..`; only zgx builds a `DebugCacheStats`. New since 0.1.10: `Field`'s `GapX`, `GapY`, `MinWidth`, `MaxWidth`, `MinHeight`, `MaxHeight` and `OutlineInnerColor`, and the stats' `images_softer`, `layers_coarser`, `shader_pipelines` and `surface_collections`. Naming and reading them is as it was.
- **`WindowCapabilities` is `#[non_exhaustive]`** (zgx-desktop): apps read it and only zgx builds one, so it can gain fields without breaking an app. Code that built one with a struct literal starts from `let mut can = WindowCapabilities::default();` and assigns its fields (a literal with `..Default::default()` isn't allowed outside zgx-desktop either), and a pattern naming its fields ends with `..`.
- **`TestApp` is made by its functions, and an inspect `Server` isn't unwind safe** (zgx-script): `TestApp` has private fields, so a literal of it no longer compiles (`TestApp::inline` and its other constructors are as they were). `inspect::Server` is no longer `UnwindSafe` or `RefUnwindSafe`: wrap what captures one in `AssertUnwindSafe` to pass it to `catch_unwind`.
- **An unstyled `switch` lays out as what it shows, in its place** (zgx): its view's percentages, `grow` and alignment reach the region's parent, as `.contents()` does and as Luau's `ui.switch` always has, so `h_full` inside it resolves against the parent instead of an auto-height box (a virtual list there built no rows at all). This changes a default: what such a `switch` shows now has its `grow` and percentages reach the parent. Any style on the `switch` (`.w_full()`, `.h(100.)`) keeps a box of its own, as `.not_contents()` does with none.

#### Luau: what a script may notice

- **`ui.router` keeps the last 100 places behind, so back stops after 100 steps** (zgx-script): `ui.router { history = n }` sets how many, and `math.huge` keeps every one as before. Each place is a few small tables made amid what that move made and dropped, so kept without end they held a page of the Luau heap each: in Zeron, 1,200 chat switches left 0.3 MB of them holding about 7.6 MB of pages, and the app's VM at rest went from 29 MB to 18–19 MB with the limit.
- **A plugin draws 3D only when granted `"scene3d"`** (zgx-script, `scene3d`): `ui.scene3d` and `ui.model` in a plugin are errors unless its host grants it `Capability::Scene3d` (`"scene3d"` in its `allow` in zgx.json). Before, any plugin of an app with the `scene3d` feature could draw on the GPU. To keep a plugin's scenes, add the grant.
- **Bounds and visibility observers deliver once per frame, before paint, as the web's observers do** (zgx, zgx-script): `NodeHandle::observe_bounds`, `observe_visibility`, `observe_visibility_within` and Luau's `on_visible` are worked out at the frame's preparation, not again before each input event dispatched between frames. A handler in the same frame (a key's, a wheel's) sees the previous frame's observed values; several scrolls between two frames are heard once, from where the last left the node. Direct reads stay fresh: `NodeHandle::window_bounds`, an element's `x`, `y`, `width` and `height`, and hit-testing read the latest layout and scroll, as `getBoundingClientRect` does. Sizes (`observe_size`, `on_resize`) are unchanged. In a scroll of a transcript with 232 observed rows, a wheel's dispatch went from 0.7–1.3 ms to 0.13 ms.
- **`truncate` forces one line only when `lines` isn't set** (zgx-script): `lines = 2, truncate = true` is now two lines ending in "…", as Rust's `.line_clamp(2).text_overflow(Ellipsis)` is; it was one line. `truncate` alone is still one line, and a wrapper passing both should check the `lines` it sends. A `lines` inherited from a parent counts as set.
- **Cancelling a promise or task drops the host work it waits on.** `p:cancel()`, or cancelling a task awaiting it, now drops a file dialog's request or a `ui.sleep` where it waits and stops waiting on a file read (its thread finishes on its own), as it already stopped a fetch: Linux's portal closes its picker; a macOS sheet or Windows dialog stays up until answered, its answer unheard. Before, the script went on waiting for the host and only dropped the answer. A host action still runs to the end.
- **A rich-text run that only acts is a button** (zgx): a span with `on_click` and no `href` (a citation badge) is exposed as a button, not a link, and Space presses it as Enter does, as a browser's button; a run with an `href` stays a link, which Space doesn't follow. A press on a button run no longer starts a text selection drag.
- **A reload that fails undoes what its scripts wrote** (zgx-script): a script that set `ui.state`, `ui.persist` or `ui.shared` values as it loaded, then failed, left those writes in place, and the keys it made, though the interface it built never showed. Now each value is set back to what it held, the keys it made go, and the overrides plugins gave stay as they were: a failed reload leaves the app as it was. A host's first load, with nothing before it to go back to, records nothing and is as before.
- **Canvas text is drawn in the drawing's order and cut by its clips** (zgx): a shape drawn after a text covers it, where text used to sit over every shape whatever the order (so where two cards overlapped, the lower one's words showed through the upper), and `c:clip` cuts text drawn while it is open, to the clip's box (a rounded rectangle's or a circle's corners too), where text used to ignore clips. A canvas whose later shapes keep clear of its text, or whose text comes last (a chart's labels, a terminal's rows), draws and costs as before: the text goes under a later shape only where that shape's bounds meet a text's, which starts another layer, up to 8. `Canvas::layers`, `Canvas::layer`, `Canvas::text_clips` and `Canvas::text_after` say how.
- **`size` sizes a box, as the reference has always said** (zgx-script): on `ui.div`, `ui.column`, `ui.row`, `ui.grid`, `ui.overlay`, `ui.scroll` and `ui.presence`, `size = 50` or `size = { 120, 60 }` is `w` and `h` at once. It sized nothing on any element: the name is also `ui.split`'s state, and a constructor's fields are read before style properties. A box given a `size` it ignored until now takes that size. Elsewhere it still does nothing, and `zgx lint` says so (`size-inert`), pointing a text to `text_size`.
- **A `ui.modal` with a state `open` runs its `on_close`** (zgx, zgx-script): `on_close` was only heard when `open` was a function, so a modal given a state and an `on_close` (the usual shape) never ran it on Escape or a press on the backdrop. It now runs as the person closes it, after the state is written, and not when the app sets the state itself. `View::on_dismiss` is the Rust side.
- **Nothing takes the pointer inside `pointer_events = "none"` but what has handlers of its own** (zgx, zgx-script, zgx-desktop): a `ui.show`, `ui.switch` or `ui.presence` holding such a layer took every press over the layer's box, and in a script any node with an `id`, a class, a ref, `on_mount`, `on_resize` or `on_visible` took the pointer as if it had handlers. A contents node has no box to hit now, and an element listens only once a draggable asks it to. The cursor now comes from what a press would reach, past what the pointer passes through, unless that says a cursor of its own (`InputDispatcher::cursor_at`). `NodeHandle::on_event` listens from after mount.
- **A node scrolled into view keeps its scroll view's `scroll_padding` clear** (zgx, zgx-script): `el:scroll_into_view` (and `NodeHandle::scroll_into_view`) shows a node within each scroll view's `scroll_padding`, as the web's `scroll-padding` keeps it and as focus already did, and `nearest` leaves alone a box spanning the view, as the web's does. Focus, find and `scroll_into_view` now show a node by one rule.
- **Items added above a keyed list's view no longer push its rows down** (zgx, zgx-script): a `ui.list` with `key` and `measured` (or `heights`) keeps the rows in view where they are as items are inserted or removed above them, as a browser's scroll anchoring does, where before it kept its offset and the content moved under the reader. Resting at the top it doesn't anchor, so a feed's newest items show. Only a change of count looks for the row; a list nothing is added to does no more work. To check in an existing app: a script that relied on the offset staying as rows went in above (to show them, or moving it by their height itself) now sets `offset`, or calls `scroll_to_row`, after the change.
- **Lists build rows earlier, and not those folded to nothing** (both under Performance): the rows about to show are built ahead, so a row function runs before its row is in view; with `heights`, a row under a hundredth of a point isn't built until it opens (its row function and effects don't run) and a row of 0 takes no room, where it was a point tall.
- **A modal's `on_close` and its state**: a script that gave a modal a state and also watched that state for closing hears both now as the person closes it (only the state changed before); let one do the work.
- **Layout fixes that can move things** (each under Fixed): a column shrinks a box no further than its content needs at the box's own width; `w = "fit-content"` on a box whose text wraps takes its room; a box its column shrinks follows its content when laid out alone.

### Changed: what to check in an existing app

- A selection that goes into part of a text area's atom (a chip) takes the whole atom, on the desktop and the DOM. A drag into a chip, a double click on one of its words and Shift+click all select it whole, so cut and delete take it whole as Backspace does. A caret alone still goes to the chip's nearer edge. This departs on purpose from the browser's `contenteditable=false` default, as chat composers' mentions do.
- **`View::atoms_when` is given the text area's model** (zgx): its closure takes `&mut TextEditor` (its text, its atoms, a new atom's id) where it took `&EditorHandle`, which only a native window has; the DOM target keeps a text area's text in a model of its own, so one API serves both. No release had the old signature (it came with the unreleased atoms): a closure reading `handle.editor.borrow()` reads `editor` itself.
- **A traced binding says where in zgx it was made** (zgx): `zgx inspect frames` names a binding `@832 binding compose.rs:6296`, the file and line in zgx that made it (a prop or feature: a style, a show, a model), beside the node's id and, with the layout inspector on, the script line that made it.
- **A box's fill reaches its outer edge under its border, as CSS's does** (zgx-gpu): the border is painted over the fill (`background-clip: border-box`), so a translucent border shows the fill under it, not what lies under the box. A faint edge on a rounded box had shown its own shadow or the backdrop through it, which read as a dark seam along a dark popover's corners. Opaque borders draw as before. A box with a border width but a fully transparent border colour now fills out to its outer edge, 1 point further than before, as in a browser.
- **A box laid out between device pixels paints its edges on them** (zgx), as browsers snap layout boxes: a dialog centred at a quarter point draws its edges and a 1 pt border sharp on whole device pixels at 1x and 2x, where they blurred across two or three rows. Each edge rounds on its own, so boxes sharing an edge still share it, and a box paints exactly the device pixels whose centres lie in its layout box, so a press on any of them hits it. Text keeps its own snapping; what a translation moves between pixels (an animation in flight) and what a transform, a zoom or a tilt holds keep their places between pixels. Only with a pixel scale set (`Scene::set_pixel_scale`, as a window sets it).
- **`zgx build` keeps licence files and lists what it left out** (zgx-script): a bundle of bytecode holds the kinds of file a host reads, and dropped the rest without a word, the licences of the icons and fonts it shipped among them. `LICENSE*`, `NOTICE*` and `COPYING*` are kept now, and the app's other files not in the bundle are listed as it is written.
- **Buttons stay out of a text selection**: inside a selectable region, a button's label (and what it holds) is left out of a selection across it, and out of select-all and copy, as a browser leaves it. A button that should be selected says `selectable(true)` itself; its presses act as before.
- **A contained panic in a host's Rust callback names it and where it was registered** (zgx-script, zgx-desktop): a panic in an action, a function, an async action or the fetcher a script called ends its report with `(in a host action registered at src/main.rs:120)`, the line that handed it to `ScriptHost`, beside where it panicked (which may be deep in a library). It costs a callback nothing as it returns.
- **macOS draws text as heavy as macOS does** (zgx-gpu, zgx-text, zgx-swash, zgx-cosmic-text): glyph outlines are thickened as CoreGraphics' font smoothing thickens macOS's own text, by the text's colour: dilated by `(0.0058 + 0.01 × level) × size` pixels (at most 0.3), the level the colour's linear-light luminance in quarters, values read from CoreGraphics. All text is heavier, light text most (off where the user turned smoothing off). Swash rasterizes it (zgx's swash fork, `Render::dilate_box`), so it costs what swash does; Core Text draws only the faces swash can't read. At 13 pt on Retina and from 32 px a glyph's ink is within 0.1% of Core Text's; swash's unhinted outlines drew text 13–26% lighter, thinner than every other app beside it. Colour glyphs (emoji) stay swash's; Linux and Windows are unchanged. `text::set_font_smoothing` lets an app follow the system's setting (the default) or draw text lighter, for a setting of its own: every window draws its text again.
- **Direct3D 11 leaves out what it can't draw yet where that can't mislead** (zgx-gpu, the experimental `d3d11` feature under `ZGX_D3D11=1`): drop shadows are left out, images a GPU view draws are left empty, and a backdrop blur or a layer shader reading the backdrop (glass) draws its box opaque in its own colour, as Reduce transparency draws glass; each kind is named once on stderr. A frame holding one of these failed whole before. Content blur, masks, blend modes and image effects still fail the frame, naming what's missing.
- **Kept pictures' decoded copies are bounded while a window scrolls** (zgx, zgx-desktop): past sixteen of the window's frames (64 to 192 MiB), those used least recently go as a decode lands. A long scroll through thousands of photos held every copy until the window rested (844 MB after 90 s on an iPhone simulator; 190 MB now), while a scroll through a few hundred decodes no more than before. Nothing runs as an image drops or a picture draws.
- **The first present's line says what drew it** (zgx-desktop): with `ZGX_SHADER_LOG=1`, `zgx: first frame presented` goes on `, drawn by the raster` or `, drawn by the GPU on <adapter> (<type>, <backend>)` (and `, from the raster` where the GPU took over from it), so a probe's evidence names the path a failed run took. Nothing is asked of the renderer without the variable.
- **A text styled as it mounts costs less** (zgx): giving a text never laid out its own size and colour no longer works out damage and walks its ancestors, which its first layout already does.
- **An inset shadow starts at the padding edge, inside the border** (zgx-gpu): as CSS draws `box-shadow: inset`, so a 0.5 to 1 point ring under a 1 point border no longer vanishes beneath it and design-system elevation tokens port as they are. A box with no border draws as before. Apps that widened an inset ring to show it past a border (a 1.5 ring for a 1 point border) should return to the CSS value.
- **A `layout` box no longer eases a size change under a hundredth of a point** (zgx-script): it takes the new size at once. Text measured again or a shrinking parent's share moved it by thousandths of a point, and easing that laid the box out again each frame for nothing.
- **Mounting a text with a size or colour of its own makes no typography signal** (zgx): a text holds no views, so nothing could read one.
- **Keyboard focus on Linux is drawn as GNOME's** (zgx): as libadwaita's focus ring, an outline 2 wide inset over the control's edge in the accent at half, its corners the control's, bordered control or not. macOS and Windows are unchanged.
- **Menus and popovers cast GNOME's shadow on Linux** (zgx): as libadwaita draws a popover, a faint outline (black at 5%) and two soft layers (1 down at 9%, 2 down at 5%), drawn outside the panel in a popup's surface. macOS and Windows are unchanged.
- **Menus and popovers take GNOME's shapes on Linux** (zgx): as libadwaita draws its popover menus, a menu's panel is rounded 15 with its items 6 in, each 32 tall, padded 12 across and rounded 9, its highlight the text at 10%, a shortcut 12 from its label; a popover is rounded 15 and padded 8; a chosen item no longer flashes before its menu closes. Colours otherwise stay the theme's. macOS and Windows are unchanged.
- **Scrollbars on Linux behave as GNOME's** (zgx): an overlay scrollbar is an indicator 3 wide that widens to 8 as soon as the pointer is on it (over 200 ms), fully round, at least 40 long, 4 from the edge, its slider the text at 20% (40% under the pointer) over a trough at 10% while widened; it fades out over a second, 2 s after scrolling stops. Where scrollbars always show, 8 wide with no trough. Each wait is a single wake. macOS and Windows are unchanged.
- **Tooltips look and wait as GNOME's on Linux** (zgx): as libadwaita draws them, dark and translucent in either scheme with white text and a faint white border, padded 10 by 6, rounded 9; shown after 500 ms, and within 500 ms of another closing after 60 ms, as GTK times them. macOS and Windows are unchanged.
- **A measured list's scroll step is about 2% cheaper** (zgx): each pass tracked every mounted row's index, which only that pass writes.
- **Over a list, a mouse wheel's line is a row** (zgx, zgx-desktop on macOS): a notch scrolls a list its lines times its row height (a measured list's estimate), as a table's rows set an `NSTableView`'s line, where it scrolled 10 points a line as any view. `Ui::wheel_line_at` says what a line scrolls under a point.
- **Text below 2x draws on whole pixels, with firmer edges** (zgx-gpu): on a screen at 100% to 175% (most Windows and Linux monitors), each glyph now goes on the nearest whole device pixel instead of a quarter-pixel offset, and its mask's edges are firmed by how light the text is, as DirectWrite's enhanced contrast does (near-black text on white keeps its weight). A vertical stem a quarter pixel off spread over two columns at part strength, which read as blur next to the system's own text. Layout, line widths and wrapping are unchanged; a glyph moves by at most half a pixel. At 2x and over, text draws as before.
- **A mouse wheel's notch moves 30 points at once on macOS, as in AppKit's views** (zgx-desktop): each notch moves its whole step on the next refresh, notches within one refresh together, at an `NSScrollView`'s line of 10 points (3 lines, 30 points, a notch at the system's default). It moved a browser's 120 points, eased in over about 150 ms. Windows and Linux are unchanged.
- **`zgx inspect memory` accounts for the whole heap** (zgx, zgx-gpu, zgx-desktop):
  - `text shaping buffers and caches`: what cosmic-text keeps between runs (harfrust's buffer and shape plans, the spans, words and lines it reuses, its font searches' caches).
  - `heap mimalloc resident` and `resident, not in use` (macOS): what footprint charges for mimalloc's pages, and of that what holds nothing live (pages a few blocks keep, size classes' rounding); elsewhere the line says it's unavailable.
  - `devtools effect names` counts the names frames' runs still hold after their effects are gone, and `frame log` each frame's costliest measures; inspected, a chat window's names read 0.6 MB where they said 0.0.
  Each is worked out when a report asks, nothing counted as frames run.
- **A trackpad scroll coasts as an `NSScrollView` does** (zgx-desktop, macOS): after the fingers lift, a scroll goes on from their speed, measured as AppKit measures it, on AppKit's own curve: a quartic ease-out that ends in a finite time, moving in whole points, each refresh showing where it will be when that refresh reaches the screen. The system's momentum events go unused, as AppKit's scroll views leave them. Under the same posted flicks, the speed zgx lifts at equals what AppKit's measure gives over the same events' timestamps, in 9 of 9; the coast follows from it as AppKit's does.
- **On a machine whose only GPU is a software one, windows now draw on the software raster** (zgx-desktop, zgx). `ZGX_RENDERER=auto` is now the default: an opaque 1x window with no hardware adapter (no render node on Linux, only WARP on Windows) draws on zgx's raster rather than through lavapipe, llvmpipe or WARP. That takes a third of the memory (an idle app: 42 MB where lavapipe held 140) and a fraction of the CPU (0.2–0.5 ms a frame where they took 1.3–9.6). A raster window that comes to show a GPU view or a layer shader moves to the GPU before its next frame, and then matches a GPU window pixel for pixel. Its menus and tooltips stay inside the window, flipped or shifted to fit. **`ZGX_RENDERER=gpu` restores the previous behaviour**, the GPU renderer everywhere. Machines with a hardware GPU are unaffected. New: `zgx::gpu_view::any_mounted`, `Scene::has_layer_shaders`.
- **A stretch let go while pushing goes on a moment first** (zgx): letting go of a stretched scroll view starts its spring back at the fingers' speed, as an `NSScrollView` does, measured: pushed at 1500 points a second it goes about 2 points further over 15 ms before coming back. Fingers rested 200 ms first let go from rest. A momentum's bounce ends after its furthest point, as AppKit's does, not after a fixed 24 ms.
- **A script's errors say where** (zgx-script, zgx-desktop): an error names the script line it came from, or the function that failed and what zgx called it for, the same in the terminal, the window's banner and `zgx inspect`.
  - A function returning what zgx can't take leads with its definition: `app.luau:40: the row function of ui.each returned nothing; return a ui node`. This covers each and list rows, lazy `ui.show`/`ui.switch` branches, components and presence content.
  - An error inside a function zgx called names its role first: `in the key function of ui.each (defined at app.luau:7):`, above the script's own line and traceback. This covers keys, handlers, effects, `ui.computed`, computed props and callbacks.
  - A refused argument to a `ui` function names the script line that called it: `runtime error: app.luau:3: …`.
  - A contribution's or plugin page's error, found as the interface builds, names the line that made it.
  - A desktop app's contained panic leads its banner with where it panicked.
- **Select All with no region pressed in**: with no selectable region pressed in yet and no field focused, ⌘A (Ctrl+A) selects the region under the pointer's last place, else the window's only region, as a page's select-all does. With several regions and none under the pointer, it does nothing, as before.
- **A native module's write to a shared state whose host is gone fails** (zgx-module, zgx-script): `Shared::set` and `Shared::remove` return `Result<bool, Failed>`, and they, `append`, `upsert` and `Host::image` fail with "its host is gone" instead of reporting success, so a module can tell that what it wrote went nowhere. In the interface, `shared_set` and `shared_remove` return `HOST_GONE` (not a count) then, and the writes that return `DONE` or `FAILED` fail saying so. Native modules are unstable before 1.0.
- **A hand's scroll lags less in its slowest frames** (zgx-desktop): a trackpad stream stands up to 40 ms behind only while its events come steadily, as a slow device's do; a hand's wandering events stand at most 20 ms behind. On real recordings the slowest 5% of frames' lag drops (one: 32 to 28 ms, another 51 to 35), the median unchanged and a steady 30-a-second stream still even.
- **An emoji asked for with VS16 takes two cells in the engine-drawn terminal** (zgx-terminal; `zgx-alacritty-terminal` 0.26.2): ❤️, ✔️ or ⚠️ (a character that is text by default, then U+FE0F) took one cell and now takes two, as Ghostty, kitty and iTerm2 show it and as programs measuring with Unicode's widths count it (Ink's string-width, ratatui), so coding agents' lines stay in step. A shell's line editor counting with `wcwidth` sees one column, as in those terminals: an edit across such an emoji puts its cursor a cell off.
- **Text that ends in an ellipsis shrinks past its longest word** (zgx): a text or paragraph whose overflow is an ellipsis (`truncate()`, `text_overflow(Ellipsis)`, `truncate = true` in Luau) has an automatic minimum width of 0 as a flex item or grid item, as one that clips does, so a truncated label in a squeezed row or a `1fr` track gives way and shows its ellipsis instead of pushing its siblings. CSS's `text-overflow: ellipsis` needs `overflow: hidden`, which does the same. It is the text itself: a box around a truncated label still needs the label's width (give the box `min_w(0.)`), and text inheriting `truncate` from a box shrinks as its own would. Set `min_w` on a label that should keep a width.
- **A list's, a scroll view's and a text input's viewport lays out within it** (zgx): each is absolute in its box now, and a width change lays that box out alone, so panes of them cost one pass, not one more for each. Nothing here changed size in the layouts compared with the last release, at any window size, but a list or scroll view with no size of its own gets its size from nothing it holds: give it a size (`h`, `h_full`, `grow`), which is what it needed before. A text input or area a percent of a parent its content sizes stays in its flow, and costs what it did.
- **Menus, popovers and tooltips take Windows 11's shapes on Windows** (zgx): as WinUI 3 draws its flyouts, a menu's panel is rounded 8 with 2 above and below its items, each item's highlight inset 4 by 2 and rounded 4, items padded 11 by 4; a popover is rounded 8; a tooltip is padded 9 across, 6 above and 8 below, in 12-point text. Colours stay the theme's. They are zgx's constants for Windows, not theme tokens: a view's own styles restyle them. macOS and Linux are unchanged.
- **Keyboard focus on Windows is drawn as WinUI's focus visual** (zgx): a ring 2 wide, 1 outside the control, in the text's colour, over a contrasting ring 1 wide in the gap (the window's background), its corners the control's grown by 3, bordered control or not; it takes no room. macOS and Linux keep the accent ring inside the control. An outline can have such a second ring anywhere: `Styled::outline_inner`, `Scene::set_outline_inner`.
- **A chosen menu item no longer flashes on Windows** (zgx): where menus are windows of their own, a chosen item flashed off and on (100 ms) before its menu closed and its action ran, as AppKit's menus do; on Windows the menu now closes and the action runs at once, as WinUI's. macOS and Linux keep the flash.
- **Plain glass blurs what is behind the window on Windows** (zgx-desktop): `Glass::plain` was Mica, which tints with the wallpaper and blurs nothing, so a window over other windows looked opaque. It is now the acrylic blur of what is behind the window, untinted, as gpui's `WindowBackgroundAppearance::Blurred` is, with no system backdrop and no frame extended under the client (DWM drew the frame over the blur). Windows 10 1809 and later. With Transparency effects off it is the material's backdrop, which goes solid. Glass of every other material keeps its Acrylic, Mica or Mica Alt, so an app that wants Mica keeps it with the same material without `.plain()`.
- **A translucent popup on Windows 11 blurs what is behind it** (zgx-desktop, zgx-gpu): menus, popovers and tooltips get the acrylic accent plain glass already has, and DWM rounds and shadows their window. `WindowCapabilities::popup_blur_behind` says whether popups blur, so an app can draw them solid where they can't.
- **Scrollbars on Windows behave as WinUI 3's** (zgx): an overlay scrollbar is a thin indicator that widens to 6 in a 12 track 0.4 s after the pointer arrives and narrows 0.5 s after it leaves, each over 167 ms on WinUI's curve, rounded 3, 1 from the edge, its thumb at least 30 long and one colour under the pointer (the text at half, over the surface's colour as it widens); it goes 2 s after scrolling stops, fading over 83 ms. Where scrollbars always show, it is wide. Each wait is a single wake: a window at rest schedules nothing. macOS and Linux keep zgx's scrollbar.
- **Tooltips on Windows wait as WinUI's do** (zgx, zgx-desktop): twice the system's mouse hover time before showing (800 ms by default) and the hover time within 200 ms of another opening (WinUI's one and a half times truncates to one), read from `SPI_GETMOUSEHOVERTIME` with the system's other settings. macOS and Linux keep 300 ms and an immediate reshow within a second. The timing is `zgx::compose::TooltipTiming` (`set_tooltip_timing`); a tooltip's own `delay` still waits its own.
- **Text shaping and measuring build without the renderer** (zgx-text): the fonts, shaped text, prepared lines and `TextServices` zgx-gpu drew text from are a crate of their own, `zgx-text`, with no wgpu or winit. A host that lays text out but never draws (a headless service measuring as a window does) links it alone. zgx-gpu re-exports all of it (`zgx_gpu::text`, `zgx_gpu::prepared`, `zgx_gpu::TextServices`, `zgx_gpu::measure_text`), so nothing that uses zgx-gpu changes.

### Added

- **A tree row's depth reaches assistive technology, and follows it** (zgx, zgx-script, zgx-dom): a row of a virtualised tree tells screen readers its depth (`aria-level`), and a row moved into another branch is read at its new depth without being rebuilt, on the desktop and in the browser.
- **Animated WebP plays** (zgx-gpu): an animated WebP decodes to its composited frames and delays and plays as a GIF does (it loops for ever: the container's loop count isn't read), in place of its first frame. A still WebP is kept as drawn and shrunk as before.
- **An app's own items lead the application menu** (zgx-desktop, macOS): a menu named `APPLICATION_MENU` puts its items first in the application menu, above Services, Hide and Quit, as AppKit apps put About, Check for Updates and Settings there. Elsewhere it is a menu like any other.
- **An app's own Window and Help menus are AppKit's, and full screen is a standard item** (zgx-desktop, macOS): a menu titled "Window" keeps Minimize and Zoom above the app's items and Bring All to Front below them, as AppKit apps' do (they were left out as soon as an app had a Window menu of its own), except one the app put there itself; a menu titled "Help" becomes AppKit's help menu, which holds its menu search. `StandardItem::ToggleFullScreen` is AppKit's Enter Full Screen item (⌃⌘F, titled Exit Full Screen while the window is); elsewhere it toggles the focused window's full screen.
- **`aria_modal = true` marks a sheet or dialog an app builds itself as modal** (`View::accessible_modal` in Rust): while it shows, assistive technology reads it alone, not what lies behind it, as it does `ui.modal` (ARIA's `aria-modal`).
- **A textarea's atom can show as a picture (an equation)** (zgx, zgx-script): `atom_style.svg = { src | code, width, height, descent }` in EM (and an atom's own `style.svg`) shows its text as one box of that size with the picture in it; one caret unit, its line as tall as it needs, its text kept for copy, search and assistive technology. (Luau internal; `AtomStyle::svg`, hidden, in Rust).
- **A text area's chips look the same in the browser** (zgx-dom, zgx-script types): an atom's `padding` and `radius` (#2424) set the chip's CSS padding and corners there too, and its `icon` shows before its label (a tinted one, or one in the text's colour, as a mask over that colour; its `bg`, `radius` and `pad` well around it). The icon holds no text, so selection and editing count the label alone. `AtomLook`'s types now have `padding`, `radius` and the icon's well.
- **A textarea's atoms can show as mentions** (zgx, zgx-script): `atom_style` (and an atom's own `style`) take a text colour, a weight, an underline in its own colour and offset at the font's thickness, and a leading icon in room before the atom's first word, which wraps with it (Luau internal, subject to change; `View::atom_style`, `EditorHandle::set_atom_style`, `Atom::style`, hidden, in Rust). A textarea without styled atoms draws as it did.
- **A textarea's atoms answer the pointer** (zgx, zgx-script): `on_atom_press(atom, at)` when one is pressed in place (true keeps the caret out of the chip) and `on_atom_hover(atom?, box?)` with the chip under the pointer and its box on that line, again when a scroll or a new layout moves it (Luau props internal, subject to change; `View::on_atom_press`, `View::on_atom_hover` in Rust). A textarea without them is unchanged.
- **A text area's atoms go through the clipboard** (zgx, zgx-script, zgx-dom): a copy or cut of text with chips puts them beside the text as `application/x-zgx-atoms`, and a paste into a text area that takes atoms (a textarea with `atoms`) makes them again, with their `id`, `data` and `style`, in one undo step. Within the app they come back exactly as copied; from another window, app or page a textarea reads their names, data and looks back, giving one a new `id` where the target already has its name. A plain-text target, or another app, gets the text alone.
- **A text area's atom is read as one unit, by its name where it has one** (zgx, zgx-desktop, zgx-dom, zgx-script): to a screen reader each chip is one character of its field's text (AccessKit) or one named image in the browser's (`role="img"`, `aria-label`), so it is stepped over and read whole, not as "at, f, i, l, e…". An atom's new `name` (in `atoms` items and `ui.editor.insert_atom`'s options) is what is read for it instead of its text: a mention's full name. It is kept through undo and the clipboard.
- **Sound in Luau scripts** (zgx-script): the `audio` global loads sounds off the main thread (`audio.sound`), streams music (`audio.music`, `audio-streams`), plays voices on an exact frame (`at = audio.now() + 0.1`) and drives buses. `sound = click` on a button, checkbox or any element with `on_click` plays on the `ui` bus as it's activated, by pointer, keyboard or assistive technology; `click:with { gain, pitch }` sets its level and pitch, and `audio.choose(function)` picks the sound as the control is activated. A gain, pan or pitch can follow a state, a function or a `ui.tween`, with no script per frame. `voice.playing` turns false when the voice ends. A reload stops the old script's voices and streams. Effects on buses (`audio-effects`: `bus:add_effect(audio.low_pass { … })`, `reverb`, `delay`, `compressor`, `bus:duck`). Ogg Vorbis, MP3 and Opus come with `audio-vorbis`, `audio-mp3` and `audio-opus`. There are five lint rules (`audio-load-in-builder`, `audio-play-timer`, `audio-sound-too-long`, `audio-param-in-loop`, `audio-effect-in-builder`). The host gives scripts its engine (`ScriptHost::set_audio`). All of this is off by default: a build without these features is unchanged, its definitions and lint rules included. See docs/reference/audio.md.
- **Plugins play sound when granted `"audio"`** (zgx-script, `audio`): a plugin's `audio` plays into a bus of its own (`plugin:<name>`, under `ui`), its controls' `sound =` too, with at most 8 voices and 4 effects. Its sounds come from its own directory and count against its memory. It reaches no other bus, the master or the device.
- **An app can ask whether the system blurs behind its glass, and whether its window is maximized** (zgx-desktop, zgx-script): `WindowCapabilities::blur_behind` is false on Linux where the compositor has no blur protocol (GNOME, sway, Weston, and X11, where zgx asks for none), where glass shows what is behind it sharp, so an app may draw solid; `WindowContext::maximized`, and in scripts `ui.window.maximized` (`ScriptHost::window_maximized`), follow the system's word on the window.
- **`zgx build --ui source` writes a bundle whose scripts stay source** (zgx-script, zgx-run): for an app whose people lay their own files over its interface, or edit it where it is installed, which a bundle of checked bytecode can't be. Every file of its folder ships (one that looks like a secret fails the build), and `ui/` is run as any folder is; its native modules are built, cached and checked as in any bundle. Bytecode stays the default, its format as it was. `Opened::files` is an `Option` now, `None` for source.
- **A host can refuse a bundle's bytecode** (zgx-script, hidden for now): with `ScriptHost::code(Code::Source)`, a bundle's scripts load as source text only, as files from the disk always do, and one in bytecode form fails to load. Luau checks no bytecode it loads, so only bytecode the app's own build compiled (`Code::VerifiedBytecode`, what `bundled` keeps) should run: scripts from anywhere else, an update downloaded, are compiled where they run.
- **A canvas draws pictures** (zgx-script, zgx, zgx-gpu): `c:image(src, x, y, w, h, { fit, radius, opacity })` draws a file, a URL, a `host:` image or a `Bitmap` among the canvas's shapes, in the order drawn, fitted to its box as `ui.image` fits one (`"cover"` crops it), cut to the box's rounded corners and the canvas's clip. A remote or `host:` picture not there yet draws nothing until it comes, and the drawing follows it. In Rust, `Canvas::image` and `Draw::Image`. A canvas with a picture rasterizes, as one with a gradient does.
- **A canvas draws radial and conic gradients** (zgx, zgx-gpu, zgx-script): `ui.radial(cx, cy, r, colors, { ry, transform })` runs out from a centre to a circle or ellipse, the last colour past it; `ui.conic(cx, cy, colors, { angle, transform })` turns clockwise round a centre from `angle` degrees off +x. `transform` (`{ a, b, c, d, e, f }`) maps either as a drawing's own transform does, so a rotated or mirrored shape's gradient turns with it. Both go wherever `ui.linear` does, a box's `bg_fill` too. In Rust, `Brush::radial` and `Brush::conic` place the unit circle by an `Affine`.
- **A canvas draws shadows** (zgx-script, zgx, zgx-gpu): `c:shadow(path, { x, y, blur, spread, color })` draws the shadow a path casts, written as a `shadow` property is (CSS's blur), under what is drawn after it: a card's drop shadow is `c:shadow(card, …)` then `c:fill(card, …)`. Any path casts one, blurred as a text shadow is and cut by the canvas's clip; `spread` grows it, or shrinks it below 0. In Rust, `Canvas::shadow` and `Draw::Shadow`. A canvas with a shadow rasterizes, as one with a gradient does.
- **Canvas text takes line height, letter spacing and a wrap width** (zgx-script, zgx): `c:text(text, x, y, { line_height, letter_spacing, width })` spaces lines and characters in points, as a text node does, and with `width` wraps to lines that wide in a box that wide, which `align` places on `x` and aligns its lines in. `c:measure_text` takes the same options, so a paragraph's height is `select(2, c:measure_text(body, { width = 240 }))`. In Rust, `CanvasText::width` and `canvas::measure_text_in`.
- **Canvas text turns with a transform** (zgx, zgx-script): `c:text(text, x, y, { transform = { a, b, c, d, e, f } })` maps the placed text by that matrix (SVG's `matrix` order), so a label in a rotated, mirrored or scaled frame turns with the frame's shapes instead of staying upright. In Rust, `Canvas::text_transformed` adds one and `Canvas::text_transform` says what a text is mapped by; its node takes the transform's scale, skew and turn about its top left.
- **A theme can set the colours of Windows' caption buttons apart from the rest of the app** (zgx, zgx-desktop): `Titlebar::Inset`'s buttons read `caption.text`, `caption.text.hover`, `caption.text.inactive`, `caption.hover` and `caption.pressed`, and Close reads `caption.close`, `caption.close.pressed`, `caption.close.text` and `caption.close.text.pressed`. Unset, each is what the buttons showed before: `text.secondary`, `text.primary` and `text.tertiary` over `hover` and `pressed`, and WinUI's red under a white glyph. An app that draws its title bar glyphs in full text colour, with no dimming in an inactive window, sets them in its theme. In a contrast theme the system's colours stand in for all but Close's.
- **A copy can carry types of the app's own, and a web page's paste reads them** (zgx-script, zgx-desktop, zgx-dom): a selectable region's `on_copy` may return `types = { [mime] = text }` beside `text` and `html`, and `ui.clipboard.write { text, html?, types? }` writes the same. Both go in one change of the clipboard. `ui.clipboard.read_type(mime)` reads a type back. The types go where Chromium keeps a page's own types (its web custom data: `org.chromium.web-custom-data` on macOS, `Chromium Web Custom MIME Data Format` on Windows, `chromium/x-web-custom-data` on X11 and Wayland). So a page's `getData("application/x-…")` in Chrome, Edge or an Electron app pastes what a zgx app copied, and a zgx app reads what such a page's copy set: a chat answer's live citations pasted into a note. Rust hosts get `Platform::set_clipboard_typed`, `clipboard_types` and `clipboard_typed`. The memory clipboard (`ZGX_CLIPBOARD=memory`) and the test desktop keep the types. A copy without types takes the path it always did. zgx-desktop's clipboard now builds on `zgx-arboard`, arboard 3.6.1 with one write of text, HTML and formats of the caller's (`vendor/arboard/ZGX_PATCH.md`).
- **Scripts read and write the clipboard's HTML** (zgx-script): `ui.clipboard.read_html()` returns what a browser or word processor copied, with its markup, or nil. `ui.clipboard.write_html(text, html)` puts HTML there with its text beside it, for apps that paste either.
- **An app can read the clipboard's HTML** (zgx, zgx-desktop): `Platform::clipboard_html` returns what a browser or word processor copied, with its markup. On macOS that is `public.html`, on Windows the fragment `HTML Format`'s header marks, and on X11 and Wayland `text/html`. `clipboard_text` still reads the plain-text fallback. Writing was already there (`set_clipboard_rich_text`, HTML with its text), and Wayland now reads what it already offered. Hidden until its shape is settled.
- **A copy can carry an image with its text, HTML and types** (zgx-script, zgx-desktop, zgx-dom): `ui.clipboard.write { text, html?, types?, image = png }` puts a PNG on the clipboard as the platform's image beside the rest, in one change, and settles with whether it took them. A figure copied with its citation pastes as the picture into an image editor or a document, as its text in a field, and with its types in a page. The image goes as `public.png` on macOS, `image/png` on X11 and Wayland, and `PNG` with a DIB of it on Windows (decoded off the main thread there). On the web it goes as one `ClipboardItem`, without the types, which can't go with an image there. Rust hosts get `Platform::set_clipboard_typed_image`. On macOS, `clipboard_image` now reads a PNG on the clipboard first (a screenshot's, a browser's Copy Image), where it read only TIFF.
- **A large script heap can ask its collector for less** (zgx-script): `ScriptHost::large_heap_goal(percent)` has a VM whose heap passes 32 MB aim its collector at `percent` of the heap in use (Luau's own lets it double, 200), going back once it settles below 24 MB. The collector can't see what a script's objects hold in Rust, so a large heap left to double keeps long-gone rows and all they hold. It trades CPU for peak: a script churning 100 MB of rows peaks about 23% lower at 150 for about 9% more instructions, and rests where it would. Off by default. No call pays to look: the VM's allocator notices the heap crossing, and the goal changes as that call ends.
- **An app can tell when a node's context menu shows** (zgx, zgx-script, zgx-dom): a state can follow a right-click menu, true while it shows and false as it closes, by Escape, a pick, a click outside, Tab, the window losing focus, a newer menu or the node going; writing it false closes the menu. A PDF reader hides its floating selection popover while the page's menu is open, as the web's does.
- **Direct3D 11 draws backdrop blurs** (zgx-gpu, the experimental `d3d11` feature under `ZGX_D3D11=1`): a backdrop blur, frosted glass among them, drew its box opaque in its own colour, and one over a box with no colour of its own failed the frame. They now draw as on Direct3D 12: blur.wgsl's passes into an atlas of their own, drawn back over the target, mixed by dual-source blending.
- **Direct3D 11 draws blend modes** (zgx-gpu, the experimental `d3d11` feature under `ZGX_D3D11=1`): a frame holding a blended layer failed; it now draws over a copy of the target under it with the same shader as Direct3D 12.
- **Direct3D 11 draws content blurs and masks** (zgx-gpu, the experimental `d3d11` feature under `ZGX_D3D11=1`): a frame holding one drew nothing, failing with an error naming it; their passes now draw into the effect atlas with the same shader as Direct3D 12, byte for byte against it on WARP.
- **Direct3D 11 draws drop shadows** (zgx-gpu, the experimental `d3d11` feature under `ZGX_D3D11=1`): they were left out of the frame; they now draw through the same effect passes as content blurs, byte for byte against Direct3D 12 on WARP.
- **Direct3D 11 draws layer shaders reading the backdrop (glass)** (zgx-gpu, the experimental `d3d11` feature under `ZGX_D3D11=1`): they drew their box opaque; they now draw over a copy of what lies behind them, as Direct3D 12 does, each region's draws done before the copy as Direct3D 12 splits its pass. Backdrop blurs still draw opaque.
- **Direct3D 11 draws GPU views** (zgx-gpu, the experimental `d3d11` feature under `ZGX_D3D11=1`): zgx-3d's worlds, `scene3d` and other `gpu_view` painters drew empty there. They now draw with wgpu on a Direct3D 12 device of the views' own, on Direct3D 11's adapter, into textures shared with Direct3D 11 behind a shared fence. Painters change nothing; `viewport::view_device_features` lists what that device asks for where the adapter has it.
- **Direct3D 11 draws images dithered and as characters** (zgx-gpu, the experimental `d3d11` feature under `ZGX_D3D11=1`): a frame holding one failed; they now draw with the same pixel stage as Direct3D 12, made when one first draws.
- **Direct3D 11 layer shaders read pictures by name** (zgx-gpu, `d3d11`): a layer shader declaring images among its inputs (`zgx_<name>`, `_texel`, `_size`) draws through them, as on Direct3D 12, instead of compositing its layer as painted. Only a layer shader reading a GPU view is still drawn as painted.
- **Direct3D 11 layer shaders read GPU views by name** (zgx-gpu, `d3d11`): a layer shader declaring GPU views among its inputs draws through them, as on Direct3D 12, instead of compositing its layer as painted. Each view's frame is painted at the box's size on the views' own Direct3D 12 device after the shader's passes, and Direct3D 11 waits for it on their shared fence before the frame samples it.
- **Direct3D 11 draws shader fills and layer shaders** (zgx-gpu, the experimental `d3d11` feature under `ZGX_D3D11=1`): an app's `FillShader`s and layer shaders that don't read the backdrop, `radial` gradients, `hue_rotate`, `sepia` and `invert`, and zgx-fx's fills and layer effects draw as they do on Direct3D 12, byte for byte, where a frame holding one failed before. Each one's pixel stage compiles off the frame from the same module, by FXC in a process of its own; until it has, a fill draws its box's colour and a layer as painted. CI now runs Direct3D 11's parity test on Windows, every ready-made effect compiled cold.
- **`ui.debounced(state, ms, { every = true })` tells at each rest** (zgx-script): what reads it hears every time `state` has stayed unchanged for `ms`, even when the value is the one it already held, as when a view's offset is scrolled away and back to where it last rested. Without `every`, a rest at the held value tells nothing, so an app that had to see every rest polled in Luau. With `every`, the wait stays a native timer, and no script runs until the rest.
- **`ui.decode_image(base64)`** (zgx-script): a script makes a `Bitmap` from a PNG, JPEG or GIF's bytes, given in base64 (a picture a server or the host handed over, kept in a database), where it could make one only from the clipboard or alpha. It decodes off the main thread as `ui.image` decodes a file, takes up to 64 MiB, and rejects text that isn't base64 or bytes that aren't a picture. `ui.image { src = bitmap }` shows it.
- A hint for assistive technology can follow state: `View::description_when`, and `description` given a state or a function in scripts, as a sheet grabber says "Double tap to expand" or "collapse" by where the sheet stands.
- **Sound on the system's output** (zgx-desktop, zgx-run, `audio`): `WindowFactory::audio()` is the app's one zgx-audio engine. Making it opens nothing: the default output opens with the first sound and closes after the quiet hold. Voices ending wake the event loop, never polled, and `WindowFactory::on_audio` hears them. The `ui` bus follows macOS's "Play user interface sound effects", read as the engine is made and when the app comes forward. `Application::audio_config` sets the engine's config. zgx-run's `audio` features (`audio`, `audio-effects`, `audio-vorbis`, `audio-mp3`, `audio-opus`, `audio-streams`, `audio-all`) run a Luau app with sound (`cargo run -p zgx-run --features audio -- app/`), and `zgx_run::play_through` does the same for a Rust host's script host. Off by default.
- **A text area's atoms on the DOM** (zgx, zgx-dom): a text area with atoms (`View::atoms_when`, `on_atoms`, `atom_wash`, `atom_style`) is a contenteditable over its model, as the native one is: each edit (typing, deleting, a paste as plain text, undo and redo) is the model's, the text shown again from it (only what changed), the caret put back from it; an input method's text is the model's once committed; its atoms show as chips (their wash, colour, weight and underline). A text area without atoms is the browser's own field, as before. Copy, accessibility and the pointer over atoms follow.
- **Sound for Luau apps in the browser** (zgx-dom, `audio`): `zgx_dom::install_audio(&host)` plays an app's `audio` through zgx-audio's mixer, the desktop's, pulled by an audio worklet. A sound's file is fetched through the host's transport and decoded in Rust. Nothing is made before the first sound, and the context is suspended once the quiet hold has passed. A strict Content-Security-Policy needs `script-src blob:`, or the worklet served and named with `install_audio_at`.
- **`ui.fetch` in the browser** (zgx-dom, `luau`): `zgx_dom::install_fetcher(&host)` gives a Luau app the page's `fetch`. Relative URLs resolve against the app's base (`document.baseURI` when installed, or the URL `install_fetcher_at` names). The desktop's grant and `FetchPolicy` checks apply. Failures reject as `"network"` errors, and a cancelled fetch is aborted. Its JavaScript ships only with `luau`.
- **Text dragged into a page from another app drops on views that take it** (`zgx-dom`): a view with `on_drop::<DraggedText>` hears the browser's drag and drop as it does an in-app drag, its text and, where the source offered `text/html`, its HTML (`DraggedText::html`), as on the desktop. Elsewhere the browser's own drop is left alone.
- **A drag held near a scroll view's edge scrolls it**, as a browser's does: the window's own drag (`on_drag`, `start_drag_from`), another window's or app's, and files from outside. The innermost scroll view under the pointer that can still move toward that edge scrolls while the pointer is within 48 points of it, up to 24 points a 16 ms frame (full speed from 12 in), on either axis, and what is under the pointer hears the drag over it again, so a drop indicator follows the content. Nothing is scheduled in the middle, at the end of what scrolls, or once the drag leaves, drops or ends. zgx-dom's in-app drags scroll alike; the browser scrolls for its own.
- **Text dragged in from another app carries its HTML too** (`DraggedText::html`, hidden while its API settles): where the source offers it (a browser's or a document's selection: `public.html` on macOS, `text/html` on X11 and Wayland, `CF_HTML`'s fragment on Windows), a destination taking `DraggedText` gets it beside the text, to drop rich content as it pastes HTML.
- **Atoms: chips that edit as one unit in a `ui.textarea`** (zgx, zgx-script): a range of the text (a `@file` or `/skill` mention) the caret never enters, one Backspace or Delete removes whole, a selection's end inside it moves out of, and that moves with the edits before it. Its label stays in the text, so copy, search, spellcheck and the accessibility value read plain text. Undo and redo bring a removed one back with its id and payload. `atoms = state` (two-way: `{ id, from, to, data?, style? }`, characters from 0) restores a draft's; `on_atoms = function(change)` hears what was added and removed (`removed` keeps the label and the range it had) and why (`edit`, `undo`, `redo`, `set`); `ui.editor.insert_atom(el, label, { id, data, style, replace })` makes one at the caret or over the `@que` typed for it; `ui.editor.atoms(el)` reads them; `atom_style = { bg }` washes them. In Rust: `TextEditor::{atoms, insert_atom, set_atoms, take_atom_changes}`, `EditorHandle::{insert_atom, set_atoms, on_atoms, set_atom_wash}`, `View::{atom_wash, on_atoms, atoms_when}`. First cut: the wash behind the label, drawn as the editor's other decorations are; padding, radius, an icon and a max width follow.
- **A text area's code blocks scroll sideways and never wrap** (zgx, zgx-script): lines in one scroll group (`LineInset::scroll_group`, a textarea `line_insets` line's `group`) scroll sideways together under a sideways wheel or Shift and the wheel, shown inside the group's frame (`GroupFrame`, `EditorHandle::set_group_frame`); a `nowrap` line is shaped once at its full width, as the web's `white-space: pre` with `overflow-x: auto`. The offset moves a group's lines after their own layout, in a layer of their own the renderer copies: a sideways scroll shapes nothing. Carets, clicks and selection follow it, a caret moved past the frame brings it into view, `LineMark::pinned` keeps a block's wash and line numbers still, and `View::group_scrollbar` (`group_scrollbar = true`) shows the scroll views' scrollbar across the block that moved last. `EditorHandle::set_group_scroll`, `group_scroll`, `group_extent` and `on_group_scroll` read and move it (all hidden while they settle). A text area without groups lays out and draws as before.
- **A text area's line can be hidden** (zgx): `LineInset::hidden()` from `set_line_insets` takes a line's room and node away (a closed toggle's lines); the app keeps the caret out of it.
- **A text area's lines can take room of their own** (zgx): `EditorHandle::set_line_insets` gives each line an indent and space above and below (`LineInset`), so a note's list items and headings sit as they do on the web. A line wraps in what its indent leaves, and its carets, hits, selection and the lines below follow. An editor without insets keeps none, and lays out and scrolls as before.
- **A text area draws shapes beside its lines** (zgx, zgx-script): `EditorHandle::set_line_marks` and a textarea's `line_marks = function(line, state) … end` give each line boxes (`LineMark`): a quote's rule, the wash behind a code block's line, a horizontal rule, a list item's number as a label (monospaced or in tabular figures). They're placed where their lines are, under any room above, scroll with them under the text (or over it), and are asked once for each line text and state. A text area without marks draws as before.
- **An editor measures its text at any width** (zgx, zgx-script): `EditorHandle::text_metrics(width)` and a script element's `el:text_metrics(width)` give the widest hard line unwrapped, the height wrapped at `width`, the line height and a revision, for sizing a composer to its text without a hidden copy of it. They measure what the editor shows (styled spans that shape, an IME composition, a final empty line), not its placeholder or insets, and are nil for a password field. A reader in an effect or computed hears content and typography changes once laid out, not focus, selection, scroll or the editor's own size. The first query at a new width or revision lays out every line, apart from the editor's own view; repeating it is free.
- **A host can have an editor check its spelling again** (zgx): `EditorHandle::recheck_spelling()` forgets what a spellchecked editor (`View::spellcheck`) had checked and asks the platform about its whole text again, so the underline under a word the host just learned or ignored through the platform (`Platform::learn_spelling`, `ignore_spelling`) goes, and an edit elsewhere does not bring it back. It does nothing while the editor isn't spellchecked. zgx's own menu is unchanged.
- **A text area takes `.scrollbar(true)`** (and `scrollbar_style`): the bar scroll views show, at its box's edge and over its padding, following and moving the editor's scroll. `EditorHandle::scroll_offset`, `set_scroll_offset` and `scroll_extent` read and set it.
- **A text area's span can draw its background as a chip** (zgx): `SpanStyle::chip(padding, radius)` pads a span's background across and down round its text, taking room in the line across, with round corners, as an inline code chip on the web. It's a look, not an editing unit: atoms (`insert_atom`) stay what edits as one unit.
- **A text area's spans take a size, a line pitch, an underline and the monospaced font** (zgx, zgx-script): a highlighter's span can set `size`, `line_height`, `underline` and `font = "mono"` (`SpanStyle::size`, `line_height`, `underline`, `mono`), so a note's headings, links and inline code show in one editor. Size, pitch and font shape the line, so carets, clicks and wrapping follow them. Spans without them stay as small as before: a span names its size, pitch and font in two bytes, each different one kept once (`SpanText`).
- **Focus and a field's selection can be put back as they were** (zgx, zgx-script): `Ui::focus_snapshot` / `Ui::restore_focus` (hidden, internal) and Luau `ui.focus_snapshot()` with `:restore()` (internal, subject to change) take what has keyboard focus and a text field's, textarea's or code editor's selection, and put them back later without scrolling; false if the node is gone or its text no longer holds the offsets.
- **`Value::folded`** (zgx-script): a table a host keeps small until a script reads it: to scripts, the table it opens to (`Unfold`), going when no script holds it. Its `id` reads without opening it, so a long list of them keys and counts with none of them opened.
- **`zgx inspect frames` names the states a frame wrote by where the script made them**: `500 × sidebar.luau:697 use_state` (or `computed`, `tween`, `spring`, `loop`), and an element's restyles or a node's style functions by what they are, where it said "500 unnamed". A list whose rows each write a state of their own now says which. Devtools only: built without `inspect`, nothing changes.
- **`zgx inspect frames` names the rest of a frame's writes by the Rust line that made their signals** (`31 × zgx-script/src/lower.rs:8290`), where no script line or kind names them: an engine's per-row or per-block state reads as where it comes from, not "31 unnamed". Devtools only (`trace`): default builds are unchanged.
- **A frozen clock for screenshot runs** (zgx, zgx-script, zgx-desktop): `ZGX_FREEZE_TIME=1` at launch, or `zgx inspect clock freeze`, holds every animation, tween, timer (`ui.after`, `ui.every`), caret blink, tooltip delay and frame at one time (a virtual clock on 1 January 2000), so spinners, carets and transitions are the same each run; `zgx inspect clock advance <ms>` moves it on by hand, waking the timers due by then in order, and `clock thaw` lets it run. Frames delivered to a `FrameClock` under a virtual clock carry its time, and baked loops (macOS) draw each tick while it is virtual.
- **A layer shader can read what lies behind its node and paint past its box**: `zgx_backdrop(p)` reads it (a heat haze, a lens, a chromatic split), `layer_bleed` runs the shader past the box (a glow, a ripple), `layer_reach` says how far past that it reads, and `input.radii` gives the box's corners. Rust: `Styled::layer_bleed` and `layer_reach`, `Scene::set_layer_extent`.
- **The engine can keep a shader up with no script per frame**: `layer_clock` and `shader_clock` step its time on the frame clock while its node shows (off screen, hidden or with motion reduced it holds no timer), `layer_on` takes it and its layer away while false, and `layer_params` may be a function, followed. Rust: `View::shader_drive`.
- **Glass that is the system's on macOS 26** (`ui.glass`, `zgx::glass::glass`): an `NSGlassEffectView` between the window's planes where it's available, and elsewhere a drawn material measured against it, with the same script. `GlassMaterial::Liquid` puts it behind a whole window.
- **An app's layer shaders compile off the frame**, as fills do: the first frames drawing one show the layer as painted, not a frozen window. `ui.layer_shader` starts compiling as it's declared.
- **`zgx inspect gc --retained` says who holds what, Luau or Rust** (zgx-script): the answer counts the matches a Luau holder alone reaches, against the rest, held only through the handles Rust keeps (the auxiliary thread's stack, where every `Function`, `Table` or userdata a Rust object holds sits). `--lua-only` shows the Luau holders' chains (a global, a module table, a cache) instead of the handle chains, which otherwise win the shortest path to everything Rust also holds.
- **`zgx inspect gc` names who holds what survives** (zgx-script): `--retained KIND` (a userdata type such as `State`, `table`, `function`, or part of a function's name or chunk) prints the commonest chains of holders from a root to those objects, `registry → [n] → thread → stack → function on_click @app:4 → upvalue#1 → table → .run → …`, with each function's chunk and line and how many objects share a chain (`--sample N`, default 5); `--mark` remembers the objects there are, and `--since-mark` lists after more actions what survived a full collection that wasn't there then (by kind, counts, bytes), `--retained` then looking only at those. Upvalues are numbered, not named: the heap dump doesn't carry their names, and objects held only from Rust show under the registry.
- Glass takes each corner's own radius: `GlassStyle::corners` and `ui.glass { corners = { tl, tr, br, bl } }`, as a box's `corners` does. The drawn glass rounds each corner; macOS's system glass takes one radius, `radius`.
- **`GpuStats::regions`** (zgx-gpu): how many damage regions a window frame drew, once nearby damage is joined.
- **`zgx inspect gc`** (zgx-script): a full Luau collection in each script VM, with the heap before and after (what was garbage) and what is left by object type, userdata type and the chunk each function's code came from (`--top N` rows each). Run it again after the same actions to tell a leak from garbage. MCP tool `zgx_gc`.
- **`zgx inspect mark` answers the frames' clock** (zgx-script): its JSON's `ms` is now on the clock of the frames' `ended_ms`, so a tool posting input through the system lines its clock up with the frames' and reads each key's time to the frame that showed it.
- **`zgx inspect` drives drags from outside the app, and reduced motion** (zgx-script): `files hover|drop|leave <target|x,y> <path…>` (files from a file manager: `on_file_hover`, then a `FileDrop` each and `FilesDrop`, `on_file_drop`), `text-drop <target|x,y> --text T [--html H] [--hover-only]` (text from another app, into a field or a `drop_when`), and `prefers reduced-motion on|off|system` (what this process reads as the system's preference, never its setting), through the same code the desktop host's events take; MCP tools `zgx_files`, `zgx_text_drop`, `zgx_prefers`.
- **`zgx inspect wait --text` is met by what a field holds** (zgx-script): an input's or an editor's value counts as well as the text a node says, so waiting for typed or dropped text into a labelled field no longer times out; a timeout names the value it held.
- **`zgx inspect route x,y` says where a click there goes** (zgx-script): to a native view (a page, a terminal) or the interface, and what decided it: overlay paint or a listener in front, a disabled or hidden node, or a modal scope the page is outside of, named by its root. It also lists each page under the point with every node it is registered at, and whether that node shows. A page that ignores clicks can be diagnosed without clicking it.
- **`zgx inspect type --no-click` and `clipboard set --files`** (zgx-script): `type --no-click <text>` types where focus is, its caret and selection left as they are (a click first would move them); `clipboard set --files a.pdf b.png` puts files on an app's own clipboard (`ZGX_CLIPBOARD=memory`) as a file manager copies them, so `key cmd+v` pastes them into an `on_paste` as files, and `clipboard` reads them back on a `files:` line. Both are the MCP tools' too (`no_click`, `files`).
- **`zgx inspect windows` lists popup panels with their owner and state** (zgx-script): each window's line is followed by every popup it wants open (`popup <node>  menu  shown  owner main`), held back ones too (the app at the back), and its record carries `panels: [{ what, role, owner, shown, held, x, y, width, height }]` beside the existing `popups` list.
- **kdialog's file chooser where no portal answers** (zgx-desktop, Linux): on a KDE session (`XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP` naming KDE) with no `org.freedesktop.portal.FileChooser`, open, save and folder dialogs show kdialog's chooser, KDE's own, before zenity's; elsewhere kdialog stands in where zenity is missing. It is run, attached to the window on X11 and ended with it as zenity's is.
- **`zgx lint` reports a row's branches built whether they show or not** (zgx-script): the new `eager-branch` rule finds a `ui.show` or `ui.switch` branch built straight in a list's or `ui.each`'s row, which every row builds even when it never shows (an editor while renaming, a retry button after a failure).
- **`zgx lint`'s `update-copy` sees a clone for one change** (zgx-script): `local copy = table.clone(state:peek())`, one change to it, then `state:set(copy)` is `state:update` written out, and costs as much: the whole value through Luau for each change. It says what does the change alone.
- **`zgx lint` flags whole-reply passes around a streamed `ui.markdown`** (zgx-script): `computed-markdown-blocks` for a computed reading all of a markdown state's blocks, `computed-markdown-rewrite` for `ui.markdown` given text a computed rewrites whole. Each makes a chunk cost the reply's length; the row builder and rewriting each piece as it's appended stay flat.
- **A list can say how a row's data makes its height** (zgx-script): `ui.list { measured = true, estimate = { … } }`, declared once, with Rust running it per row. Rows out of view stand at about what they'll be, so the scrollbar is the right length and a dragged thumb lands where it points, before any is built.
- **Markdown links GFM's autolink literals, when asked** (zgx-script): `ui.markdown(text, { autolinks = true })`, `ui.markdown_view { text, autolinks = true }` and `markdown::Options::autolinks` link `https://…` as written, `www.…` to `http://…` and emails to `mailto:`, leaving out trailing punctuation, an unbalanced `)` and an entity-like `&name;` as GFM's spec says, and keeping the text's marks. As remark-gfm (what a web chat renders with), `ftp://` is no link. Code, links and files hold none, and without the option nothing changes.
- **Markdown shows inline HTML's marks, when asked** (zgx-script): `ui.markdown(text, { html_marks = true })`, `ui.markdown_view { text, html_marks = true }` and `markdown::Options::html_marks` style `b`/`strong`, `i`/`em`, `s`/`del`/`strike` and `code`/`kbd`/`samp`/`tt`, link an `a href` (relative, or a protocol GitHub's schema keeps), and break the line at `br`, as a web chat rendering markdown with raw HTML and GitHub's sanitizing schema does. Other tags are dropped with their text kept, block HTML stays dropped, and without the option nothing changes.
- **Markdown reads its options from the table given** (zgx-script): `ui.markdown`'s and `ui.markdown_view`'s `breaks`, `paths`, `html_marks` and `copy` are read raw, by names a VM makes once, so a call costs no string made or protected call for each; options a props table only inherits through a metatable's `__index` are no longer read.
- **A memory report says what script states and fonts hold** (zgx-script, zgx-gpu): `zgx inspect memory` lists the named states holding the most, each with its rows and its text, every value two states share counted once, and all of them together; and the bytes of fonts given as data (on the heap) and of those mapped from files. Worked out only when a report asks.
- **A memory report names what an interface holds beyond its slots** (zgx, zgx-script, zgx-gpu, zgx-desktop): nodes' child lists, layout results, advanced layouts, extras and plain texts, and the scene's tables; the reactive runtime's effects (each closure's captures and what it reads), the signals they read and the values effects own; input listeners; semantics; what the interface keeps per node; the font database and how many fonts are loaded; the accessibility projection; and, of a script's VM, the pages pooled outside the program's heap. Each is worked out only when a report asks. A page of a thousand nodes at rest has 87% of its heap named, which a test holds above 85%.
- **A model posed at the script's time** (`ui.model`): `time` (seconds: a number, a state's or a function's) poses its `animation` there, followed as it changes, in place of playing the clip round on its own clock. A video's frame, a slider or a scrubbed timeline says where the pose is; a render in chunks poses each frame alike.
- **A native module shows images by name** (zgx-module, zgx-script): a module's thread writes a picture into an `ImageBuffer` (RGBA8, rows tightly packed) and a job shows it as `host:<module>.<name>` with `host.image`, its bytes allocated as the image's own so nothing is copied; `host.remove_image` removes it. What a module sets goes with its host, and as the module is loaded into its host again. [The guide](docs/modules.md) says how.
- **Native modules reach their host: threads, timers, shared state and async actions** (zgx-module, zgx-script): a module's threads post jobs to the interface's thread through a `Send` handle (`Host::ui`, `Ui::post`), where values are made; `host.after` and `host.every` run on zgx's clock; `host.shared(key, initial)` is the host's shared state scripts read with `ui.shared`, set reconciling, appended to and observed; `#[export(async)]` takes a `Promise<T>` the module settles later, which scripts get from `ui.call` or the module's table, typed by `zgx types`. Nothing is made until a module uses it, and a module holding its host costs an idle app no wake. Unstable before 1.0, as native modules are. [The guide](docs/modules.md) says how.
- **The `zgx_monitor` example follows an app's process tree and shows the names an app gives its processes** (zgx-desktop): `--attach <name>` (or its Attach menu) narrows the sidebar to the processes of that name and every process they started. On macOS an app can name its helpers, which other tools can't rename, and publish counts of its own (its open tabs) in `$TMPDIR/zgx-monitor/labels-<pid>`, written only while the monitor's heartbeat beside it is fresh; the example's module documentation gives the format. Shipped in an app bundle, the monitor runs the scripts in `Contents/Resources/monitor/`.
- **An app's windows by name, and the one that opens first, from `zgx.json`** (zgx-run): `"named_windows": { "main": { … }, "intro": { …, "script": "intro.luau" } }` names windows, each with the window keys and platform blocks of the top level and a script of its own if it opens one; `"first": "main"` opens that one, and `zgx run --window intro app/` (`zgx-run --window`) another, a name it doesn't have an error listing those it does (`ui.args` are as they were). A key comes from the first that has it of the named window's platform block, the window, the top level's platform block and the top level.
