- **A scroll tells what shows by a range check, not a walk** (zgx): every node whose visibility is observed (`NodeHandle::observe_visibility`, `observe_visibility_within`, Luau's `on_visible`) keeps what of it shows inside the clips that move with its nearest scroll content, and a frame that only scrolls checks that, moved with the content, against the clip above it, worked out once for each scroll content. Where it comes within a rounding of an edge, the full walk answers, so observers hear what they did. Any other translation, a change to a clip, a node hidden or shown, an opacity to or from zero, layout or the node's own move works it out again; what was kept goes at rest. In a transcript of rows nested 30 boxes deep with their visibility observed, the visibility pass a frame went from 1.3–1.7 ms to 0.2–0.5 ms on an M5.
- **A frame looks only at observed sizes that changed** (zgx): after a layout, every node whose size is observed (`on_resize`, a canvas's size, a terminal's rows) had its size worked out and gathered, then compared before publishing. Now each is compared as it's worked out, and only those resized are gathered. heavy-scroll −0.115% and layout-drag −0.105% instructions; a terminal at 64 rows −1.9%.
- **A package's modules compile once for every host that requires them** (zgx-script): a script host made after another or beside it, on any thread, loads a package's bytecode (`ScriptHost::package`, zgx.json's `"packages"`; zgx-video's `@video` and `@fx`) rather than compiling it again: a video's stages, its render workers and a player building its stage again compile `@video` once. No host holds it: the shared table keeps it until a host goes idle, which releases it all, so an app at rest holds what it did before. A hot-reloading host keeps its packages' modules by path, as its other files.
- **A damaged paint walk no longer reserves room for the whole window** (zgx): each walk sized its list from the window's last walk, so a frame that repainted a strip or two after a whole repaint (a streamed reply's fade, after a chunk scrolled the view) allocated for every item and gave it back. Whole and damaged walks keep their own size hints.
- **The paint walk does less per node** (zgx): nodes it reaches through the tree (children, as the paint and ink walks find them) are looked up without checking their generation again; a style whose fade bands are all zero is known not to fade before the loop over them; and a frame with one damage rect (a scroll's, most) tests each node against it once. What is drawn is the same.
- **A looping animation plays from its sheet under a panel that paints elsewhere** (zgx): what counted as painting over a box was any later box whose ink, the union of everything it holds, reached it, though nothing it held landed there: a window-wide panel with a shadow at one edge, or an overlay column, kept every declared loop under it drawing each tick (a chat's two "working" rings cost about 3% of a core this way). What it holds is now asked in turn, each inside its clip, and only what lands on the box counts; what is scaled, turned, blurred or shaded still counts its whole ink. Carets and native views over the window, which ask the same, follow.
- **A paused video player gives back the pixel buffers it no longer shows** (zgx-video, zgx-gpu, macOS): a player shown as pixel buffers kept two past the one shown for as long as it was paused, 7 MB at 1280 by 720. It now gives them back once it has rested a few seconds (once a pause, never while playing), and its pool is flushed before CoreVideo's texture cache, which held a texture, and the buffer with it, of each buffer the pool still had.
- **A picture in a list's row built ahead starts decoding before it shows** (zgx, zgx-gpu): a kept JPEG or PNG decoded only as it was first drawn, so in a fast scroll through photos each showed its box empty for frames while its turn came. A row built ahead in spare time now asks for its pictures' pixels at the size they'll be drawn (`ImageData::decode_ahead`, by `zgx::image::fitted_size`, which the renderer uses too), the rows the view goes towards first, nearest first. At most `AHEAD_MOST` (4) wait at once, after any a picture drawn waits for; one whose row goes before its turn isn't decoded. A list with no spare time, or one at rest, asks for nothing.
- **A text the cache doesn't hold is hashed once** (zgx-gpu): measuring plain text hashed it to look it up, then again to file it. The second time it reuses the first, and heavy-both's flush is 0.4% shorter.
- **A paused video player holds what a gone one does** (zgx-video): left alone, it gives back its stage (the composition's script, interface and renderer) as well as its kept frames, and builds it again when next asked for a frame. Showing the showcase in a 960×540 view, a paused player rests at about 62 MB, the same as with the player dropped, down from about 102 MB; a seek from rest shows in the same 60 to 85 ms.
- **A PNG drawn small is read row by row and averaged as it comes** (zgx-gpu): a kept PNG used to decode whole (48 MB for a 4000 by 3000 photo) before it was shrunk to the size drawn, and the allocator kept that peak; it now decodes at the largest whole fraction that still covers the drawn size (about 896 across for 448 points at 2x), holding a few MB at once. Drawn larger, it decodes again at the larger size; interlaced PNGs still decode whole. JPEGs already decoded scaled.
- **A pointer event's table is made in one call** (zgx-script): each handler a pointer event reaches gets a table of `x`, `y`, `local_x`, `local_y` and the keys held. It was filled field by field from Rust, and with a memory limit set (as every script host has) each `set` was a protected call of its own, about 18 a table. A small Luau function, compiled once per state, now makes it: the same table, new for each event, with the same fields. On a chat page at 120 moves a second, a move's dispatch went from about 515k to 217k instructions.
- **On Linux a present copies into the window only what changed** (zgx-gpu, Vulkan): each frame copied its whole retained image into the swap chain's, a memcpy of the window under a software rasterizer for a hover over two cells. Each swap chain image now takes the changes of the frames it missed (its age, by image), and the compositor is told which regions changed (`VK_KHR_incremental_present`, where the device has it), so it redraws those alone. Anything uncertain (a new or resized swap chain, a suboptimal image, an expose, the window shown again) copies all of it. On sway and X11, frame_clock's moving box copied 11,700 of 124,800 pixels a present.
- **A frame lets go of what the renderer keeps for nodes from those that changed, not a walk of all it keeps** (zgx, zgx-gpu): each frame whose content changed (a terminal's rows drawn again, a list scrolled) looked over every cached shape, raster and texture, and walked the whole scene for the images it shows. The scene now names the nodes removed (`Scene::resource_changes`); the renderer counts the images nodes show as it draws them and looks again only at those, so a frame with nothing removed and no image replaced prunes nothing.
- **A window at rest a while gives Metal's queue back** (zgx-gpu, wgpu-hal, zgx-desktop): the driver keeps a queue's memory for as long as it lives, a pool of command buffers' resources and render scratch, up to 174 MB on an M5 Max. A window at rest 10 s past its idle trim lets its queue go, and the next frame makes another. A queue idle that long starts as slowly as a new one.
- **The software raster parses its bundled font when text first needs it, once for the process** (zgx-desktop): every window drawn on the raster parsed DejaVu Sans with fontdue as it was made, every glyph's outline (about 17 MB of heap), though only text drawn without the scene's text services falls back to it. It is parsed the first time a raster draws with it, and shared by every raster in the process.
- **The software raster draws a layer only where its frame repaints it** (zgx-desktop): a group drawn as a layer (faded as one, transformed, blended, masked) was drawn whole into two mattes for every area a frame repainted, even one far from it, and each matte copied the bundled font, every glyph's outline. A layer away from the repaint now costs nothing, and the mattes share the font. A terminal window's close button is two rotated strokes: under output, each frame paid for them though only the terminal's rows changed.
- **The software raster fills a box's inside without its coverage math** (zgx-desktop): a window drawn on the raster (a machine with no GPU) took a rounded-corner distance and its square root for every pixel of every box it painted, though inside the box, past its corners and its border, that comes to the fill colour. Those pixels are now filled directly (a plain fill when opaque); the corners, edges and border as before, pixel for pixel. A terminal under output, repainting its background each frame, used 17% less CPU (the 200 MB flood in a window on Linux, 2.11 s to 1.75 s) and 20% less for `seq 1 1000000`.
- **The software raster backend moves a scroll's pixels rather than repainting its view** (zgx-desktop, zgx-gpu): `Raster::render` copies what scrolled by whole pixels within its view and repaints only the strip scrolled in, and what the move carried of anything over or around the view, with the GPU renderer's own rules (`zgx_gpu::scroll_copy_repaints`). A 1280 by 800 list scrolled at 0.17 ms a frame where it took 13.3.
- **The software raster draws an image without a transform a row at a time** (zgx-desktop): each pixel of an image (a canvas's drawing too: a terminal's rows are canvases) was mapped back through the image's transform, divided into its texel and clamped, though with no transform and no dither the texel's column depends on the pixel's column alone and its row on its row. Those are now worked out once each and the pixels blended along the row, as before pixel for pixel; and a clear or opaque colour is blended without the sums it comes to.
- **A removed subtree is named to a renderer without copying it** (zgx): removing a node copied each node's children under it into a list of its own, to name them to a renderer that drops what they held (`Scene::resource_changes`). The subtree is now walked into the list it is named in, which keeps its room between flushes. A fast scroll through deep rows (scroll-glass) allocates about 30 times a frame fewer.
- **A window at rest keeps what it shows, and gives back the rest** (zgx, zgx-gpu, zgx-script, zgx-desktop): as a window goes idle, text keeps only what is drawn, slots past the last node in use go from the scene and its effects, rasters no node shows go, and markdown streams not asked for in a minute. The glyph atlas goes after 6 s at rest, not 30.
- **A window at rest keeps the sizes of a node's other layout results, not their children's places** (zgx): about 11% less memory per node on a list of rows. The first layout after rest that needs one lays it out again.
- **`ui.router`'s history keeps each place in its own bytes** (zgx-script): a place behind or ahead is kept as one string (its route's name and path, interned), not the route's tables. Those were made as the app moved, among what the move made and dropped, so with unlimited history (`history = math.huge`) each one held a Luau heap page long after the rest of it was free: 1,200 moves held 1.1 MB more than keeping one place, and now hold about 30–130 KB more. Back and forward return the same routes, params read from the path as `navigate` makes them.
- **A long list's rows are no copy of it in the VM** (zgx-script): a list's row table, read through `state:at` or `ui.list`, lives while a script holds it. The conversion cache held every row read for as long as the list stood, so scrolling a chat from end to end built a second copy of its transcript in the script heap, which a collection could not free. The cache also gives back its size as a host goes idle.
- **Turning on `scene3d` adds no test for every node** (zgx-script): with the feature on, `needs_context` tested each node for a 3D scene on its own, and the variant in the middle of `Kind` moved the others' discriminants. It's the last variant now, tested with the kinds that need a component context.
- **A screen reader hears a name that changes every frame without the tree's geometry sent each frame too** (zgx-desktop): with VoiceOver, Switch Control, Narrator, NVDA or JAWS on, a name or value changing each frame (a timer, a status) updated the whole tree each frame, every moved node's box and text runs with it. A change a reader hears still goes out the frame it happens, alone; geometry keeps its pace (at most every 100 ms), as it does for any client. A sidebar sliding beside a long transcript with a status changing each frame spent 64% less time in the tree's updates.
- **A Luau node takes about half the memory** (zgx-script): the fields few nodes set (`layout`, `mask`, `theme`, `tooltip`, `disabled`, `pointer_events` and the rest) and the drag and drop handlers are each boxed, made only for the nodes that set them. A Luau table of 10,000 rows in the browser peaks at 43.6 MB of wasm memory, down from 47.8, and a second create of the rows holds 34.4 MB live rather than 38.1.
- **A script's node is written into its place** (zgx-script): each `ui.*` call's node (hundreds of bytes) is written once into the allocation that holds it, where `Rc::new` moved it twice. The mount benchmark does 0.26% fewer instructions.
- **A Luau node with a style that changes keeps less** (zgx-script): its style closure no longer holds a background image and a shader's fill and parameters (two 64-byte blocks) inline, on the many nodes with neither. A Luau table of 10,000 rows in the browser holds 24.5 MB live rather than 27.7, and peaks at 38.4 MB of wasm memory rather than 43.7.
- **A steady trackpad stream follows the fingers sooner** (zgx-desktop): after eight regular intervals, input arriving at a multiple of the display rate gradually uses half the sampling delay. Sparse, irregular and mismatched input retains its interpolation margin; wheel easing and the number of frames requested are unchanged. No motion is predicted beyond the input received.
- **Scrolling content with nothing that paints as a layer skips its paint walk** (zgx): each scrolled frame used to walk every visible node of the content to confirm it could be copied. Now the scene keeps a list of the nodes that may paint otherwise (layers, edge fades, images with an effect). The list is brought up to date from what each frame repainted, and the walk is skipped when none of those nodes is inside the content. A transcript under a frosted composer scrolls with about 6% fewer instructions a frame; no setter does more than before.
- **A glass bar over a scroll view no longer repaints the whole view** (zgx, zgx-gpu): what's clear of the bar's blur moves as a copy, and only the band it reaches repaints. A long transcript under a glass composer: 4% fewer instructions over a scroll.
- **Scrolling copies pixels past effects that move with the content**: tables and code blocks fading at their edges, clips fading at their foot and layers inside a scroll view no longer make it repaint its view each frame. A fade the view's edge cuts repaints just what it holds; a backdrop blur, a blend, a shader reading the window or the backdrop, or a filtered layer under a fade still repaints.
- **A scroll view copies past a looping animation in its content**: a spinner played by a layer (a working indicator at the foot of a streaming reply) no longer makes the view repaint on every move; only the spinner's own box repaints.
- **A scroll view copies its pixels under overlays and over a see-through card** (zgx-gpu): something that stays put over a scroll view (a composer floating on a transcript) repaints only its own ink where it is and where the copy put it, and a card of one colour under the view, however see-through, repaints only its rounded corners. The view used to repaint whole. A blurred overlay still repaints with what's behind it.
- **A trackpad's scroll follows the fingers more closely at 60 Hz, and slow scrolling no longer steps at 30 Hz** (zgx-desktop): at a refresh of 12 ms or longer, a scroll stands 8 ms behind the motion that has come, not a whole event interval, while events keep up. Events about 30 a second, on any display, are bridged rather than running out between them and jumping.
- **A scroll at 60 Hz shows newer motion on macOS** (zgx-desktop): a frame starts as late as it still makes its refresh, so what it shows is its work and a few milliseconds old, not a whole refresh. Faster displays don't change.
- **A long column's frames find what they repaint by search**: a damaged frame asked every child of a list whether its paint met the damage, so a long streamed reply cost more to draw each chunk as it grew. A container of 64 children or more whose rows run down in order is now searched for the few that can.
- **A labelled node's accessibility information takes 80 bytes, not 336** (zgx): about 10% less memory per node on a list of labelled rows, with or without an assistive client. Fields few nodes have (`min`, `description`, `placeholder`, …) read and assign as before.
- **A `ui.show` showing nothing is one node, not two** (zgx, zgx-script): closed with no `otherwise`, it is its region alone, hidden out of layout as the empty box in it was (no flex item, no gap), not a region holding a hidden empty box. A long list of rows, each with a closed show (a tool row's opened part), makes a node fewer a row. `zgx::compose::switch_or_none` is the switch whose cases may show nothing.
- **A column that grows where it stands repaints only what it adds**: a streamed reply's column damaged every block it held each time it grew (a chunk's cost grew with the reply). A plain container that only grows or shrinks in place now damages nothing of its own, and each child whose box changes damages itself.
- **A picture of what a window showed scales only when it must** (zgx-desktop, macOS): a snapshot that fits its width is converted a pixel at a time instead of box-filtered, about 4× faster (a 1151×817 pt area at 2×: 12.9 ms to 2.7 ms). A picture of part of a node reads and scales only that part, so a dragged row's preview out of a long note takes about 0.1 ms, not 13 ms.
- **Rows a list builds in spare time leave observers to the frame** (zgx): each row a list builds ahead of its view in a refresh's spare time was laid out by a frame's preparation, which gathered and published every observed node's bounds and visibility too, some 47 times a second while scrolling. It is now a preparation ahead of the frame's, as dispatching input is: rows are built and laid out, and the frame's own preparation delivers observations once before it draws. On an M5 at 120 Hz with a transcript's rows observed, a scroll's CPU a frame went down about 9%.
- **A frame with a backdrop blur makes its bind groups once, not every frame** (zgx-gpu): it made the blur's bind groups (up to five) and two viewport groups anew each frame, each a trip through wgpu's validation, because they bound ranges of that frame's vertex buffer. The blur's records are now bound at dynamic offsets and its groups kept; the viewport's groups are kept by where the frame's regions lie, which a frame whose quads kept their size shares with the last; Direct3D 11 keeps its blur records buffers from frame to frame; and the frame reads the device's storage alignment once, not through wgpu's limits each frame. A scrolled frame under a frosted panel makes one bind group where it made seven, and about 93 fewer allocations (of some 490).
- A deep tree on a small stack (iOS's main thread, 1 MB) mounts and lays out without mapping a stack segment per node: past the low mark, every sibling ran on a segment of its own, mapped, guarded and freed each time. On iOS each thread now keeps one and reuses it; desktop builds are unchanged.
- **A table a script puts in state is read faster** (zgx-script): its strings take the fast UTF-8 check, and the converter's address-keyed maps (strings by their Luau string, values held by the cache) hash by a multiply, not SipHash. The mail example's script load, which puts 4,000 messages in state, does 9.7% fewer instructions (221.4M → 200.0M), with the same values and tables.
- **A table given to state is read with one allocation of its own** (zgx-script): each list and map a state was given gathered its items in a vector of its own, then copied them into its value (a list twice); the walk now gathers every table's on two stacks it reuses, and each table's are moved off into one allocation. A 10,000-message feed given to `ui.state` converts in 18-23 ms, not 22-29 (headless, M5 Max).
- **A streamed Markdown chunk's script and update cost the same at 800 sections as at 100** (zgx, zgx-script): `ui.markdown` given a state answers its blocks as a list grown from the last answer, in chunks it shares with it (held folded, so no other value pays for it), and `ui.each` hands `keyed` only the keys after those that stayed (`keyed_grown`, `Keys::Grown`), which it moves and orders alone (`Scene::reorder_children_from`). An edit to a long text finds the line breaks of what it adds alone, and a text without carriage returns is not read for them. Per chunk, script 98K → 103K and update 137K → 135K instructions from 100 to 800 sections (they had grown to 127K and 367K); `md_chunk_flat` holds both flat.
- **A streamed paragraph prepares, measures and lays out only what each chunk changes** (zgx-gpu): each chunk of a reply streamed into one paragraph took a copy of every word before it, packed them all again to measure, and broke and placed every line again. It now takes the words and lines of the state it grew from, measures from the first line its new words can change, and lays out from there, the lines before kept: its words, measuring and lines cost the same for a chunk at 10 lines as at 1,000 (the text is still hashed whole to find it), laid out bit for bit as the whole paragraph is.
- **A box laid out by taffy finds each child's measure without hashing** (zgx): the vendored taffy keeps a node's context in a dense map by slot, where it hashed the slot with SipHash every time it laid a child out. A split dragged between panes of wrapping text costs 0.7% fewer instructions, and the mail and sketch apps 0.1%.
- **Plain ASCII output is dispatched without a UTF-8 check** (zgx-terminal): each run of output between escapes was checked as UTF-8 and then walked again for its printable text; ASCII now goes straight, and the check starts only at the first byte that isn't ASCII. Printing a coloured 20 MB log takes 4% fewer instructions.
- **A terminal cell is 16 bytes, not 24** (zgx-terminal): its joined characters, underline colour and hyperlink are kept by its row, so a cell has nothing to drop: clearing a line is a copy of blanks, and text in the default colours is blanked in one copy and given its characters. Printing a coloured 20 MB log takes 25% fewer instructions, and a 10,000-line history 11 MB less memory (35 MB to 24 MB resident). A search through the history reads a candidate's second character before matching the rest: 7% fewer instructions searching 100,000 lines.
- **Plain text written into a fresh line is stored without a check per cell** (zgx-terminal): a flood writes each line into a row the scroll just reset, whose cells hold nothing, so they're now stored straight instead of each being read for a wide character and an extra first. The parser's share of printing a coloured 20 MB log went from 378M instructions to 243M, 17% of the whole.
- **A terminal reads a stream of small writes in fewer, larger reads on macOS** (zgx-terminal): a program writing in small pieces (`seq`, a build's log) no longer waits on the tty's lock at every write while the session reads a few bytes at a time. From the second short read in a row, the session's thread lets go of the screen and sleeps 2 µs before reading on. `seq 1 1000000` in a window finished in 0.58 s instead of 0.85 s, with the app's CPU from 1.08 s to 0.52 s. A keystroke's echo and a stream filling the queue (`cat` of a large file) never wait, and both measured as before. Linux reads as it did: its ptys measured slower with any pause.
- **A terminal row splits into its runs more cheaply** (zgx-terminal): telling where a run's style ends compares every cell's two colours with its neighbour's, now as numbers (a named colour by its index, an RGB by its three bytes at once). A many-coloured log scrolling through the engine-drawn terminal takes about 1.4% fewer instructions.
- **A flood of plain text parses about a third faster** (zgx-terminal): a run of printable text is found eight bytes at a time rather than one, written over its cells in one pass (nothing mapped or cloned for plain ASCII with no hyperlink or underline colour), and a line scrolled into the history with nothing extra in its cells is cleared field by field. Printing a coloured 20 MB log went from 540M instructions to 378M in the parser, and from 185M to 163M clearing lines; the gate's `terminal` and `terminal-reader` scenes count 3% fewer.
- **A terminal tells a screen reader of new output for what changed, not the whole screen** (zgx-terminal): each read's news compared every row on screen and searched what was typed for the whole of it, so a line streaming in at the bottom cost the screen's text, more with every row. Rows that didn't all change are compared alone, and output longer than what was typed is said without searching for its echo. On a full screen of identical lines, a line rewritten in place is now said alone: the search for a scroll took it, and every line below it, for lines scrolled in.
- **A terminal row's same-style cells are built as a run** (zgx-terminal): the cells after one in its colours and flags, plain ASCII, are taken as it was rather than each looked over again (its colours resolved, its flags, cursor and selection asked), so building a row of text costs about half what it did.
- **A terminal's rows scrolling and its runs coming and going lay out no other row** (zgx): children turned round by one, the one that went round absolute (a terminal's rows as it scrolls a line), leave the parent's layout as it was, since an absolute child's place is its own; any other reorder is main's. A canvas's text labels added to or removed from their group, a box of the canvas's size, lay out that group alone instead of walking to the screen.
- **A terminal's runs of text are not measured** (zgx-terminal): each run's size is what the grid gives it, its cells by the line's height, and the scene keeps that instead of shaping the run again for layout and repaint. Measuring cost about a sixth of the terminal's scene work: a log streaming in takes 3.5% fewer instructions, a line at a time 1.5% fewer. A debug build still measures each run and checks that the two sizes agree.
- **The engine-drawn terminal reads its output for escapes a word at a time** (zgx-terminal), not a byte at a time. Streaming a build log costs 1% fewer instructions, and the read for shell-integration commands 77% fewer.
- **A text that changes as it is drawn no longer trims the text cache every frame** (zgx-gpu): past its 64 KB budget of text no longer drawn, the cache now keeps half of it rather than all, so the next changed text doesn't put it straight back over. A 54-character label changing every frame (a counter, a header rolling its count) trims once in 7 frames instead of every one, and a frame that changes one text renders in about 93K instructions instead of 124K (−25%, callgrind, `micro_bench SCENE=state`). What it holds still comes back down at rest.
- **A streamed paragraph's chunk hashes only what it appended** (zgx-gpu): each new state of a growing paragraph, plain or rich (a Markdown reply's), was hashed whole to find it in the text cache, reading the reply so far once a chunk. Once a text is found to grow, the cache keeps the state its bytes left the hash in, and the next state that starts with it is hashed on from there; the same state looked up again is not hashed at all. The hash is the one the whole text makes, bit for bit. Only a text of 256 bytes or more is looked up this way, out of line; every shorter one as before. And the cache's maps take a text's hash as their own instead of hashing it again, so every lookup is a little cheaper.
- **A picture nothing holds any more gives its texture back as soon as no node shows it** (zgx, zgx-gpu): one replaced by another and dropped, as a video's frames are, can't be drawn again, so it no longer takes room among the textures kept for pictures scrolled away. A playing or paused video player held nine past frames there, 34 MB at 1280 by 720. Pictures an app or a cache still holds keep their textures as before. `ImageData::holders` tells whether anything holds an image.
- **A `ui.switch` or `ui.show` costs less to mount** (zgx): the region it shows its branch in, laid out as that branch and styled by nothing, no longer keeps a style effect and a subscription to the typography around it; what it shows inherits that typography directly. A library table scrolled a row at a time mounts about 2% fewer instructions.
- **A render no longer plays its script through once more for its sound** (zgx-video): what its own stage hears placed as it draws is what the sound is made of, muxed in once the frames are encoded, where a pass of its own played every frame of the script again first (about 3.6 G instructions and a quarter of a second for the showcase, and in chunked renders before any chunk began). The streams are byte-identical; a single-file render's MP4 differs only in its `btrt` box's average bitrate, now the stream's own rather than 0. A file joined from chunks, or muxed, no longer loses its last frames to a sound that ends a moment before them.
- **Motion blur mixes a frame's samples in a sixth of the instructions** (zgx-video): sums are 16 bits up to 256 samples, the first sample is written rather than added to zeros, and the average shifts for a power of two of samples or multiplies by an exact reciprocal for other counts, where it divided each byte. Frames are byte-identical; the showcase's 4-sample render went from 387 G to 243 G instructions, its 3-sample one from 327 G to 198 G.
- **A video renders in chunks at once** (`zgx-video`): `zgx video render --concurrency N` (or `-j N`, by default the CPUs' count up to 4) renders N chunks of frames, each on a thread with its own virtual clock, stage and encoder. The frames are those of one render, and an encoded video's chunks are joined without encoding them again. The showcase with motion blur renders about 2.6 times as fast with four chunks.
- **ffmpeg holds a video's frames no longer, 1.8 GB less for a 1080p film with sound** (zgx-video): the encoder was told to end at the shorter of the picture and the sound (`-shortest`), and to know which, ffmpeg came to hold 10 s of decoded frames in a video longer than that. The mix is now made in whole frames of the composition rounded down, so it never outlasts the picture, and the flag is gone. The showcase's file is byte-identical, and ffmpeg's peak went from 3.0 GB to 1.2 GB. A file written at another rate than its composition's no longer risks losing its last frame to sound rounded short.
- **A video player at rest holds no frames** (zgx-video): its scrub cache (up to 512 MB) filled with every frame it played, and kept them once it stopped. It now keeps only frames shown while paused or scrubbing, gives them back when it plays, and gives them back 5 s after nothing more is asked of it. A 1080p clip in an app's window went from about 1 GB of footprint playing and 0.6–0.8 GB at rest to 0.47 GB and 0.2 GB.
- **Scrubbing back in a video player's view shows kept frames at once** (zgx-video, macOS): a player's own view keeps the pixel buffers of frames shown while paused, as a script's player keeps their bytes, so a frame scrubbed to again is shown as it is rather than played again from the composition's start. Scrubbing back over 20 frames of the bench composition at 640 x 360, a second time: 25 ms a frame to 0.3 ms at frame 300, and 55 to 70 ms to 0.3 ms at frame 800. The buffers count in the same 512 MB as bytes, and at rest are given back as the bytes are (5 s), with the pool's spare buffers: the process's footprint at rest is no larger than before.
- **A video player opened just before its view is built shows the frame it was opened at** (zgx-video): a view asking for pixel buffers, a script asking for bytes, a player seen again or reloaded drew the frame it was at, replacing a frame asked for in the same moment, so a view made right after `open(…, 40)` could show frame 0.
- **A video player's view shows its frames without copying them through the CPU** (zgx-video, zgx-gpu, macOS): `Player::view` takes each frame as an IOSurface pixel buffer its thread copies on the GPU (`GpuRenderer::readback_surface`, from a pool of at most four, the free ones given back at rest; a frame finding all four held, by images an app keeps, gets a buffer of its own, so the view never stalls), where it read each back to bytes and the window uploaded it again. The pixels are the same. A 1080p clip playing in a window: app CPU 22% to 14%, instructions 2.65 G to 1.36 G a second, CPU power 1.0 to 0.54 W, app GPU time lower, footprint 0.97 GB to 0.43 GB playing and 0.63 GB to 0.12 GB at rest. A script's player (`expose`) and other platforms read bytes as before. `Player::image` is still the picture shown: its pixels are read from the buffer on screen when first asked for (`ImageData::on_demand` and `on_demand_premultiplied`, new, make an image's pixels when first read; `PixelBuffer::to_rgba` reads a buffer's).
- **A video player no one sees does no work** (zgx-video): `Player::watch(seen)` tells a player whether what it shows is seen; unseen, its thread draws nothing and doesn't wake each frame, its clock runs on, and seen again it draws the frame due then (a looping one as far into its pass as time says). `Player::view` says so itself, from whether any of its box shows and its window shows (not hidden, minimized or covered), and unseen when it goes; a script's player takes `"<name>.watch"` (from `on_visible`, say). A 1080p clip playing hidden in an app went from 1.3–1.6 G instructions a second to none. `NodeHandle::observe_shown` (zgx) says whether a node's window shows.
- **A video's frames come back already RGBA** (zgx-gpu, zgx-video): `GpuRenderer::readback_into` copies the BGRA target to an RGBA texture on the GPU (the present blit, a load and a store) before reading it, so the read is a copy of rows with nothing swapped a pixel at a time. Frames are byte-identical; readback went from 7.9 M to 5.3 M instructions a 1080p frame, the showcase's render from 31.0 G to 25.7 G.
- **A video reads each frame back in about a third of the instructions** (zgx-gpu, zgx-video): `GpuRenderer::readback_into` reads into a buffer the caller keeps, through a staging buffer it keeps too, and copies each row and swaps red and blue in one pass a pixel a word. A render no longer allocates and fills a fresh 8 MB frame and staging buffer for each 1080p frame: the showcase's readback went from 21.5 M to 7.8 M instructions a frame, and its whole render from 68.3 G to 41.3 G, with every frame's bytes the same.
- **A terminal writes a run of plain text at once** (zgx-terminal): its parser hands each run of printable ASCII over in one call, rather than one a character, and the screen writes the run's cells together (`zgx-vte` 0.15.0, a fork of vte with `Perform::print_str` and `Handler::input_str`, and `zgx-alacritty-terminal` 0.26.4). A frame of plain output costs about 9% less.
- **A display link's frames no longer re-arm the event loop's timer each frame** (zgx-desktop, macOS and Windows): the native frame source's watchdog stays where it is while ticks come, and moves on to 250 ms after the last tick only when it falls due, so the run loop's timer is set about four times a second instead of once a frame. A link that stops is still backed up 250 ms after its last tick.
- **On Wayland a frame repaints only what changed** (zgx-desktop): every frame repainted the whole window. A frame the compositor granted was always drawn whole, and damage that arrived while the window waited for it was promoted to the whole window. Now that damage is kept and painted with the granted frame's own; each present still copies the whole retained frame, so nothing relied on the swap chain's old contents. On a headless sway, a switches test changing about 8% of a 1280x800 window and a hover over two 24-point cells had repainted all of it on every frame.
- **A pane's edge dragged on Windows follows the pointer at the display's rate** (zgx-desktop): a turn of the event loop handles its pointer moves as one, the last, as on Linux. Windows folds only the moves not yet read, so with a 1000 Hz mouse a move that relaid the window out (a pane's edge dragged) found the next already waiting: one turn laid the window out for each, 11 times in one frame, and paints waited, up to 0.8 s, until the pointer stopped.
- **A window on Windows arms its wait's timer once per deadline, not once per event** (this repository's winit, so apps from crates.io, which get upstream winit, don't have it): a turn of the event loop that waited for a deadline set Windows' high-resolution timer again every turn, though it was already set for that time. Under a burst of input, such as a 1000 Hz mouse, that was a kernel call per move for nothing.
- **A long paragraph's words are read without copying**: its word ranges grew by doubling, each growth copied them; a long paragraph's now go into room the last one left. Mounting a page of text: `mount-text` −0.24% (arm64).
- **What shows of a node is worked out in one walk up** (zgx): `visible_bounds` and `shows_within`, read for every observed node and every list scrolled with the view around it, walked a deep node's ancestors once for its place and opacity, then again for its clips, and again from each clipping box for that box's own place. One walk now does it all, each clipping box summing its place on the way, the same sums in the same order, so the results are bit-identical. Damage keeps the two short walks, which cost it less.
- **A pointer move on X11 doesn't work out the keyboard's modifiers again** (zgx-desktop on Linux): every move carries them, and the keyboard state was updated from them each time; it is now updated only when they change. About 400 to 1,600 instructions fewer a move under cachegrind (1–5%). A winit-only change: apps on crates.io's winit keep the old cost.

### Fixed

- **An accessibility update between frames no longer reads a removed node** (zgx-desktop): with an assistive client attached, an update held back for pacing could fall due after a handler unmounted part of the window (a sidebar closing) and before the frame that shows it, and fail with "stale scene node" or tell the client an old tree. It now looks at the whole tree then.
- **A client reading a window's accessibility tree on macOS no longer slows it** (zgx, zgx-desktop): a launcher or window manager was treated as a screen reader, so every name and value went out at once; they now keep to the 100 ms geometry does unless VoiceOver or Switch Control runs, which `NSWorkspace` says live. `Scene::text_lines` walks a node's ancestors once rather than once per grapheme while the scene has a transform, so a slide no longer costs its text's length in walks. A label gone from the scene no longer leaves its runs' place behind in the tree: a window that had shown many rows held one for each.
- **A list scrolled by the view around it builds ahead** (`virtualize = "ancestor"`, `virtualize_in_ancestor`): given spare time, it builds the rows a viewport beyond the view before they show, and keeps them as the view scrolls away, as a list that scrolls itself does. It keeps no anchor of its own, so its view moving read as its rows settling: it never left rest, and built only the rows that showed, in the scroll's own frames.
- **A list scrolled by the view around it runs its pass only as the rows it shows change** (`virtualize = "ancestor"`): the boxes around it move on every frame of a scroll or an animation, and each move ran its whole pass again, going through every row it shows. Its pass now follows the rows shown, and while those stay the same (and nothing else it reads changes) it isn't run at all.
- **A finished animation of a state or a table lets its properties go** (zgx-script): as a cancelled one did already. The engine kept an owner for every state or table property any animation had played, for as long as the app ran, and with each one the animation's allocation. An app animating a fresh state each time (a reply's clock, a row's value) held one per use. A memory report now counts the clips playing and the state and table properties owned (`anime` lines).
- **`app_menu_bar`'s items show their shortcut and check as menus do** (zgx-desktop): an item's shortcut was written into its label after four spaces and its check as a "✓ " before it, beside the menu's own check; now the shortcut sits at the item's end in the menu's style (`shortcut`, and told to assistive technology apart from the label), the check is the menu's alone, and a separator is the theme's divider (`border.subtle`, 4 above and below) rather than a fixed grey.
- **The Services submenu stays where it is when an app's own items lead the application menu** (zgx-desktop, macOS): AppKit's services were put on the application menu's first item, which with `APPLICATION_MENU` items is the app's own (About, Check for Updates…), so that item became the Services submenu and Services stayed empty. The menu bar's Services item is now found by its title, after the app's items.
- **A line may break before an atom's icon** (zgx-gpu): an atom with a leading icon right after text, no space between ("…2010.pdf" then a chip), wraps to the next line whole, as the web's inline-flex chip does, rather than gluing to the text before it as one unbreakable word. Text next to text breaks where it did, and a mention still wraps at its own spaces with its icon on its first word.
- **A misspelled word in an atom has no mark** (zgx): a spellchecked textarea leaves its atoms' text unmarked and offers no spelling items for it, as the web leaves a `contentEditable=false` chip's label; the same word outside an atom is marked as before.
- **A backdrop blur in a rounded clip is cut along its curve**: it was cut by the clip's rectangle alone, so blurred corners showed past a rounded container's.
- **A layer shader reading what's behind it keeps what lies under its node's shadow**: a node with a box shadow and a shader calling `zgx_backdrop` composited over its whole layer, shadow included, but only what the shader could read was copied for it, so the window under the rest of the shadow came out clear.
- **Glass reads what's behind it with other glass in the frame** (zgx-gpu): with several layer shaders reading the backdrop (`zgx_backdrop`) changing at once, a frame could draw holes and wrong backdrops between and under them, on Metal and Direct3D 12. The frame grew its damage over what each reads, but found its items only within the damage before that.
- **Glass draws once a frame, after the glass reading where it lies** (zgx-gpu): when a layer shader reading the backdrop (`zgx_backdrop`) couldn't share the copy of the target an earlier one took, readers after it shared both copies and drew twice, the second time over themselves; and one could draw before a reader whose read covered it. On Metal and Direct3D 12.
- **A layer shader reading the backdrop sees all of it on macOS** (zgx-gpu): now and then 16-pixel blocks of what it read were the backdrop from before this frame's copy, showing black or flat bright colour where it had not read before. Apple GPUs' lossless compression let draws sampling the backdrop miss part of a copy into it ordered before them; the backdrop is now made without it.
- **A frame drawn from its kept frame no longer drops or doubles text**: on windows drawing in a single pass, its draws were batched after the kept frame had gone, by the rules for draws in order, so a line could go missing or draw twice, brighter.
- **A blur or mask shown first in a frame that grows the glyph atlas is drawn** (zgx-gpu): the frame is drawn again with the atlas larger, and the effect slots the failed try had planned were taken as drawn, so the node showed whatever its slot held before (another paint's pixels, often a square) until its content changed.
- **A runaway Luau script in the browser is stopped about a millisecond after its time budget, however slow each step is** (zgx-script, zgx-dom): the budget check read the clock once in 1,024 safepoints, so a loop of slow steps (a long `string.rep`) ran seconds past a 30 ms budget. Its stride is now paced to about 1 ms between looks, and a call that ends early still reads no clock. A state write off the script's clock reads the clock twice, not four times, and each stopped call's account (`last_budget_stop`) is its own.
- **A bundle's `zgx.json` is checked as its other files are** (zgx-run): the window and host options of an app opened from a bundle (`zgx run <bundle>`) were read from the file on disk unchecked, so one changed since `zgx build` could change its window. Read through the bundle's checked files now, one changed or removed since it was built is refused.
- **What a script's update costs no longer depends on when Luau's collector last ran** (zgx-script): the cache of state converted for scripts held its tables weakly, so a computed's result filed one tick was walked, copied and weighed again the next if a collection had come between, an extra pass that came and went as unrelated code moved the collector's timing. The cache now holds its tables strongly; what no script holds goes in one pass as the window goes idle, after the collection the idle trim already runs. A long list read and let go of still leaves the script heap at idle.
- **A canvas whose lines changed and then moved no longer fails with "stale scene node"** (zgx): the box holding a canvas's lines is laid out again as they come and go. It was marked and then missed, so what next moved the canvas (a sidebar closing beside a terminal printing) laid out lines already gone, and left the window half laid out.
- **A script's canvas values and calls stay within bounds** (zgx-text, zgx, zgx-gpu, zgx-script, zgx-desktop):
  - A glyph past 2048 pixels (`size = 1e6`, or a canvas zoomed far in) is drawn by no renderer, as the GPU's already drew none. Before, the CPU's asked for its mask's memory, size squared.
  - A canvas shadow blurred past where any pixel of it shows draws nothing, and a wide one averages down as far as its blur needs.
  - A canvas with thousands of layers of text joins them in about a pass.
  - Tiles cover at most 32 a side of a box's middle.
  - Pans past a billion pixels clamp.
  - `ui.render_canvas`, `ui.decode_image` and `ui.load_font` share 4 threads instead of starting one each.
  - A plugin is shown only the pictures it may open itself, not those the app's cache holds. Its `ui.decode_image` pictures count against its memory.
  - Loading the same font file again adds nothing.
  - A zip's file being built has a name of its own, and what a dropped zip was asked is skipped.
- **Canvas text stays under an overlay drawn over a screen of bordered cards** (zgx): a canvas keeps its texts in its drawing's order in at most 8 layers, and a stroke drawn after a text counted as covering everything inside its bounds, so each card's border, drawn after its own words, started a layer. Past 8, the texts left were drawn over every later shape: a scrim and a dialog over a dozen cards showed the cards' words through them (a window's canvas and `ui.render_canvas` alike). A stroke round a rectangle or an ellipse now reaches a text only along its outline, so such a border keeps its words in its layer; and past 8 layers the two neighbours whose joining lays the least text over a later shape are joined, again and again, so a large overlay keeps its split. A canvas whose text all comes after its drawing does none of this, as before.
- **`capture_keys = true` works on a node with no input handlers** (zgx-script): it did nothing without one, such as an `on_key`, so bindings and the menu bar still took the keys while focus was in it.
- **The caret after spaces that hang past a full line stays at that line's end** (zgx): a word that filled its line, then two spaces, left the caret drawn at the text's start while typing went on at its end (the second space has no glyph once it wraps, and the caret fell back to the layout's origin). It now stays after the line's last glyph, where the spaces hang, as a Mac text view keeps it.
- **A window that is not key shows no caret and wakes for no blink** (zgx): the focused editor's caret kept blinking, drawing a frame a phase, in a window that was never key (an offscreen one: 60 of 68 frames in 30 s) or sent to the back; AppKit draws none there. The caret now hides when the window stops being active, wakes for nothing while it is not, and shows solid and blinks again as it becomes key. The desktop host takes the window's focus at creation rather than assuming it is active. A test covers the hide, the silence and the return.
- **`zgx check` passes a props type made with `Merge` handed back to an element** (zgx-script): `Merge<ContainerProps, { … }>` given to `ui.column` failed as "too complex to typecheck" once an element's props grew past luau-lsp's iteration budget, so every new prop tipped some app over. `zgx check` gives the solver five times the default budget (`LuauTypeInferIterationLimit=100000`); set the same in your editor's `luau-lsp.fflags.override` (docs/scripting.md shows it).
- **CJK text is as wide as Chrome lays it out on macOS** (zgx-gpu): a character the run's face lacks draws in the face Core Text gives it as Chrome asks (system-ui's substitute, matched again at Blink's weight), so Han and CJK punctuation take `.PingFang UI SC`'s Display face and kana `.CJK Symbols Fallback SC`, at the text's optical size and with those faces' tracking. Han was 14.29 px at 14 px where Chrome has 13.42, and CJK punctuation and kana took Hiragino or Arial Unicode MS, so Han-heavy lines wrapped a glyph early. Faces the fallback lists can't name (they live in a private framework) are loaded as they're first needed; `ZGX_LOG_FACES=1` prints each character's face.
- **A clear node over a scroll view no longer stops its scrolls from copying** (zgx-gpu): a box or rectangle that paints nothing (an overlay scrollbar's track away from the pointer, over every list it scrolls) counted as drawing over the content, so each scroll repainted the whole view where it could have copied its pixels. It now counts as what it paints: nothing.
- **Copying a picture no longer keeps it decoded** (zgx-desktop): a kept file's whole pixels were held for as long as the image after a copy to the clipboard. New `ImageData::pixels_now`.
- **Colour emoji are opaque**: a colour glyph's layers (COLR, as Segoe UI Emoji's on Windows) were composited dividing by 256 where they meant 255, so a fully covered pixel came out at alpha 253: every such emoji showed a little of what was behind it, its colours a little dark. swash is now vendored as `zgx-swash` with that fixed.
- **A column shrinks a box no further than its content needs at the box's own width** (zgx): a box narrower than its column (a set width, a share, or centred) had its floor measured with its text at the column's width, a line where it wraps to several, and the column shrank it below what its text needs. Its floor is now its content's height at its own width, less its padding and border, as CSS's `min-height: auto` is.
- What a component's caller says to assistive technology reaches the node it builds: a label, role, description or `accessible_hidden()` set on a `component(…)` from outside it was dropped, so a control a row already stands for still read as an element of its own.
- **A long list a script has let go of leaves as the window goes idle** (zgx-script): each list and map shown to a script stays converted, and alive in Rust and in the script's heap, until nothing else holds it and the cache next doubles in weight. After a long list was replaced by a short one that might be never, so a long list switched away from stayed in memory. Idle now lets go of what only the cache holds, as it collects the script's garbage.
- **A magnified layer is as sharp as drawn at its size** (zgx-gpu): a node shown larger than laid out (`scale` above 1, its own or an ancestor's) painted its subtree into a layer at a √2 step at most 4× the device's and stretched it, so a 1.07× push showed text and hairlines soft and a deep zoom showed them blocky. It now rasterizes at the scale it shows at, its texels on the target's pixels; too large whole for the texture limits or the layer budget, it draws the part in view and a margin to pan into; and no finer than its largest glyph still fits the glyph atlas (2,048 pixels), so text zoomed past that draws slightly stretched rather than not at all. Moving (scaled, moved or turned since the frame before), it keeps its raster while that is fine enough and its texels where they were, so text moves as one picture instead of its glyphs landing on other pixels each frame; it draws again at the next √2 step as it grows past it, and at its scale exactly once it holds still. Shrinking, rotation and skew raster as before.
- With Direct3D 11 (`ZGX_D3D11`), a fade (`fade_edges`) fades what crosses its band as one, as with Direct3D 12: a card's paper no longer shows through its art as a light veil.
- With Direct3D 11 (`ZGX_D3D11`), `GpuRenderer::debug_present_offscreen` gives the frame as it would be presented instead of an error, so tests of scroll copies and presentation run under Direct3D 11 too.
- With Direct3D 11 (`ZGX_D3D11`), a layer shader with inputs (passes, pictures, GPU views) composites its layer as painted and is named on stderr, instead of drawing nothing (or, reading a GPU view, panicking), until Direct3D 11 runs passes.
- With Direct3D 11 (`ZGX_D3D11`), a popup's glass blurs what its window drew under it, as with Direct3D 12, instead of black.
- **Direct3D 11 runs layer shaders' passes** (zgx-gpu, `ZGX_D3D11`): a layer shader reading passes or tagged boxes (`FillShader::with_inputs`, a script's `ui.layer_shader` with passes) draws through them as on Direct3D 12, its passes drawn with Direct3D 11 ahead of the frame (feedback, steps and once-only passes alike), where its layer used to composite as painted. One reading pictures or GPU views still composites as painted, named once on stderr.
- On Direct3D 12, every renderer in a process draws with one device, held while one is alive: renderers made and dropped on many threads at once (zgx-video's chunks, its tests) each made a device, which hung NVIDIA's driver. A lost device fails every renderer on it, and each makes a new one, as before.
- **A script's layer shader with passes draws through them** (zgx-script): `ui.layer_shader(wgsl, { passes, images, geometry })` declares its shader for every renderer to start before it draws, and that start compiled it as a plain layer shader, which its inputs can't be. It failed to compile, and its layer showed as painted every frame. A declared shader with inputs now compiles with their bindings, as one first met in a frame does.
- **A hint from state given to a component reaches the node it builds** (`View::description_when` on a `component`): it was dropped when the component's own view said anything to assistive technology, so a grabber placed by a sheet lost "Double tap to expand the sheet."
- **Animations run in the browser** (zgx-dom): a DOM view now has a frame clock, so `ui.tween`, `ui.spring`, `ui.spring_to`, `anime.animate` and a Rust view's `cx.frames()` tick there, where they never moved (and `cx.frames()` panicked) before. The page's `requestAnimationFrame` drives it, asked for only while an animation waits for a frame: a page at rest asks for none, and a hidden tab's are held back by the browser.
- **A browser's animation frames never go back in time** (zgx-dom): a frame's time is at least the last one's. Before, a coarse clock, or a refresh interval shortened at once for a faster display, could put a frame's time before the previous frame's. A browser can still give two frames the same timestamp, so a frame can come with no time passed.
- **The browser gives back what a page let go of once it's still** (zgx-dom): a DOM view now has the desktop's idle trim (`zgx::idle`), run 1.5 to 3 s after the DOM was last written. A Luau app's cleared rows are collected there rather than held until its script next allocates a lot. A Luau table that showed 10,000 rows held 18.3 MB live after a clear; it now holds 3.7 MB. Nothing is armed once the trim has run, so a page at rest still runs nothing.
- **A DOM view's tasks and timers run in the browser** (zgx-dom): `cx.tasks()` no longer panics there, and a Luau app's `ui.after`, `ui.every`, `ui.persist` saves and promises (`ui.call`, `ui.fetch`, file reads) run, where they never started before. The page's event loop runs them: one `setTimeout` waits for the earliest timer, and an idle page has none armed.
- **A double click on a `window_drag` region zooms the window live** (zgx-desktop, macOS): the window's last frame was stretched over it for the whole animation, then snapped to the new layout. The zoom ran inside the event that asked for it, so winit held every size AppKit reported until the animation ended. It now runs once that event has been handled, as a zoom from the Window menu does.
- **An `on_drag` returning nil starts no drag** (zgx-script, zgx): a row can drag only some of what it shows, deciding as each press passes the drag threshold, as a recycled list row must. The press stays a press: no preview and no drag events, and let go on the node it clicks. Before, a nil payload started a drag carrying nil, and every target reading its fields raised an error. A script error in `on_drag` starts none either.
- **A row that's all button can be dragged again** (zgx): since nodes with an `id` or `ref` stopped taking the pointer, a press landed on a clickable covering its draggable, which kept the press, and `on_drag` never started. A clickable covering most of its draggable now leaves the drag to it. A small control inside one, such as a tab's close button, keeps its press.
- **Lines leaving their scroll group while they show no longer panic** (zgx): deleting a code block's fence, or the block, in a text area whose lines were in a scroll group (`LineInset::scroll_group`) removed the group's layer with line nodes the text area still held, and its next draw panicked ("stale scene node"). Their nodes go with the layer now, an area whose insets are taken away shows its lines outside their old group's layer, and its group scrollbar goes.
- **A scroll group isn't left scrolled past its lines** (zgx): a code block scrolled sideways, then cut short by an edit made elsewhere (`EditorValue::set`, undo), showed nothing, its lines left of its frame. It comes back to as far as its lines reach, and `EditorHandle::on_group_scroll` is told once the draw is done.
- **A one-line field follows `control.height` and the system text size as they change** (zgx): its box kept the height it was built at, so a larger text size clipped its line. `control.height` now also grows, under a system text size above 1, to hold a body line and its padding, as iOS's controls grow with Dynamic Type.
- **Lines' room follows a highlighter state an edit above them changed** (zgx): a fence typed above a text area's lines, whose `line_insets` depend on the state before them, puts them in its scroll group and room at once; lines out of view kept their old height, and the group none, until the insets were set again. An edit measures again the lines its state reaches, and no more; a text area without line insets does nothing new.
- **A layer drawn again for the budget no longer panics over its blurred layers** (zgx-gpu): a layer past the layer budget draws again, coarser, in the room left. The effect slots (blurs, masks, drop shadows) its first try planned for the layers inside it stayed queued, and the first of those layers drawn again (one waiting on a picture or a shader) took them while its own texture was out of the cache: `no entry found for key` in `encode_effects`, ending the frame or a video's render thread. The first try's slots are now given up and planned again with the second.
- **A trackpad push's speed is on the interface's clock** (zgx): what a stretch let go of while pushing goes on at was measured on the wall clock while its spring ran on `zgx::clock`, so under a `VirtualClock` (a video made frame by frame, a test) the push read as near-instant and the stretch ran on too far. Its test pushes on a virtual clock and checks the stretch 16 ms after lifting against the measured curve, rather than sleeping, which a loaded CI runner overran.
- **A list's estimate no longer keeps every folded row open** (zgx-script): `ui.list { estimate = … }` read each row's fields to estimate it, and a folded row (`Value::folded`) read that way stayed opened for good, so a long transcript held all its rows unfolded. Each folded row now opens for its scan and closes again, and its scan is kept, so a settled row is never opened again for an added row or a new width. 15,000 folded rows under an estimate hold 24.5 MB rather than 37.3 at rest; an append costs 44M instructions rather than 115M, a new width 7M rather than 76M.
- **`zgx_sample` reads a layer shader's own pixels exactly, however far away** (zgx-gpu): the texture coordinates it read at came from the screen derivatives of the composite quad's, a little off, and that error times a read hundreds of points away blended in the texel beside the one meant, or landed in it: a shader reading data from its own pixels (a map, block ids) showed wrong cells, tearing and flicker, worse under zoom. The coordinates now come with the shader's uniforms, worked out from the quad.
- **A layer magnified 2 or 4 times reads its own texels exactly**: its raster scale was √2 squared, a hair under 2, so a layer shader's read of a texel landed just off its centre, and some GPUs (an RTX 4090 and WARP on Direct3D 12) sampled a little of the next one: 253 where 255 was drawn. Whole steps of two are now exact.
- **A fade's band repainted alone draws as a whole repaint draws it** (zgx-gpu): each band of a fade (`fade_edges`) is drawn as one, through a texture, only where what it holds stacks, and that is now decided band by band. It was decided for all of a fade's bands together, from those the frame repainted: a scroll view whose bottom band stacked (a code block's text on its box) drew its top band as one in a frame repainting both, and item by item in a frame repainting the top band alone, so a frame after a scroll could change a few pixels of the top band (a glyph's overlap with the next one, a box's shadow under it) that a whole repaint draws otherwise.
- **A `ui.list` whose `key` fails keeps its rows** (zgx-script): the rows it knew keep the keys they had, with their nodes and states, and the error is still reported. Every row used to take its index as its key, so a slip remade the whole list.
- **A character a font lacks falls back alone, and to the system sans on macOS** (zgx-text): in text asked for in a family that isn't installed, a word holding a character such as `…` was drawn whole in the fallback face for that character (Times), apart from the words beside it; it now takes the same face as they do, and only characters that face lacks come from another. On macOS a character missing from a Latin-script font comes from the face Core Text substitutes from the system font (SF, at the text's weight), as AppKit draws it, not Chrome's standard font, Times: Geist without `…` drew it in a serif. Japanese, Korean and Chinese text keeps its standard fonts.
- **A render ffmpeg stops part way says why** (zgx-video): it said "ffmpeg stopped taking frames: Broken pipe", and now gives what ffmpeg said as it quit (a file it can't open, an encoder that refuses the size).
- **An AV1 render smaller than 64 pixels a side writes** (zgx-video): SVT-AV1 before 2.0, as Ubuntu 24.04 has, refuses such frames, so they go to libaom or rav1e first; rav1e is also used when it's the only AV1 encoder.
- **Mounting new text in a family with no face of its own is about a third cheaper** (zgx-cosmic-text): a plain `SansSerif` on macOS, or a font name that isn't installed, walked every installed face for each new word to find the same fallback. It's kept now, as a family's own face is.
- **`w = "fit-content"` on a box whose text wraps takes its room, as CSS's does** (zgx): its max-content was measured with its own `max_w` still on, so its text wrapped first and the box came out its widest wrapped line, the same as with no width. A chat bubble capped at 70% now reaches the cap (401.8 in a 574 column, where it was 393.6), as in a browser. A box with no width still shrinks to its widest line.
- **A flex item that names its minimum width is no longer laid out at zero width** (zgx): taffy measured every item at min-content for an automatic minimum and then used the one it named, so a column given `grow`, a basis of 0 and `min_w = 0` (a chat row's text beside an avatar) laid its paragraphs out a line a word, in Chinese or Japanese a line a character, on every layout. On an iOS simulator one such paragraph made an 83 ms frame scrolling a feed; only an automatic minimum measures min-content now, as later taffy releases do.
- **A cell the pointer crosses between two moves of one turn hears its enter and leave** (zgx-desktop, Linux and Windows): the moves the event loop heard in one turn are handled as one, their last, and a cell crossed between two of them was never entered, so its hover never showed (a hover benchmark missed one cell in 13 runs). The fold stays, for layout and paint; hover now follows the pointer through each folded move (a hit test each, `Ui::try_pointer_passed`), and a turn with one move costs nothing more. macOS hands an app one move a turn itself.
- **Watching scripts for hot reload holds no file open per script on macOS** (zgx-script): one FSEvents stream watches the folders that hold them, where each watched file took a descriptor of its own. An app the Finder starts may open only 256 files, and a few hundred watched scripts used them all, after which pictures, lazily loaded screens and themes failed to open. A file beside a watched one (an editor's swap file, the Finder's `.DS_Store`) or one written in place inside a plugins folder no longer wakes a reload.
- **zgx-fx's Luau module type-checks in an app's scripts** (`luau/fx.luau`): its effects took their options as required, so `fx.glow { preset = "neon" }` and a bare `fx.ripple()` were type errors under luau-lsp, and `fx.with`'s result didn't fit the element it went to. Its options are now typed optional and its effects and `fx.with` typed for what they are. `ui.glass { … }` also takes a plain `h = 84` again: its props merge over a container's, as a scroll's do, where an intersection rejected plain values. The gallery's one-shots fire where they're pressed (it read a click's position, which a click doesn't carry), and CI type-checks the gallery with the other examples.
- **A GPU view has a texture budget of its own** (`zgx-gpu`): a 3D scene's texture shared 64 MB with pictures, and past it the frame stopped with "visible image and GPU view textures exceed their budget", though pictures may rightly hold that much (a video's large wallpaper, or a clip's past frames). Views now answer to their own budget, which a view truly past still fails, saying what holds it. Pictures not showing still give their textures back first; nothing on screen goes softer or blank for a view.
- **A GPU view's frames are on the interface's time, one instant a frame** (zgx-gpu): `ViewportFrame.time` was the wall clock's at each view's draw, so a view shown in two boxes was drawn at two instants a frame (whatever steps once a frame time stepped twice), and a view in a video made on a virtual clock (zgx-video) drew each frame at whatever time the machine took to make it. It's now `zgx::clock::now()` (the virtual clock's while one runs), read by the first view drawn in a window's frame and given to every view and box of that frame.
- **`justify_stretch` stretches a grid's `auto` tracks** (Luau `justify = "stretch"`): they share the grid's free width, as CSS's `justify-content: stretch`. A grid without it, a flex row or column and an overlay lay out as before.
- **Text laid out without a renderer breaks where the app's does** (zgx): the fallback layout (`FallbackTextLayout`, what `zgx test` and a scene with no shaper use) wrapped by grapheme, splitting any word that didn't fit at a line's end, a link span after a sentence too. It now wraps by word as the shaper does (UAX #14 over the whole text, so a break where one span ends and the next begins is a break like any other; spaces hang past the edge; a word wider than the line breaks by grapheme), and centred or right-aligned plain text is aligned row by row. A test lays a corpus out both ways and asserts the same line breaks.
- **A view scrolled while a Wayland frame waits is painted whole with the next** (zgx-desktop): a scroll's flush damages only what copying the view's old pixels can't give (a fading view's bands, the strip scrolled in), for a copy over the frame drawn from the flush before. A frame-paced window held that flush's damage for the frame the compositor granted next, but not its move, so a view whose next flush moved nothing (its rail, a pill, a hover changing beside it) or whose moves fall between device pixels (1.25x, 1.875x) kept its old pixels: every row of a transcript off by as far as it had scrolled, torn against its freshly drawn bands, until something repainted it. The held frame now holds each view it scrolled.
- **A window that isn't presenting frees what it removed** (zgx, zgx-desktop): a large removal's nodes were freed over later frames, so in a hidden, minimized or covered window they stayed, and the idle trim never ran, until it next drew. The host now frees them with no frame, then trims. `zgx::idle::request` asks for the idle trim from anywhere, with no frame.
- **A host name registered twice says so** (zgx-script): in a debug build, an `action`, `function` or `async_action` registered again from another place in the host's code warns once, naming both places, on stderr, in `ScriptHost::warnings` and in the lint strip. The later handler answers, as before. One registered again from the same place (a host rebuilt) stays silent. A release build records nothing.
- **A new picture past the texture budget no longer blanks one on screen** (zgx-gpu): a picture a frame painted from what it kept wasn't marked as drawn, so one on screen could give its texture to a new one and stay blank, the frame reporting success. Those the scene still shows are spared now, worked out only when a texture has to give way: the new one goes softer, as designed, and pictures off the window still give way.
- **Pictures drawn together share the image budget evenly** (zgx-gpu), whichever is drawn first: small ones keep their size, large ones share what's left, and take their room back as others go.
- **At 16,384 images, those not drawn this frame give way** (zgx-gpu), rather than a new one being left out.
- **A layer scaled far down rasterizes at what shows** (zgx-gpu), down to a sixteenth rather than a half, so a picture laid out at full size under a scaling parent takes about a megabyte, not 13.
- **Pictures past the image budget always show, and raise no issue** (zgx-gpu): at the budget's end a picture is cut to at most 64 pixels on its long side rather than left out, and one drawn softer for the budget uploads once, not on every frame. Kept files decode no larger than their textures will be.
- **An image used as a mask takes the size it masks at** (zgx-gpu), not its file's. A kept file still decoding no longer fails the frame or shows the masked content unmasked.
- **Layers past their budget draw coarser with no issue raised either** (zgx-gpu): `zgx inspect memory` shows those layers and how many images are held softer, worked out when it's read.
- **Images and SVGs land on whole device pixels as the text beside them does** (zgx-gpu): an untransformed image's or SVG's origin is rounded to the device pixel as a text node's is, as Chrome paints them (`ToPixelSnappedRect`). An icon whose top fell on half a device pixel (a line a quarter point off at 2x) drew across two rows, half strength, a row above Chrome's; it now draws whole, and stays the same distance from its text as a scroll moves both by fractions of a pixel.
- **A control inside a disabled container is disabled too**, as one in a disabled fieldset is: it takes no clicks, keys or focus, shows its disabled look (a button dims), and assistive technology hears it disabled, on the desktop and in the browser. Enabling the container gives each back its own state, without building it again.
- **A citation inline in a paragraph is read once, where it stands** (zgx, zgx-desktop, zgx-dom): a rich-text run that acts and is named apart from its text (a citation's "1", named "Citation 1") was read twice, its "1" in the paragraph's text and a button after it. As a browser reads an inline link, it is now the button alone, between the text around it: the paragraph reads "step ." with the button between "step " and ".". It stays drawn and selectable, and a copy keeps its text.
- **A devtools server stops listening and takes its files when it is dropped** (zgx-script): a host closing a window dropped its `inspect::Server`, which removed `<pid>.sock` while its thread went on accepting on the unlinked socket and the app's `<pid>.json` stayed, so `zgx inspect list` named an app no one could reach. Dropped, a server now stops its listener and removes both files, each only while it is still its own (a server made since at the same path, as when a window is made again, keeps its socket and file).
- **Devtools serve from an iOS simulator app** (zgx-script): its temporary directory is about 150 bytes, past the 103 a Unix socket's path may be on Apple's systems (107 on Linux), so the socket never bound and `zgx inspect` found no app. Where the temporary directory is too long, an app now serves from `/tmp/zgx-inspect-<uid>` (its user's alone, as before), and `zgx inspect` looks there too; `ZGX_INSPECT_DIR` names another directory for both; a path still too long is an error naming the limit and the override.
- **The cursor follows a clip, a reorder or a paint order changing under a still pointer** (zgx): `Scene::interaction_revision`, on which the host keeps the cursor it worked out, didn't move with them, so the cursor could stay as it was over what had changed.
- **Simplified Chinese after Japanese draws in a Chinese face** (zgx-gpu): in a line with a Japanese sentence before a Chinese one, the simplified characters Japanese faces lack (车, 见) drew in Arial Unicode MS on macOS, the first of every installed face the fallback search tried. A Japanese or Korean run now tries the Chinese faces next, as Core Text does. That last search, every face loaded in turn (some 400 on iOS, 14 ms on a paragraph's first layout), now runs once a character: what it found is kept until the fonts change.
- **A control in a layer just built takes focus with a dialog open** (zgx): with a modal open, a button in a `ui.layer` built that frame (a `ui.presence`'s new content) focused from its `on_mount` was refused (`el:focus()` false), as the layer counted as one only once it had finished opening. It's live under the modal from its first mount, as it was once open.
- **A window on Linux draws on the GPU its display server composites with** (zgx-gpu): the device a Wayland compositor names in its linux-dmabuf feedback, or the one an X server opens over DRI3 (Xwayland, its compositor's), taken as the Vulkan adapter at its PCI address. wgpu's low-power preference took an integrated GPU wherever there was one, so on a desktop whose displays are on a card beside it each frame was drawn on the integrated GPU and handed across: Hyprland on an RTX 3080 showed a window drawn on a Ryzen's integrated Radeon (RADV) black, and under Xwayland its surface failed to configure (a panic). Where the server names no device, or no adapter is at its address, the low-power one is still taken, as on a laptop whose integrated GPU drives its displays.
- **A row dragged out of a list is the same node when it's dropped** (zgx): a `ui.list` with heights doesn't build rows folded to nothing, so a row whose place folded away while it was held (as a sidebar's closes up behind it) was taken down, and built again on the drop: its focus, hover and accessible element lost. The row a drag started from now stays built, thin or not, through the drag and its drop; other rows folded away still aren't built.
- **A list's keys leave long rows off the screen out of the script** (zgx-script): a `ui.list` of `data` with a `key` copied every row into the script to ask their keys, before showing any. A row of 2 KB or more now gives `key` its numbers, booleans and short strings, so 2,000 rows holding 40 MB of text grow the script's heap by under 3 MB, not 41 MB. A key that reads anything else is given the whole item, as before.
- A list at rest no longer asks for spare time to decode its rows' pictures when room for decodes ahead comes back: a resting window does no work.
- **A long list reaches a script without spending its budget** (zgx-script): making a state's long list into a table (`:get()`, `:peek()`, a `ui.list`'s keys) is the host's work, and no longer counts against the 50 ms a call has. A chat's long transcript took more than that, so the next line of whatever read it stopped with `script exceeded its time budget`, a computed kept its old value and a list lost its keys.
- **A looping layer can't show where its node doesn't** (zgx-gpu, macOS): its place is set at every flush, frame or none, read back rather than trusted, and told again when it disagrees, which the log reports. A frame that fails hides every looping layer until one draws.
- **A baked loop's layer goes where its box went, whatever else the frame draws** (macOS): a frame drawn a second time for a layer whose content grew past its corner lost the move it had just made for a spinner, which stayed behind until its next move, usually a scroll.
- **Documents opened from Finder or Open With reach the app on macOS** (zgx-desktop): an open-documents Apple Event's files were read with `stringValue`, which gives an HFS path ("Macintosh HD:Users:…") rather than a URL, so validation dropped every one. They are read with `fileURLValue` and arrive as `file://` URLs.
- **A streamed Markdown reply converts only its new blocks whenever the collector runs** (zgx-script): the list each answer derived its table from was kept only weakly, so a collection between two chunks left the next to convert the whole reply again; it is held as a list converted whole is.
