- **Markdown shows the same streamed as parsed at once** (zgx-script): a picture after a word in a list item stays among its words where a paragraph with a line break came before it; the indent of an HTML block no longer joins the next block's words, or makes a paragraph of its own in place of a list; and a link definition in a quote or list, or with its label across two lines, links what came before it as the reply streams. A property test streams random Markdown in random pieces, and compares with the text at once at every step.
- **A menu button with a tooltip opens as it is pressed** (zgx): a bare button opening a menu is a menu button, a press, Enter, Space or Down opening it; with a tooltip on it (`.tooltip`, Luau's `tooltip = …`) the menu saw the tip's wrapper, not a button, and nothing opened it. A tooltip's wrapper around a bare button now counts as one.
- **Menus' separators can be seen in the dark theme** (zgx, zgx-desktop): the editor's context menu and `app_menu_bar` drew theirs in `border.subtle`, 1.03:1 against `zgx-dark`'s menu panel, invisible; they take `border.strong` now (1.86:1 dark, 2.06:1 light).
- **`fx.metaballs` draws each blob whole at its centre** (zgx-fx): its antialiased edge came from the field's slope, which climbs without bound where a blob is centred, so a few pixels around each centre took in the background as dark specks (and each GPU drew them a little differently). The edge now comes from the field held under 1, the same at the edge and flat at the centre.
- **Drags and throws keep time on the interface's clock** (zgx, zgx-script): a drop target's spring-loading delay, a selection drag's autoscroll, an anime draggable's throw and a 3D view's flick read the wall clock, so on a clock of the interface's own (a test's, a video made offline) a slow or loaded machine changed when a target sprang open, how far a selection scrolled, how far a draggable was flung, and whether a flick coasted. They now read `zgx::clock::now()`, the wall clock in a window, as before. Chord timeouts, find's counting and type-to-select keep the wall clock: they measure a person's real pauses.
- **A window at rest lets go of a native surface it no longer shows** (zgx-gpu, macOS): CoreVideo's texture cache kept the last one (a video player's last picture, after the player was gone) for the window's life, since nothing flushed it once frames stopped. Going idle flushes it now, once: no timer, and no wakeups at rest. A 1280 x 720 picture: 3.3 MB less at rest.
- **Two text changes in one frame lay out as they should** (zgx): where one changed text sat inside a box laid out alone for another (a header and a nested label under a fixed-width panel), that box could stand on what the inner text answered before it changed, and the inner text kept its old box: its old width, or too few or too many lines. Since 0.1.9.
- **An offscreen renderer compiles its shaders on its own thread** (zgx-gpu): it waited for the compile thread anyway (no window to wake), and meanwhile both threads allocated, so its frames' work moved run to run with how their allocations interleaved: the gate's shader cells (`fx-layer`, `fx-reader`, `fx-passes`) counted up to 0.46% apart on one binary. A window's frames still go on while its shaders compile off the frame.
- **Text drawn by a renderer is measured by its text, even where the host installed none** (zgx, zgx-gpu): a scene mounted and drawn offscreen without `GpuRenderer::install_text` was laid out with zgx's estimate while the renderer drew real glyphs, so a word sized by its own width (a checkbox's or radio's label, a text beside a grown slider) was laid out narrower than it draws and lost its last glyph ("Lamps" drew "Lamp"). A renderer now makes its context's text services its thread's default (`Scene::set_default_text_services`), and a scene with none installed takes them at its next layout, right from its first frame. A host's own installing still wins.
- **A text area's chips are one drawing, under the selection** (zgx): a span a highlighter styles as a chip (`SpanStyle::chip`) is drawn as an atom's wash is, a mark behind the text, so selecting it shows the selection over it (its background used to hide it). Its padding across still takes room in the line, added to an atom's leading and trailing room where both apply. Each chip and atom has its own corners (they used to share one radius per text area, which a search's matches took too); search matches keep the theme's. Existing atoms draw exactly as before. A chip span's height now follows an atom's (`size × 1.16` plus its padding down, centred in its row), which can differ from the font's content area by about a point.
- **A page shown by two nodes at once is the one that shows** (zgx-desktop): one web view (or terminal) mounted at two live nodes, as a pane mounted again while its old node lingers, or the same page in a hidden pane, went to whichever the host placed last. If that was the hidden one, clicks where the page showed reached nothing, while the app's own controls still reacted. A page now shows, and takes input, at its node that is laid out visible (else the one registered last), and nowhere else.
- **A caret in a virtual row no longer selects the whole row while the row comes back** (zgx): an end kept for a row scrolled away is found again by the row's path to its text; a row mounted again before its text was where it had been (a reply still building its blocks) left both ends parked in a mounted row, every leaf of which compared equal to them, so a click's caret lit and copied the whole row, and a selection grew by it. A parked end's own row now shows none of the selection until its text is back (the end comes back with it), and a copy takes that row's part as the selection cut it.
- **Pictures on screen stay showing when a new one past the image budget makes their share smaller** (zgx-gpu): a kept file whose decoded pixels had gone waited on a decode with nothing drawn, and could go on blank for as long as decoding took. Each is drawn as it was until its replacement is ready, and what it held goes then.
- **A picture scrolled back into a list draws at once** (zgx-gpu): its row's removal took its texture, and its decoded pixels were long given up, so a large picture showed an empty box until it decoded again, every time it returned. Its texture now stays, up to 32 MiB of them, until the window goes idle.
- **A box's shadow reaching a native view no longer lifts the box over its own page** (zgx, zgx-desktop): a card with an outer shadow (a drop shadow reaches three blurs out) whose shadow touched another page was drawn on the overlay plane, fill included, over the page it held (a pane's web view invisible in a light theme, where the shadow shows, and fine in a dark one). The plane is now chosen by where the box and its outline paint; a shadow reaching a page goes under it.
- **A video player whose GPU device is lost draws again on a new one** (zgx-video): after a driver reset or a GPU removed, it stopped with "GPU device lost" until the app made it again; now it builds its renderer anew and goes on from the frame it was at.
- **A video player played to its end tells its last frame before it stops** (zgx-video): behind time, it said it stopped before drawing the last frame, so what listened (the studio, an app's player) could stop on a frame short of the end. It now draws the last frame first, and `Player::listen` delivers a frame before any event that follows it.
- **A plugin's page is cut to its box** (zgx): `zgx::compose::confined` clipped at a box a hundred times its parent's width, so what a `ui.plugin_page` drew or opened past its right edge showed and took the pointer inside the `ui.sealed` region around it. It's the parent's size now. `View::top_layer`'s holder had the same mistake, without showing: its panel was held to the window.
- **A rounded popup's glass stays inside its curve on Windows** (zgx-gpu): past the corners of a popup with `backdrop_blur`, the popup drew a wedge of its window's frame, opaque, over its own shadow, because the copy of what lies under the blur came from the window and not the popup. Frames drawn at presentation, as Windows draws them, took that path; those mixed by blending never did.
- **A popup's glass blurs on Linux, and past its window's edge everywhere it's drawn this way** (zgx-gpu, zgx-desktop): on X11 and Wayland a menu, popover or tooltip with `backdrop_blur` blurred nothing, as on Windows before. Both now blur what the window last drew under them, and on all three a popup reaching past its window carries the window's edge on, so a dropdown's glass is opaque throughout instead of fading to sharp near the edge.
- **A popup with no compositor is cut to the panel it shows, however deep or inset** (zgx-desktop, X11): its window was shaped to rounded corners only when the popup's root, or a child its very size within four levels, was the rounded panel, so a menu whose panel is inset in padding or nested under wrappers (Zeron's) showed black corners and a black inset under a tiling window manager with no compositor. The panel is now the largest box in the popup that paints a fill, at any depth, and the window is cut to it, inset and corners.
- **A masked view draws in a popup's own window** (zgx-gpu): a node with a mask (`mask`, `mask_gradient`, `mask_radial`) inside a popup shown in a window of its own, or on the overlay plane above a native view, drew nothing at all. Those renderers draw their items from the plane's corner, and the mask was placed from the window's, so it lay off its node by the popup's position and hid all of it. It is placed from the same corner now.
- **Hovering a window at the back no longer draws over the app in front** (zgx, zgx-desktop): a tooltip, hover card or menu wanted while none of the app's windows is active stays wanted, and shows as the app comes forward, on every platform; the app's state isn't changed. On macOS showing a popup no longer raises its window, popovers stack at their window's level and tooltips at AppKit's.
- **A script's `print` reaches stderr a whole line at a time** (zgx-script): the line and its newline went out in two writes, and another thread's output in between (libwayland's `WAYLAND_DEBUG` trace) landed inside it, so a tool reading `LOCK true` read `LOCK true[3664360.153] wl_pointer@45.frame()`. So do `ZGX_PRESENT_LOG`'s and `ZGX_GPU_STATS`' lines.
- **A gradient shown again takes the texture it had** (zgx-gpu): a box with a gradient along one axis that went and came back (a navigation stack's page edge on every push, a scroll edge's fade) made its ramp texture and sampler again each time, keeping the old ones until 256 piled up, so a long session's memory crept. A ramp no box shows now stays while its texture does, freed with other unshown images when the window goes idle.
- **A raster window marks its first frame as presented** (zgx-desktop): a window drawn on the CPU (`ZGX_RENDERER=raster`, or no GPU) shows each frame as it draws it, but only the GPU renderer's present marked the process's first. On macOS the menu bar, which waits for that first frame, then waited out its one-second limit in a raster window; it now comes with the first frame, as in a GPU window. `ZGX_SHADER_LOG=1`'s `zgx: first frame presented` line and `ZGX_PROFILE`'s mark come from either.
- **The software raster draws rich text where and as the GPU renderer does** (zgx-desktop, zgx-gpu): it laid rich text out in a font system of its own, its glyphs kept their fractions of a pixel, and none was thickened for its run's colour as macOS's font smoothing thickens it, so a window falling back to the raster drew rich text unlike the GPU (4,900 pixels off, by up to 207 of 255, in the parity test). It now shares the renderer's fonts, layout and placement, as plain text did, each run keyed for its colour.
- **A reactive `outline_color` keeps the node's outline width** (zgx): a function-valued `outline_color` is a style patch of its own, which had no width and so forced one of 1 point over the node's own; `outline = 0` with a function colour drew a 1 px ring. The patch now leaves the width to the node's own (0 included), and a node with no width set still draws 1 point.
- **A `ui.list` that is itself a selectable region names its rows by its own id** (zgx-script): `on_copy`'s `request.rows` gave `list = false` for `ui.list { id = …, selectable = true, … }`; the list's own id counts now, as it does for a list inside a region. `zgx inspect node` on a paragraph with a citation (or any labelled control) in it also says what a screen reader hears, `reads: "step " [button "Citation 1"] "."`, from the same code the desktop's accessibility tree is built with.
- **A reload keeps focus where it was even when its load moves it first** (zgx, zgx-script): where focus is is now taken before the scripts run, not after. A load that sets state the shown interface reads (a `ui.presence` keyed by it, its focused button ending) moved focus to the next stop before the old tree was replaced, and the reload kept it there. `switch_keeping_focus_held` and `FocusHold` give a host the same: take where focus is before running what may move it, and the switch finds it again in the branch it builds.
- **An image whose remote `src` keeps changing shows each version without a blank frame, and holds memory to the cache's budget** (zgx-script): a picture fetched again and again under a new URL (a tile a program redraws, `…/tile/5.png?v=12`) showed nothing while each new URL loaded, kept its file to decode as it was drawn (a frame later, and again at each new size while zooming, so a zoomed canvas of tiles went blank), and piled up in the cache until the window went idle (about 1 MB a version: +150 MB over 300 updates). Now the image shows its last picture until the next arrives, a remote picture up to 1024 by 1024 pixels arrives decoded off the main thread, and past twice its budget the cache drops the pictures no node shows as the next one arrives. A 512 px tile rewritten 10 times a second: no blank frames (from 10% of frames), 48 re-uploads in a 6 s zoom (from 527 to 691), +41 to 68 MB (from +150 MB).
- **A rendered canvas draws its text in the window's layers** (zgx-gpu): `ui.render_canvas` drew every text over all of a canvas's shapes, uncut by its clips and upright, while a window draws each text in its layer, under shapes drawn after it, cut by its clip's box and turned by its transform. A picture now paints each layer's shapes and then its text, cuts a text by its clip's box and draws a turned text mapped by its transform, so it matches the window: a mean channel difference of 0.007 at 1x and 0.002 at 2x on Direct3D 12, none over 32 (4.7 before).
- **The rubber band moves as macOS's does** (zgx): a stretch is a twentieth of the push in a straight line, pulling back moves it as far as the hand moves, and letting go and bouncing follow AppKit's measured curve.
- **A scroll that jumps too far to copy no longer leaves behind what went in the same frame** (zgx): a move that can't copy its pixels repaints all its view shows. Before, it repainted what its content held, at the old offset and the new, plus earlier damage shifted back by the move. A box removed after the content moved was damaged at the new offset, out of the window, and so was dropped, while the walk at the old offset no longer held it. Zeron left a turn's text on screen this way when its transcript jumped as the turn's rows were rebuilt.
- **Scrolling goes to the view under the pointer again, not the one last scrolled** (zgx): a scroll gesture's latch held for as long as scrolls kept coming within 300 ms of each other, a trackpad's coast included, so a new swipe over another view (or a click on it, then a swipe) still moved the last one. As AppKit's, a latch now holds for its gesture and that gesture's momentum only: a trackpad's next gesture, or its momentum's end, lets go, and a press always lets go.
- **A mouse wheel's notch goes to the view under the pointer once the pointer has moved** (zgx): a notch has no gesture to end, so one soon after the last still went to the view that one latched to, even with the pointer over another by then. Held still, a wheel stays latched through an edge as before.
- **A heavy scroll keeps the display's full rate on macOS** (zgx-desktop, zgx-gpu): while a scroll is live, a frame ready after its refresh's point is handed over at once, and no frame is held a refresh more to keep the last one's cadence. Frames needing about 5 ms or more at 120 Hz used to drop to every other refresh for the rest of the gesture. With `ZGX_SCROLL_DRAWABLES=1`, a live scroll's frames also draw into drawables that Metal presents once the GPU is done, so the main thread doesn't wait for each frame's GPU work (off by default: their latency is still being measured).
- **Scrolling keeps its full distance after a stall** (zgx-desktop): a new event after a 50 ms gap no longer discards movement that the previous frame had not delivered, including when a gesture ends before another frame.
- **Layout settles when an observed position only rounds differently, or a size is unbounded** (zgx): observed bounds (`ViewHandle::observe_bounds`, what anchors a popover or a tooltip) are told within a 1024th of a point, as sizes are since #1027, so a position flipping between neighbouring floats under a reader that moved it no longer ran out the frame's layout passes. An infinite size was told again on every pass (infinity less itself is NaN, near nothing) and never settled; it is the same size now.
- **Shaders kept on disk are never read back cut short** (zgx-gpu, Windows): threads compiling the same shader at once (concurrent tests) wrote one temporary file per process, so one could rename it into place while another was still writing it, and a reader then passed part of the bytecode to Direct3D 12, where WARP crashed reading past its end. Each writer now writes a file of its own, and a cached shader is used only as a whole DXBC container, at the size its header gives. This applies to both the pipeline cache (wgpu-hal's) and the app-shader cache (`fxc_child`).
- **A clocked shader's theme-token parameters draw the token's colour** (zgx, zgx-script): with `shader_clock = true`, a row of `shader_params` given as a theme token (`"$accent"`) drew as zeros, fully transparent, because the engine's drive wrote the literal row over it. The drive now resolves tokens where the node is (`ShaderDrive::tokens`) and follows the theme as it changes, without putting the clock back. `layer_params` takes theme tokens too, under `layer_clock` or without it.
- **Text services let go of the table their shaped words filled** (zgx-gpu): `TextServices::release_unused` emptied cosmic-text's word cache but kept its table at the size it peaked at, 13 MB after shaping a long transcript, so a headless host never got it back. A memory report's "shaped runs" now counts that table with the words.
- **Shift with the mouse wheel scrolls across** (zgx-desktop, Windows and Linux): a wide table in a horizontal scroll view could not be scrolled with the wheel. The system reports Shift and the wheel as a vertical scroll; it is now a horizontal one, as in browsers. macOS already did.
- **Shortcuts on punctuation and digits can be typed on any layout** (zgx): where a layout needs Shift to produce a shortcut's character (`/` is Shift+7 on German and Shift+: on French, whose digits need Shift too), the key arrived with Shift held and no binding matched it, so ⌘/ or Ctrl+1 couldn't be typed. A character with no letter in it that Shift only produced now matches as though Shift were up, as browsers and VS Code match it, once no binding takes the keystroke exactly: a binding that names Shift (`ctrl+shift+/`) still wins, and a letter's Shift is still its case.
- **A menu shortcut acts before the key typed right after it** (zgx-desktop): a key typed a moment after a shortcut that moves focus (⌘N opening a new document, say) reaches what the shortcut focused, not what had focus before it. A shortcut the window's keymap takes is carried out as soon as its key is handled, on every platform, and a macOS menu item chosen by its key is carried out before the next window event.
- **A box its column shrinks follows its content when laid out alone** (zgx): a box of a set height that a column too short for it held at its content's height kept that height after its content shrank, where its column shrinks it to the new one. And a box of a set size found, in the frame a box around it was laid out alone, to need a layout from the top was left as it was: what the outer box answered first, from the inner one's old answer, served that layout.
- **`gap_x`, `gap_y`, `min_w`, `max_w`, `min_h` and `max_h` take a theme token** (`"$space.1"`) as `w`, `h` and `gap` do; they ignored one before. And `gap_x` or `gap_y` refine `gap` whichever form each is given in (`gap = "$space.1", gap_y = 0` spaces lines 0 apart, as CSS's `row-gap` over `gap`). A layout that gave one of these a token now gets its size: a row with `min_h = "$space.12"`, say, is that tall where it was its content's height.
- **Text in a `snap` box doesn't wrap and unwrap as the box narrows** (zgx): a box's edges round to whole points separately, so its width came and went by a point as it only narrowed (429, 428, 429, 427), and what it holds was laid out at that: text at its wrap point went down a line and back, several times in a drag. While its size is changing, what it holds is laid out at that size rounded, which goes one way. Its edges, and the gaps between boxes that share them, are where they were, and once it has held still for 300 ms it's laid out as before.
- **A row built in a frame's spare time knows whether it shows by the frame that shows it** (zgx): observers of visibility registered since the frame last told them are told ahead of it, as spare time builds and lays out their rows, so content a row builds once it is near the view (`on_visible`) is there when it first shows, not a frame later. Every other observer still hears once a frame, from the frame's preparation, as before.
- **A spellchecked editor lets go of what it holds as it unmounts** (zgx): the editor kept the closure that rechecks its spelling, and that closure held the editor, so a spellchecked field was never freed, and with it everything its handlers captured (a chat composer's state). The editor now holds the recheck weakly.
- A property bound to `ui.spring` moves by the time that passed between its frames, not by the display's reported refresh interval: where a display reports a shorter interval than it draws at (an iPhone), springs ran up to three times slow. It steps no longer than a 120 Hz frame however slow the display, so 24 or 30 Hz keeps the same curve, and a window back after a while catches up a quarter second, not all of it. `TestApp::report_interval` and `skip_frames` give a test such a display.
- **`ui.spring_to` keeps time on the interface's clock** (zgx-script): it read the wall clock for how long had passed since it was called, so on a clock of its own (a test's, a video made offline) its `delay` and the curve followed real time instead: a loaded test runner started a delayed spring early. It now reads `zgx::clock::now()`, which is the wall clock in a window, as before.
- **A box a spring or tween moves with `x` or `y` rests on a whole device pixel** (zgx-script): its translation is rounded to device pixels, as scroll content is, so a fractional target no longer leaves its edges and text between pixels. Needs no rounding in Luau. `View::pixel_snap` does the same for a Rust view.
- **A window drawing only video frames lets go of each it no longer shows as it draws on** (zgx-gpu): with no picture beside them, it kept every native surface it had shown until it went idle (up to the 64 MiB the cache allows), each holding a buffer its pool couldn't reuse, and where it did let go its only flush came before CoreVideo's cache would.
- **A sideways swipe over a `scroll_x` in a page no longer stops it** (zgx): the swipe's slightest drift downward moved the page and latched the gesture there, so the view stopped until a pause. A horizontal view now takes the whole of a swipe mostly sideways, and a gesture stays with the view it latched to through a finger's drift, until a pause or a clear turn to the other axis.
- **A terminal answers a program's queries in the order it asked** (zgx-terminal): a colour query (OSC 4, 10, 11, 12) was answered at the end of each read, after the cursor and device reports asked after it. termenv (gh, glamour, lipgloss) asks for the background, OSC 11, then the cursor, CSI 6n, took the cursor report first as "no colour" and stopped reading, and the colour's answer that followed read as typed keys (gh's prompt: "unexpected escape sequence from terminal"). Every answer now goes back in the order asked, in one write, each colour with its query's terminator. Linux and Windows; macOS's Ghostty terminals already did.
- **A glyph from a fallback font no longer moves the terminal's text off its grid** (zgx, zgx-gpu, zgx-terminal; `zgx-cosmic-text` 0.19.12): a braille spinner or graph (⠋ Loading, btop), a letterlike symbol (℃) or a Thai letter, drawn in a fallback font a little wider or narrower than a cell, pushed every character after it off the grid. A terminal run now takes exactly its characters' cells, each glyph centred in its own, whichever font draws it. `LetterSpacing::cells(width)` asks it of any text: a grid kept in the spacing's own bits, so a font style is no bigger.
- **A terminal's cells are whole device pixels** (zgx, zgx-terminal): a cell was the font's advance exactly (15.6 pixels at a scale of 2), so columns' edges and glyphs fell at a different fraction of a pixel across a line. Its width is now rounded and its height rounded up to device pixels, and measured again when the window's scale factor changes; on a 2x Mac the grid is a little wider (8 points a cell for 7.8). `NodeHandle::observe_text_geometry` and `Scene::pixel_scale` are public, for views that measure text by device pixels.
- **Closing a terminal gives back its memory in a window showing no frame** (zgx-terminal). The idle trim arms as a frame presents, so a terminal closed in a covered, hidden or minimized window kept its screen and pictures' memory, freed but not returned, until the window next drew: 71 MB against 36 MB after four screens of pictures. Closing now asks for the trim (`zgx::idle::request`).
- **A terminal on Windows announces pictures only where they can arrive** (zgx-terminal): Windows' own ConPTY drops the kitty graphics and sixel strings, so a program saw sixel announced and sent pictures that never showed. There, device attributes no longer include sixel and picture commands go unanswered, as in a terminal without pictures. Shipped beside the app, Windows Terminal's `conpty.dll` and `OpenConsole.exe` (MIT, `Microsoft.Windows.Console.ConPTY` 1.25.260930003 tested) carry them, and pictures show.
- **A terminal opened while its window runs no longer panics on its first output** (zgx-terminal). Output read before the view's first layout built the rows while the slots they take theirs from were still borrowed. The panic was contained, but it ended the tasks of that tick with it, an app's among them.
- **`zgx test` gives an app seconds, not milliseconds, and clears what killed runs left** (zgx-script): a test's fixtures can be heavy and the machine busy (CPU time grows under load too), so a mounted app now gets 5 s per call and 30 s to load, where a loop that never ends is still stopped. A run also removes the `zgx-test-storage-<pid>-<n>` directories of runs that were killed (a process that is gone, or one older than a day): a SIGKILL, a crash or a power cut cannot remove its own, and thousands had piled up.
- **The Edit menu's Select All selects a selectable region's text** (zgx-desktop): with no field focused it takes the text last pressed in, as ⌘A does; before, it did nothing there. A copy from a field by a key that reaches the view directly (tests, `zgx inspect key`) now goes through its `on_copy`, as the window's does.
- **A test runs the Edit menu's items** (zgx-script): `app:command("copy")`, `"cut"`, `"paste"` and `"select-all"` do what the desktop's Edit menu does, in the focused field or the selectable text last pressed in, a copy through its `on_copy`, and say whether anything took it.
- **`zgx test` interrupted on Windows leaves nothing in the temp directory** (zgx-script): Ctrl-C or Ctrl-Break mid-run removes the directories its tests made before it ends, with a console interrupt's exit code (`STATUS_CONTROL_C_EXIT`), as Ctrl-C and SIGTERM already did on Unix.
- **`zgx test` interrupted while a test writes leaves no directory behind** (zgx-script): on Ctrl-C (or Ctrl-Break) its cleanup removed what the run's tests made while a test went on running, so a file a test wrote in that moment could make its directory again, or keep it from being removed. The cleanup now holds the run's list from its start until the process ends, and a test makes and writes its files under that list, so a write under way finishes first and none starts after.
- **`zgx test` leaves nothing in the temp directory** (zgx-script): each test's storage and each mount's files go once the test's apps have (an app saves as it goes, which wrote its storage again after the test removed it), what is left goes as each file's VM does, a failing or panicking test's too, and on Ctrl-C (Unix) the run removes what it made before it ends. `zgx test` had left a `zgx-test-storage-*` directory for nearly every test, tens of thousands on a machine that runs them often. zgx-script's own test suites leave theirs no more either.
- **A window snapped by a window manager keeps its title bar's buttons where they are** (zgx-desktop, macOS): a window resized by a frame set from outside (Raycast, Rectangle and others set it through Accessibility, its position then its size), or by a drag, reported the inset title bar's buttons (`WindowInfo::controls`, `ui.window.controls`) as far down the window as its height grew, and the title bar's area as tall as that, until the window next moved or resized: winit reports the resize from the content view, before AppKit moves the title bar to the new top. They're measured from the title bar's own container now, so a title bar laid out around the traffic lights stays beside them.
- **An input method asking about an editor that is gone no longer ends the app** (zgx, zgx-desktop, Windows): Windows' Text Services Framework can ask where text is from inside its own message handling, after the focused editor left the scene and before zgx tells it focus moved. `Scene::text_range_area` now answers `None` for a node no longer in the scene, and `EditorHandle::input_method_area` an empty rect, where both panicked (`stale scene node`). A panic in the text store's COM methods now fails that call instead of aborting the process, which a panic can't unwind out of.
- **`ui.editor.insert_atom`, `atoms`, `undo`, `redo`, `can_undo` and `can_redo` work on the DOM** (zgx-dom, zgx-script): a textarea with `atoms` in the browser takes a script's chip (replacing the typed `@que`, one undo step) and its undo, as on the desktop; they returned nil or false there. A plain browser textarea (no `atoms`) keeps the browser's own undo, which a script can't reach.
- A window at rest gives back the vertex memory its largest frame took (a long list scrolled, a big page): on Vulkan, GL and the other plain upload paths its vertex buffer stayed at that frame's size and its staging chunks were never freed. On Apple silicon and Direct3D 12, the buffers the GPU is done with are dropped even when rest comes right after a frame.
- **A clip shows the file's frame on screen at its time, wherever it starts** (zgx-video): ffmpeg's rate conversion took the file frame nearest each of the composition's frames, so at a rate that doesn't divide evenly (a 7 fps file in a 30 fps video) a frame could show before its time, and a clip started between two of the file's frames could begin on the next one. The decoder now seeks a second early, keeps the file's times, takes for each composition frame the last file frame to start at or before it, and starts at the frame asked for, as `docs/video.md` says and as the iOS decoder does.
- **A video draws its large pictures, and names its own fonts** (`zgx-video`): a picture larger than 256 by 256 pixels decodes off the thread a frame after it's first drawn, as a window wants, so a render or still drew it as nothing. A video's frames now decode it as they draw it (`zgx::image::set_whole_frames_on_this_thread`). Fonts in a `fonts` folder beside a composition's script are its text's to name by family (`font = "Geist"`), where before only the system's were.
- **A sound inside `video.loop` plays on every pass** (zgx-video): each pass placed it at the loop's start, so it sounded once; it's now placed at the start of the pass it mounts in, as its pictures already are. A sound under a `video.freeze` keeps its place.
- **Nested sounds land where the sequence places them** (zgx-video): a series, loop, freeze, sound, clip or sequence given to a `video.sequence` as a table rather than a function was built before the sequence, in the time around it, so its sounds started at the wrong frames (a series in a sequence from frame 20 placed its first scene's sound at 0) and its frames counted from the wrong start. Each now takes its time where it mounts, from the sequence around it, so offsets add up however children are given. `video.audio` adds an empty box of no size, out of the flow, and places its sound when that box mounts: a call whose result is dropped (a statement, not among a node's children) now places nothing. A script nesting through functions, as the showcase does, renders byte-identically.
- **A player's translucent picture shows as drawn** (zgx-video, zgx, zgx-gpu): the frames a `Player` reads back are premultiplied, and shown as images they were premultiplied again, darkening every translucent edge (half-transparent red over white showed (191, 127, 127), not (255, 127, 127)). `ImageData::new_premultiplied` (zgx) makes an image of pixels premultiplied already, which the renderer draws as they are, and `is_premultiplied` says which an image holds; the player's frames are made so.
- **A script hears its video player stop.** `ui.shared(name).playing` stayed true after `ui.invoke("<name>.play", false)` until another frame came, and a stopped player sends none: it's told as the player is played or stopped.
- **A video's scripts run under a render's limits** (`zgx-video`): a composition that mounts a whole app (its first build) no longer stops with "script exceeded its time budget"; a render's calls may take a minute and its load two, since nothing waits on a frame (`zgx_video::script::RENDER_LIMITS`).
- **Debug builds no longer crash on Vulkan loaders before 1.4.345** (zgx-gpu): those loaders' debug-utils calls race across threads (Vulkan-Loader#1866, Ubuntu 26.04's 1.4.341), and wgpu's debug layer makes them every frame. On such a loader a debug build leaves that layer off and says so once on stderr; `ZGX_VULKAN_DEBUG=1` turns it back on. Release builds never used it.
- On WARP, a renderer made on one thread as the last one on another was dropped no longer crashes: WARP's one device is kept for the process, where a second device was made beside the first while it finished its last frame.
- On WARP (Direct3D 12 on the CPU, as in VMs, remote sessions and machines without a Direct3D 12 GPU), renderers on several threads of one process share one device: a device each made WARP 10.0.26100 crash, or draw nothing, when they drew at once.
- On WARP, wgpu devices of their own on several threads of one process (zgx-3d's, GPU views', an app's) no longer crash or draw nothing when they submit at once: their submissions take turns.
- **A window covering its display on Wayland is the display's size** (zgx-desktop): `WindowOptions::covers_display` sizes it in the compositor's logical pixels, as xdg-output says them, from its first frame. It asked for the display's mode as logical pixels where the compositor says a window's scale only after its first frame, as Hyprland does: over a 3840 by 2160 output scaled 1.5 it opened 3840 by 2160 of them, half as large again as the output, its centre off the output's; under sway it opened that large and shrank a frame later.
- **A click on a Wayland popup open more than a moment is heard at once** (zgx-desktop): with nothing left to do, the event loop slept through a popup's pointer input, a compositor's dismissal and its pings until something else woke it, so a menu open longer than 1.5 s took no click.
- **A menu opened between pixels stays sharp on Wayland** (zgx-gpu, zgx-desktop): a popup at a place that isn't a whole pixel (a context menu at the pointer) drew a buffer a pixel wider or taller than its surface, all of it resampled to fit, its text blurred. A popup now shows on the whole points round its box, its buffer exactly its surface, and the pointer lands on what it shows there. `zgx_gpu::plane_size` says what a plane's surface should measure.
- **A Linux web view no longer makes Orca 50 lose the app's window** (zgx-desktop, `webkitgtk`): clicking or tabbing into a page focused GTK's offscreen window that draws it, and Orca 50, finding no top-level for that window, cleared its focus and left the app. GTK's side of a page (its window and view) no longer sends screen readers events or says it is active or focused; the page's own tree, which the app's window embeds, still reaches them.
- **A web view's load that a policy decision or a plug-in took over is no failure** (zgx-desktop, macOS): WebKit's `FrameLoadInterruptedByPolicyChange` (102) and plug-in-handled load (204) are passed over as `NSURLErrorCancelled` is, so a download link or a redirect the app's policy takes no longer reports a load failure.
- **A mouse wheel's scroll shows a new frame on every refresh** (zgx-desktop, macOS): a frame applying a wheel's notch kept the cadence of the frame before it, as an animation's steady frames do, so once one frame showed a refresh late the frames after it, each finished in a couple of milliseconds, waited a refresh more (11-16 ms) until eight in a row came 1.5 ms early. A notch's frame answers input and keeps no later cadence now; ready past its refresh's point it still waits for the next, so each refresh takes one frame. Scrolling a deep transcript under glass by a wheel tape: 104-117 frames a second with 3-15 missed refreshes became 119.6-120 with at most 0.4.
- **A mouse wheel's notches show on every refresh** (zgx-desktop, macOS): each notch now moves the view as it arrives and draws at once, as an `NSScrollView` takes it, and its frame hands over at the refresh grid's point. Notches used to wait for the next refresh's tick: the system hands them over about once a refresh, near its start, so one arriving just after a tick waited a whole refresh and that refresh showed nothing new. Real wheel notches at 144 Hz into a 7,000-row list on a 120 Hz display showed a new frame on 104-113 refreshes a second; now 118-120, at the same latency (p50 9.2 ms from receipt to shown; p90 9.3, from 15).
- **A mouse wheel spun fast moves content at a steady speed** (zgx-desktop): each notch's ease made speed follow the distance left, so a fast spin pumped at the notches' own rate, up to two and a half times frame to frame, which read as text vibrating. In a spin each notch is now handed over evenly until the next is due. A notch on its own moves as it did.
- **A wheel tape's notches play at the start of their refresh** (zgx-desktop): `Ui::wheel_tape` played each `lines` notch at its own time, so a tape at 144 Hz landed nearly all of them in the last quarter of a 120 Hz refresh, where macOS never hands an app a wheel's notches, and its frames measured pacing no real wheel meets. Each notch now plays at the start of the refresh it falls due in, on the display link's grid; a tape with the line `# notches as timed` keeps its own times, and a trackpad's motion and the pointer's events are as they were.
- **A window asked for on a screen of another scale opens there on macOS** (zgx-desktop, through winit): a window's physical position and size (`WindowBounds`, a restored frame, `display`) were read at the scale of the key window's screen. A window asked for on a 1x screen beside a 2x one opened at the wrong place or size, or on the other screen. Both are now read at the scale of the screen that holds the window's top left.
- **A window asked for on another screen opens there on macOS** (zgx-desktop, through winit): a window given a position (`WindowBounds`, a restored frame) on a display other than the one holding the key window opened on the key window's display instead, its height mirrored, as AppKit reads a new window's rect relative to the key window's screen unless it is given one. It is now made on the screen its top left is on, in that screen's coordinates.
- **A window on Windows no longer runs out of GDI handles** (this repository's winit: apps from crates.io get upstream's, which has no occlusion check reading it that often): reading a window's DPI took a device context and never gave it back, and the occlusion check reads it twice a second while a window draws and each second while it's covered, so after an hour or two of that `GetDC` failed and the app panicked with "`GetDC` returned null". The context is now taken only where it's read, and released.
- **An event loop asked to exit as it starts ends at once on Windows** (zgx-desktop's winit): an exit asked for in `resumed` (which winit delivers as the loop first goes idle) or in `about_to_wait` no longer waits for a window message before ending. Where none came, as on an idle desktop, the loop never returned: `a_turns_pointer_moves_come_as_its_last_on_windows` hung for minutes on a quiet PC while CI's runner, which always had a message coming, passed.
- **A popup on Windows shows the cursor zgx chose** (zgx-desktop): a menu, a dropdown or a popover in a window of its own always answered Windows with the arrow, so its rows' hand and its fields' I-beam never showed. It now answers with the cursor zgx last set.
- **Tests that failed on every Windows machine pass there, and CI's Windows job runs them** (zgx, zgx-script). A test pasted a temp path into a Luau string, whose backslashes read as escapes. Another joined a path with `/` and compared it with the native one the CLI prints. The reference test wrote a doc example's `(granted …)` note into a file name, so the plugin it shows never loaded on any platform; it now loads, granted. The scroll tests assumed menu items without spacing and scrollbars without arrow buttons, which Windows' have. `luau_pages` runs where Luau's page pool exists (macOS, Linux).
- **Shift with the wheel over a window without focus** (zgx-desktop on Windows): a mouse message's Shift and Control are now read from the message, so a window behind another scrolls across with Shift and the wheel, and Shift- or Control-clicks there are seen. They were read from the keyboard's state, which only the focused window's thread has. A winit-only fix: apps on crates.io's winit keep the old behaviour.
- **A script's files are found under a linked directory** (zgx-script): a root reached through a symbolic link (macOS's temporary directory, a linked project folder) failed its containment check for every file that exists, so a `.gltf`'s buffers came up "not found". It's as strict as before: a link in the root that reaches out of it is still out.
- **`ui.write_file` writes any bytes, and `ui.read_file` reads them** (zgx-script): a string with a byte that isn't UTF-8 (a PNG, any binary file) failed with "error converting Lua string to String" and wrote nothing; it is now written as its bytes. `ui.write_file(file, { base64 = … })` writes bytes given in base64 (a rendered canvas's PNG), up to 256 MiB. `ui.read_file(file, { base64 = true })` reads a file's bytes whole, in base64, where text replaced each byte that isn't UTF-8.
- **An app starts on an X server without RandR** (zgx-desktop, through winit): winit asked the server for RandR's version and panicked without the extension (`Xvfb -extension RANDR`, some VNC and bare servers). Without it the root window is now the one monitor, at its size and scale, and screen changes go unheard.
- **Popups look right on X11 with no compositor, and under picom** (zgx-desktop): with no compositing manager, a popup's window kept the margin its shadow draws in and its corners' clear pixels, all of them black, and translucent colours were drawn over black: a menu showed as a black box, its light theme's text hard to read. Its window is now its panel alone, corners cut, and `ui.prefers.reduced_transparency` (`system_preferences`) is true there, so glass turns solid; a compositor started later is heard as a window is activated. Under picom, whose own shadow went round that whole margin, a popup now asks for none (`_COMPTON_SHADOW`).
- **A box zero on purpose no longer reads as squeezed** (zgx): `layout_warnings`, and so `zgx inspect errors` and the debug banner, said "zero width" of what's inside a box given `w = 0` or `h = 0` (an anchor for what overflows it, a panel slid shut), and of a wrapper sized by such a child. A box squeezed to nothing by its parent is still reported.

## 0.1.10 (2026-11-04)

A release for apps that run plugins: what a plugin may reach is now the host's to decide (`ScriptHost::guard` and its new `GuardKind`s, `fetch_policy`, `reserve_prefix`, decision controls, sealed regions, a top layer), and a host can ask where a plugin's additions show and forget its state as it is uninstalled. It also has lists that scroll with the view around them, a window's full-screen state in scripts, glass on Linux and in Windows' popups, and terminals that follow the theme.

Three things change for a plugin, each with its migration under Changed: `Capability::Override("*")` no longer reaches the app's modules, a plugin's `ui.slot` is named `<plugin>.…`, and its shortcuts need a modifier or a grant. The app's own scripts are as they were.

Published with it: `zgx-cosmic-text` 0.19.10 (a family's nearest face for a weight it lacks).

**Apps from crates.io get upstream winit**, not the copy patched in this repository, and a fix that lives only in that copy doesn't reach them. In this release that is two: glass blurring on Wayland compositors other than KWin, and `set_theme` on Windows holding through a system change. Entries that need the patch say so, earlier releases' too, and [releasing](docs/releasing.md#what-stays-patched-in-the-repository-alone) lists all it does; [getting started](docs/getting-started.md) has the `[patch.crates-io]` lines that take the repository's.

### Changed: what to check in an existing app

- **`WindowOptions` has a new field, `caption_height`** (zgx-desktop): a `WindowOptions` written out field by field stops compiling. *Migrate:* end it with `..Default::default()`, or set `caption_height: None` (the default).
- **`Capability::Override("*")` no longer reaches the app's modules** (zgx-script): replacing a module runs as the app's own code, so it takes a grant that says so. `Capability::OverrideElement(target)` is for `ui.contribute { replace }` and `ui.filter`, `Capability::OverrideModule("@app/…")` for `ui.override`, each with `"*"` for any. The older `Override` reads by what it names, so `Override("#x")`, `Override("*")` for elements and `Override("@app/x")` work as before. *Migrate:* a plugin given `Override("*")` that overrides modules needs `OverrideModule("*")`; in `zgx.json`, `override-module:*` beside `override:*`.
- **A plugin offers only slots of its own** (zgx-script): `ui.slot(name)` in a plugin's VM takes `<plugin>.…`, so a plugin can't pose as the app's slot or another's and take what is contributed there. *Migrate:* name a plugin's slot `<plugin>.name`, and have what contributes to it say so. The app's own scripts name theirs as before.
- **A plugin's shortcut has a modifier** (zgx-script): `ui.command { key = "k" }` from a plugin (no modifier, or Shift alone on a character) would take that key from every field, page and terminal, and is now an error; one a state or function says is dropped as it reads so, in `ScriptHost::dropped_shortcuts`. *Migrate:* give it a modifier, or grant the plugin `Capability::Named("zgx.command.bare_keys")`.

### Added

- **Decision controls no plugin reaches or covers** (zgx-script): `ui.decision { key, … }` holds controls drawn and hit over all a plugin opens, in the room it keeps in flow, scrolling with it.
  - A plugin can't make one, or get into one; replacing, hiding or disabling what declares one is refused and reported.
  - While one is mounted, a plugin's popups, tooltips and context menus stay in the window, under it (`zgx::compose::PopupsInWindow`).
  - `ScriptHost::decision_shown(key)` says whether it is on screen, for a host's fallback in its top layer. In Rust, `zgx::compose::hoist`.
- **`ScriptHost::guard` covers terminals, web pages and what else leaves the interface** (zgx-script): new `GuardKind`s `Term` (`ui.term`, by its command or the shell: refused, it raises and nothing starts), `TermInput` (a terminal's `:input`, `:paste`, `:binding`), `Web` (`src`, `:load`, `:html`, `html`, and each navigation the platform starts: a link, a form, a redirect, a page's script, history), `Clipboard`, `Dialog`, `File`, `Folder` and `Notification`. A refused `src` leaves the page where it was; a refused navigation is cancelled and reported. What a page fetches once loaded, and in-page `history` steps, no platform shows: `docs/plugins.md` says so, and lists what isn't asked.
- **`ScriptHost::guard` is asked of links, pictures, windows, kept values and a download's dialog** (zgx-script, zgx): new `GuardKind`s `OpenLink` (a link in text opening, on a click or its Open Link item, through zgx's new `platform::LinkGuard` service), `Picture` (a remote picture, apart from `Fetch`), `Window` (`ui.window`: refused, it raises and nothing opens) and `Persist` (`ui.persist`: refused, it starts from what is saved and its writes are held in memory, so a preview in the app's VM looks as the app does and overwrites nothing), and a download's `d:ask()` under `Dialog`. `ScriptHost::keep_held_state` saves what was held, when the person keeps what they previewed; `ScriptHost::drop_held_state` or quitting lets it go. A refused `ui.fetch` rejects its promise rather than raising, so a top level isn't failed and what handles a failure says so.
- **A host's top layer** (zgx): `View::top_layer` is a layer filling the window, above every other layer, dialog and popover and live under them, for the host's own controls that no plugin may cover. A script's `z` can't reach it.
- **`zgx inspect key` taps modifiers alone** (zgx-script): `zgx inspect key alt` (or `ctrl+shift`) holds them and lets them go, as a window reports a tap, so what watches `ui.modifiers` (an Alt-to-show menu bar) can be driven; it used to fail with "unknown key".
- **A window's glass blurs the desktop behind it on Linux** (zgx-desktop): a window with `glass` asks the Wayland compositor to blur what is behind it. It was only transparent before, so on KWin, which blurs only what asks, it was see-through. From crates.io that reaches KWin alone, through its own blur protocol (upstream winit's `with_blur`). With the repository's winit ([getting started](docs/getting-started.md)) it goes through the standard `ext-background-effect-v1` first, so Hyprland blurs too. How strong the blur is stays the compositor's (Hyprland: `decoration:blur`, and only with blur enabled there).
- **A list can scroll with the scroll view around it** (zgx, zgx-script): `ui.list { …, virtualize = "ancestor" }` (Rust: `View::virtualize_in_ancestor`) is as tall as all its rows and builds only those the scroll view around it shows, plus its overscan, again as that view scrolls or resizes. A panel that scrolls as a whole, its sections lists, builds about 20 rows of 302 instead of all of them. With `height` or `heights`: Tab and focus go through its rows, a row asked for (`scroll_to_row`) or found in the page scrolls that view to it, and a reload finds focus again on its row. Opt-in: a list's rows otherwise build as before.
- **`ScriptHost::contribution_bounds(plugin)`** (zgx-script, with zgx's `NodeHandle::window_bounds`): where what a plugin added shows in the window now, a box for each thing mounted in a slot or before, after or in place of an element, through any transforms and zooms. What a `ui.filter` changes stays the app's own. Costs nothing without plugins.
- **`ScriptHost::forget_state(script)`** (zgx-script): for uninstalling a plugin, `"plugin:<name>"` forgets its `ui.state` and `ui.persist` values, what waits to be saved and its saved files, gone by the time it returns; a write already queued can't bring them back.
- **An app can police what its plugins reach over the network** (zgx-script, zgx-run): `ScriptHost::fetch_policy(impl FetchPolicy)` is asked of a request's URL, of every address its host resolves to, and of its headers, with the plugin's name as the caller (`None` for the app). zgx-run asks it on every redirect, before connecting to any address (so a name that later resolves to this device or the LAN is refused too), and for `ui.fetch`, remote images and `ui.socket` alike. A plugin's refused or failed remote image is its own: the app showing the same URL fetches it at once, not after that plugin's backoff. A plugin never takes a proxy from the environment, and never sets Host, Connection, Transfer-Encoding, TE, Upgrade or `Proxy-*`, with or without a policy. A host with a transport of its own reads the same with `Body::caller`, `Body::fetch_policy`, `Socket::caller` and `Socket::fetch_policy`.
- **`ScriptHost::reserve_prefix(prefix)`** (zgx-script): keeps `prefix` and `prefix.…` for the app's own scripts: no plugin, whatever it is named or granted, offers a `ui.slot` or declares a `ui.command` `id` so named.
- **`ScriptHost::guard(|kind, name| …)`** (zgx-script, with `GuardKind`): asked before the app's own scripts reach a host action, async action or function (by name), or a `ui.fetch`, remote picture or `ui.socket` (by URL), each time; an `Err` stops it, its text the script's error. For previewing someone's template in the app's VM. A host that sets none reads one thread-local flag at each.
- **Sealed regions** (zgx-script): `ui.sealed { … }` is a region no plugin contributes into, replaces, filters, targets or draws over (a Settings page). In it, `ui.plugin_page { plugin = name }` shows what that plugin alone gives `ui.contribute("plugin_page", …)`, cut to its box, portals included (`zgx::compose::confined`).
- **Terminals take their colours from the theme** (zgx, zgx-terminal, zgx-desktop): the tokens `terminal.foreground`, `terminal.background`, `terminal.cursor`, `terminal.cursor_text`, `terminal.selection_background`, `terminal.selection_foreground` and `terminal.ansi.0` to `terminal.ansi.15` colour a terminal where the theme sets them, and follow a theme switch, the system's appearance or a `theme =` scoped over the terminal live, scrollback included, without restarting it. Under Ghostty they go over the user's own Ghostty configuration; in the engine-drawn terminal (Linux, Windows) over its palette. No built-in theme sets them, and one left unset keeps the terminal's own colour, so an app that never sets them sees no change. VS Code themes set them from their `terminal.*` colours. Before, switching an app to a light theme left Ghostty's text in the user's dark colours, pale on white. In Rust: `zgx::terminal::TerminalColors` and `follow_theme_colors`, and `TerminalBackend::set_colors`.
- **`ui.window.full_screen` and `ui.window.full_screen_transition`** (zgx-script, zgx-desktop): whether the app's window is in full screen, however it went in or out (the green button, Ctrl+Cmd+F, the menu, Mission Control, a script), and, while macOS animates it, which way it goes and about how long it takes (`{ phase = "entering" | "leaving", duration }`, nil otherwise and always on Linux and Windows). `ui.window.controls` is nil in full screen, so a title bar laid out around the traffic lights can slide over as they go. Rust hosts have `WindowContext::full_screen_transition` (`FullScreenTransition`) beside `WindowInfo::fullscreen`, and pass both to scripts with `ScriptHost::window_full_screen`.
- **`WindowOptions::caption_height`** (zgx-desktop; `caption_height` in `zgx.json`): how tall `Titlebar::Inset`'s caption buttons are on Windows, in the content's points, so they span an app's title bar at any zoom. Drawing, hit-testing and `WindowInfo::controls` all use it. Unset, they stay 32 points of the display.

### Performance

- **Text inputs a width change reached lay out once, however many**: each text input whose width changed ran a layout pass of its own, from the root, for what the input before it had just dirtied: N inputs, N + 1 passes a frame while a window resized (9 at 8 inputs). They now draw from the frame's layout, and the frame's next pass lays out what they dirtied: 2 passes a frame at any number of inputs.

### Fixed

- **A window keeps drawing through a full-screen transition, and says where its title bar's buttons end up** (zgx-desktop, macOS): AppKit counts a window covered while it animates its snapshot of it, and zgx stopped drawing for those 0.55 s, with `ui.window.shown` false meanwhile; now it goes on, and counts the window covered only if it still is once the transition is over. Through a transition `ui.window.controls` and `titlebar_area` hold where they end up (nil going in; their place going out), never AppKit's place partway. `full_screen_transition`'s `duration` is as long as the last one that way took, about 0.55 s at first, where AppKit's resize estimate said 0.35 s in and 0.07 s out.
- **Text taking its colour from a token around it follows every theme switch** (zgx): a text inheriting `$text.primary` (or any colour token) from a row or button around it took the new theme's colour on the first switch and kept it after: the second switch's paint-only style run saw nothing of its own change and resolved the inherited size and family again, but not the colour. Sidebar rows and button labels kept the old scheme's text after switching themes twice. The inherited colour is resolved again with them.
- **A layer over the window doesn't dim it** (zgx): a portal made a layer (`View::layer`: `ui.toast`'s host, a heads-up) or shown at a caret got a modal's backdrop (`backdrop`, `#00000082`) over the whole window, though a layer has none: every toast dimmed the window while it showed. Only a modal has one now.
- **A list scrolled with the view around it follows a box clipping it** (zgx): a `virtualize = "ancestor"` list inside a box that eases open (or shut) now builds the rows the box shows as it grows, not only once the scroll view moves; before, reopened, it showed none until a scroll.
- **One bad menu item no longer costs the whole menu bar, and every shortcut with it** (zgx-desktop, zgx-script). In Zeron, a plugin's sheet called `set_menus` without the app's commands, and every shortcut died until relaunch. Three things are fixed:
  - `WindowFactory::set_menus` (and `Application::menus`) refused the whole bar when one item had no label, repeated another's action, or had an accelerator another item held or no key names (⌘é, ⌘?). Now that item is left out, or keeps no shortcut, and the rest is shown. Each problem is written on stderr (`zgx: menus: …`) when it first appears. `set_menus` errs only when the application is closed. Its docs now say it replaces every window's menus at once.
  - A plugin whose script failed as it loaded kept the `ui.command`s and `ui.window`s it had declared before failing. They stayed in the menu bar, and `run_command` returned false for them. Everything it declared now goes with it.
  - On macOS, a menu bar AppKit could not make (`Event::MenusChanged`, or the first bar) ended the app. Now it is reported, the last good bar stays, and a first bar falls back to the standard application and Window menus.
- **A family without the weight asked for draws in its nearest face**: Medium (500) text in a family with Regular and Bold alone, most installed families, drew in another family (the fallback list's first with a Medium). It draws in the face CSS's font matching picks in its own family (zgx-cosmic-text 0.19.10). Thanks to @katulevskiy.
- **A popover's arrow is painted as its panel is, as it changes** (zgx): the arrow took a copy of the panel's styles as it mounted, so a panel whose background, border or opacity followed state (a reactive style) drew its arrow in the colour it had first. The arrow takes what the panel's reactive style gives each time it runs.
- **A popup's glass blurs on Windows** (zgx-gpu, zgx-desktop): a menu, popover or tooltip with `backdrop_blur` showed what's behind it sharp, as a popup's window has nothing of the app's to blur. It now blurs what its window last drew under it, taken again when the window repaints there, at the cost of a texture the popup's size while it shows and a copy per redraw. Past its window's edge, or over another popup, what's behind still shows through its fill.
- **A font token a script names a family for takes it**: a script's theme override (`theme = { ["font.sans"] = "Inter" }`) or `ui.theme.tokens` gave a font token a family's name as text, which the theme refused for a font token, so the override was dropped and text kept the default family. It is read as a theme file reads a font token's word: a CSS font-family list, its first family. Thanks to @katulevskiy.
- **The terminal follows the theme's monospaced font**: the engine-drawn terminal drew its grid and its composition in the platform's monospaced font whatever the theme said. It takes `font.mono` from the theme scope its view mounts in, and follows it as it changes (the cell measured again, every row made again). Where the theme names none, it keeps the platform's monospaced font. The Ghostty backend on macOS keeps its own font settings. Thanks to @katulevskiy.
- **Windows' caption buttons show everywhere, in the app's own ink, and agree when zoomed** (zgx-desktop): they were an icon font, which a Windows 10 tester saw as empty boxes; they are now drawn as boxes and bars of whole pixels, with no font. They followed the system's light or dark, not the app's, so they were invisible on a light app on a dark system. They are now inked from the app's theme, as its own title bar controls are (`text.secondary` at rest, `text.primary` under the pointer, `text.tertiary` in a window in the back, over its `hover` and `pressed`; Close keeps its red), with an outline icon's line weight, so this changes how every Windows app's caption buttons look. With the repository's winit ([getting started](docs/getting-started.md)), `set_theme` on Windows also updates `theme()` and a system change no longer undoes it; from crates.io, a system change puts the window's own light or dark (its Mica) back to the system's, while the buttons stay in the app's ink. In a zoomed window the buttons drawn, hit-tested and reported disagreed (at 150%: 207 pixels drawn, 138 clickable); all three, snap layouts included, now read one geometry and stay 46 by 32 points of the display, whatever the zoom. A window that isn't resizable, or whose smallest and largest sizes match, no longer shows Maximize or answers resize bands.
- **Windows' Transparency effects setting is honoured** (zgx-desktop): `ui.prefers.reduced_transparency` and `SystemPreferences` report `EnableTransparency`, and follow it as it changes. With it off, glass is opaque, as on macOS.
- **In a zoomed window on macOS, baked loops and opaque GPU views lie where their boxes are** (zgx-gpu, zgx-desktop): their Core Animation layers were placed in the zoomed window's points where the host layer's hold no zoom, so a ring with a looping `shaderTime` floated up and left of its row at 110%, further the further from the window's corner, and was too small. A host sets it with `GpuRenderer::set_zoom`. A window read back shows the caret there too.

## 0.1.9 (2026-11-03)

A release of fixes before work pauses, from bugs Zeron's apps found and from the remaining bugs on Linux, Windows and macOS; the few additions are what those fixes needed (a host-loaded locale catalog, opt-in partial hot reloads, the window's size in plugins). Rust and Luau code builds as before, but for a struct literal of zgx's `trace::Frame` (it has new fields), below with what to change. Every published 0.1.8 crate builds against this release. Forks published with it: `zgx-cosmic-text` 0.19.9 (glyphs drawn through Core Text where only `hvgl` has their shapes) and `zgx-alacritty-terminal` 0.26.1 (a Windows program's last output read after it exits). Behaviours change too, each below with what to check.

### Changed: what to check in an existing app

- **`trace::Frame` has new fields, `events` and `layout`** (zgx): the input events a traced frame took, and what layout measured afresh in it (`zgx inspect frames`). A `Frame` written out field by field stops compiling. *Migrate:* end it with `..Default::default()`.
- **A picture that can't be shown is told, never a script error** (zgx-script): a `ui.image`, `ui.svg` or `bg_image` whose file is gone or isn't a picture draws nothing, shows the image's `fallback` (now for local files too, not only remote ones) and is told to its new `on_error(why)`, else warned of once, with the script line naming it. It was a script error, which in a gallery stopped every window's reloads, though a file can go at any time. `on_load({ w, h })` tells a picture's size once known (a file's from its header, before it draws; a remote one's as it arrives), and `ui.image_size(src)` reads it from the header alone, nothing decoded, so a design system can hold a picture's place at its final size.
- **A style re-run with the same result no longer resets paint set elsewhere** (zgx): as with an element only changed props touch, an unrelated state change that re-runs a node's reactive style (or a script's style function) to the same result writes nothing, so paint an `anime.animate`, a script or a direct scene write set on the node (its effects, transform, clip) stays until the style's own value for that property changes; then the style's new value wins. Before, such a re-run restored the node's own paint, undoing what was set since (as a style changing only a box's size already did not).
- **WebView2's browser keys are off** (zgx-desktop's `webview2`): Ctrl+F, F3, Ctrl+P, Ctrl+R, F5, zoom, Alt+arrows and F12 in a page reach the page, then the app's bindings when the page leaves them alone, as on macOS and Linux, instead of WebView2's find bar, print preview, reload and DevTools. Editing keys stay the page's. Find with `WebView::find`; DevTools with `WebView::open_devtools`.

### Added

- **A screen reader moving to a node in a scroll view scrolls it in** (zgx-desktop, zgx): nodes in a scroll view offer AccessKit's `ScrollIntoView` (macOS `AXScrollToVisible`, UIA's and AT-SPI's scroll-into-view), and the tree with the node's new place goes out at once, not paced as geometry is. `Ui::scroll_into_view(node)` does the same for an app.
- **A shortcut recorder takes every key** (zgx, zgx-script, zgx-desktop): `capture_keys = true` on a node (`View::capture_keys`, `InputDispatcher::set_capture_keys`) sends every key to what is focused while focus is in it: no key binding takes one, and on macOS the menu bar's key equivalents (⌘Q, ⌘C, the app's commands) don't fire, the key going to zgx's view. A settings page records ⌘J as ⌘J instead of running its command. As focus leaves, shortcuts work again; a host asks with `InputDispatcher::captures_keys`, and `binds_key` answers no meanwhile.
- **A command's shortcut can follow a state** (zgx-script): `ui.command { key = state }` or `key = function() … end` publishes the commands again whenever what it reads changes, so a shortcuts setting rebinds a command at once, menus included, with no reload. While a key follows a state, a key an earlier command already holds stays with it and the later command is published without it, reported in `ScriptHost::dropped_shortcuts` (its `plugin` empty for the app's own), so a rebinding never gives the menu bar a key twice. `ui.shortcut_owner(keys)` names the command holding a key now.
- **Drop shadows** (zgx, zgx-gpu, zgx-script): `Styled::drop_shadow(x, y, blur, color)` (`Scene::set_drop_shadow`, `DropShadow`), Luau `drop_shadow` written as `shadow` is: a shadow of what a node and its subtree paint, following their alpha rather than the box (CSS's `filter: drop-shadow`): an icon's, a cut-out's, a rounded card's. The node paints as a cached layer; its shadow is a second slot of the effect atlas, blurred by the same passes as `filter_blur` and tinted there, then composited under the layer: a shadow moved or faded composites again without repainting the layer, and one blurred anew re-runs the passes only. Nodes without one cost nothing. Not drawn by the Direct3D 11 proof, as no layer effect is.
- **Focus moving into a list scrolls it there, clear of what floats over it** (zgx, zgx-script): Tab, Shift+Tab or `el:focus()` onto a control in a `ui.list`'s row (a transcript's tool row built beyond the rows in view) scrolls the list to it, the offset state following, as a plain scroll view already did. `scroll_padding` (`ui.scroll`, `ui.list`, `View::scroll_padding`; a number or `{ top, bottom, left, right }`) keeps that much clear inside its edges, so a focused row doesn't land under a composer or a title bar. `el:focus({ scroll = false })` (`NodeHandle::focus_with`, `InputDispatcher::focus_in_place`) leaves the views where they are.
- **A GIF in `ui.image` plays** (zgx, zgx-gpu): an image view whose source gives an animation's still (`ImageData::animated`, which zgx-gpu now decodes a GIF to) plays its frames as `animated_image` does, paused off screen, hidden or with the window unseen, its last frame kept once a finite one ends. The playback is set up the first time a source gives one, so a view of still pictures costs nothing more; a reactive `src` that changes to another GIF plays that one, and to a still stops. A GIF whose frames pass 4 MiB decoded streams them (`Animation::streamed`, zgx-gpu's `GIF_KEPT`): its file and a frame or two held, however long, all of it given back as it goes; a smaller one keeps its frames and costs nothing to play. Under the system's reduced motion an animation (`animated_image`'s too) shows its first frame, still.
- **A modifier pressed alone is heard: `Ui::modifiers()` and `ui.modifiers`** (zgx, zgx-desktop, zgx-script): the modifier keys held now, as a state that changes as one goes down or up, a modifier alone included (Command held to show ⌘1–9 hints, which no key event reports). Luau reads `ui.modifiers.shift`, `control`, `alt` and `meta`, each tracked. All are up as the window loses focus, so a ⌘-Tab away leaves none held. The state is made the first time something asks for it; until then the host writes nothing.
- **`ScriptHost::i18n(dir, source)` loads a catalog with no script** (zgx-script): a host loads the locale files in a folder of its choosing before any script runs, and `ui.t` and `ui.locale` work in the app's scripts and its plugins' with no `ui.i18n` call. A hot-reloading host now watches every catalog's locale files, so an edit to one shows in place without reloading the scripts, wherever the folder is. A host that preloaded its catalog through a one-line plugin no longer needs that plugin, which made every hot reload a full one.
- **`zgx inspect frames` measures an interaction as a whole** (zgx-script, zgx-desktop): `until` (a mark or a frame) ends the frames read, as `since` starts them, and `summary` answers what they cost together: their count and pacing, the work's median, p90, p95, p99 and most, the input events they took and the work per event (a drag's moves), and each phase's spread. Each frame says the input events it took (`events`). A tape `replay` plays can move the pointer too (`ms pointer x y down|moved|up`, from the target's centre), so a scripted drag lands a move a frame through the window's own input path, and its answer gives the same percentiles. The CLI reads a command's own fields given as `key=value` words (`frames n=300`, `mark name=drag`) and refuses a number field given anything else, where it fell back to the default.
- **`zgx inspect frames` says where layout's time went** (zgx, zgx-script): each traced frame counts the nodes layout measured afresh (not taken from a measurement kept from before) and their time, and names the costliest by their own time, their children's apart, as the script made them (`layout: 14 measured afresh, 5120 µs; costliest: @88 #chat (column screens/chat.luau:41) …`; `layout` in its JSON). An interaction's `summary` adds them up by node. While nothing traces, a fresh measure pays one relaxed load for it.
- **`scroll_padding` follows a state** (zgx, zgx-script): `scroll_padding` on `ui.list` and `ui.scroll` takes a state or a function as well as a number or `{ top, bottom, left, right }`, read again as what it reads changes (`View::scroll_padding_with`), so a composer growing to more lines keeps a focused row clear of it, rather than the height it had as the list was made.
- **A long reply landing whole is parsed over frames** (zgx-script): `ui.markdown(text, { key, lazy = true })` (or `lazy = bytes`; 4 KB by default) answers a long text arriving at once (not by appends) with one entry a block at once, those not parsed yet `{ t = "pending", hint, lines, from, to }`, and parses them over the next frames, the end first, a block asked for with `first` before the others. Blocks keep their index and, parsed, their table; a computed around the call runs again as they come. The frame showing a 25 KB, 385-block reply parses it in 0.25 ms instead of 0.94 ms. Streaming appends and short texts are as before. Rust hosts call `Incremental::set_text_lazy` and `parse_pending`.
- **Nine-slice images** (zgx, zgx-gpu, zgx-script): `Styled::image_slice(slice, widths)` (`Scene::set_image_slice`, `ImageSlice`), Luau `slice = { top, right, bottom, left }`: an image drawn in nine pieces (a nine-patch, CSS's `border-image` with `fill`). Its corners keep their size, its edges stretch along their side and its middle both ways, so one small image frames a button or a bubble at any size. Drawn as nine quads of the image's texture, each sampled half a texel inside its cut so pieces don't bleed into each other; widths past the box shrink together, as CSS's do. Images without a slice check one empty map.
- **A plugin reads the window's size** (zgx-script): `ui.window.size` is readable in a plugin's VM, read-only and with no grant, so a picture it opens fitted to the window (a lightbox) opens at the window's size, not 1 by 1. The rest of `ui.window` (zoom, visibility, the title bar's area and controls) stays the app's.
- **`ZGX_RELOAD=partial`: a hot reload runs again only what a save changed** (zgx-script, opt-in while it is proven on real apps): while a host watches its files, a save keeps the app's VM and runs again the modules it changed, what requires them, and the app's script; the other modules keep the values they returned and what their top levels made, and what a module running again made at its top level goes first (#1369). Everything loads again, as before, when keeping couldn't be right: plugins are loaded, a module running again sets a global or starts a task, animation, `on_open` handler, actions or router at its top level, a module kept declares a command, window, contribution, override or `on_mount` there, every module changed (a template swapped whole), or the last reload failed partway. A failed partial reload leaves the last load running as it was. `zgx inspect reloads` says which path each reload took and why; without the variable every reload loads everything and says what it would have kept. It lists the reloads a host asks for (`ScriptHost::reload`) too, a watched file whose bytes a reload already read no longer reloads the scripts again, and a global zgx makes on first read (`anime`) no longer counts as the module setting it.
- **Spans take context menus** (zgx, zgx-script): `ui.span { context_menu = … }` (`TextSpan::context_menu`) gives a run a menu of its own, on a link in place of Open Link and Copy Link; a secondary click inside the text selected shows the selection's menu (Copy, Select All), over a plain link's too, and Shift+F10 or the Menu key opens a focused link's. A `ui.rich` paragraph's `context_menu` is told the run it was asked on (`e.span`: `{ index, href, text }`; `View::context_menu_with_span` and `ContextMenuSpan` in Rust), and asked first on its links. A span's `on_click` returning `true` keeps its `href` from opening (`TextSpan::on_click_handled`). `zgx test` gains `app:right_click(target, { x, y }?)` and `app:opened()`.
- **Specular and index of refraction in 3D scenes** (zgx-3d): `World::set_specular(id, Some(Specular::new(1.).color(tint).ior(1.7)))` sets how much a dielectric mesh reflects, tinted how: as much facing it as its index of refraction says (water a little, diamond a lot), that and its grazing reflection scaled by its factor. glTF models bring `KHR_materials_specular`'s factor and colour (not yet its textures) and `KHR_materials_ior`'s index for any material, not glass's alone. A mesh reflecting otherwise than any does draws with a pipeline of its own; a world with none binds nothing new.
- **A web page's downloads are the app's to save** (zgx `WebView::set_download_handler`, `DownloadRequest`, `DownloadResponder`, `DownloadStatus`; Luau `ui.webview { on_download = function(d) … end }` with `d.url`, `d.name`, `d.mime`, `d:save_to(file)`, `d:ask()` and `d:cancel()`): each download a page starts (a `download` link, an attachment, a response it can't show) reaches the app, which saves it at a file the person chose, as through its save dialog, or not, and hears its progress and its end, on macOS (`WKDownload`), Windows (WebView2, its download bar hidden) and Linux (`WebKitDownload`). With no handler nothing is saved, as before: a page never saves into the person's folders without the app deciding.
- **`WebView::find`** (zgx, with `FindOptions` and `FindResult`, and `WebView::clear_find`): find text in a web page as a browser's find bar does, on macOS, Windows and Linux. The next match (or the previous) is selected and scrolled to, wrapping past the end, and the reply says whether there is one and, on Windows and Linux, how many. Asking again with the same text steps to the next match.
- **Drops into web pages on Linux** (zgx-desktop, `webkitgtk`): a page's own HTML drags now drop within it (its `dragstart` data, any types), zgx's dragged text drops on it, and files from other apps drop as `File`s with their bytes, each as the page's `dragenter`, `dragover`, `dragleave` and `drop` where the pointer is. Before, a page's own drag left it for zgx's, and nothing dropped on a page reached it.

### Performance
