- **3D frames find their pipelines by a variant's bits** (zgx-3d): what sets a mesh's pipeline apart is one 16-bit number, built, compared and hashed as one, where it was eleven flags; which of light passing through, a coat or a specular a mesh has is kept as one byte as they change; and a pipeline's shader is written from an index of the shader's code made once, instead of every byte of it read again. `3d-mixed` falls 1.5% (148 K instructions a frame, 0.66 M of setup), `3d-textured` a little.
- **A reflow with a screen reader running sends only the nodes near the window** (zgx-desktop): a node more than a window away, still on the side of it it was last sent on and otherwise unchanged, keeps the box it was sent with until it comes near; above stays above and below below, so a reader's order and the way it scrolls are the box's. Resizing a 3,000-row list with an assistive client attached: 3,032 → 178 nodes an update, AccessKit and its adapter 3.7 → 0.25 ms, zgx's projection 4.5 → 3.3 ms.
- **Accessibility updates hash with FxHash** (zgx-desktop): the projection's maps, keyed by node ids zgx hands out, hashed with SipHash, ~17% of each update. Resizing a 3,000-row list with an assistive client attached: 6.4 → 4.2 ms an update in zgx's projection (median of three runs each). Nothing changes with no assistive technology running.
- **A reflow with a screen reader running shapes only the text near the window** (zgx-desktop): each accessibility update shaped every label's text again for its text runs, on screen or not: resizing a 3,000-row list with an assistive client attached cost ~30 ms an update in zgx's projection. Text more than a screen away keeps its runs, shaped again as it comes near; text first seen there gets runs of its whole text placed by estimate, so a reader still gets every character (`AXStringForRange`) and a box near where it is. ~30 ms → 6.6 ms an update on that list; nothing changes with no assistive technology running.
- **A custom fill never freezes the frame that first draws it**: a `bg_shader`'s pipeline compiles on a thread of its own, from the moment `ui.shader` declares it. A window's frame that draws it before it's ready shows the box's colour and draws again once it is, instead of waiting. The first frame drawing Zeron's status ring took 117–151 ms on an M-series Mac (the frame waited for the compile); it takes 0.5 ms, and the ring follows about 150 ms later. Offscreen renderers (snapshots, tests) still wait, so their pixels stay deterministic.
- **The system font cache is checked as the app first goes idle, with no thread** (zgx-gpu): the check that a cached font file hasn't changed in place ran on a thread of its own two seconds after launch, and a process that has ever had a second thread locks glibc's allocator on every allocation. It now runs on the renderer's first trim (as the host's frames stop, no wake of its own), or after 600 frames for an app that never goes idle. On Linux, the headless lab stream no longer pays the locked allocator (about 12 M instructions).
- **A change a row or column's child keeps to itself lays out that child alone** (zgx): a text changing inside a child its row or column asked one size of (a fixed-width list's rows, a document's blocks) used to lay out the whole row or column and every ancestor; the child is now laid out again on its own and, if it answers as it did, its siblings stay as they are. A streaming Markdown append with 256 paragraphs above it costs 30% fewer instructions (7% with one), and `update_bench`'s rows updated in place 21% fewer. A change that does move its siblings lays out as before.
- **A Luau app's modules compile on a worker while its window is made** (zgx-script, zgx-run): `zgx-run` calls `zgx_script::compile_ahead(script, packages)` before the app's window, the GPU and AppKit are set up. A worker compiles the script and what it reaches by `require` with a string literal (`.luaurc` aliases and lent packages resolved as `require` does; no tests, type files or unused modules; files up to 1 MB). The first load's `require`s then find their bytecode compiled, rather than compiling each on the main thread. It is compiled in memory from the text and used only for the very bytes `require` reads; no bytecode is written to or read from a file, and nothing waits for the worker. What the load didn't use is let go as it ends. An app hosting its own `ScriptHost` calls `compile_ahead` itself, first thing in `main`. A 170-module app (466 KB): main-thread compile 7.3 → 0.5 ms, its build 15.7 → 7.9 ms (macOS, medians of 12), peak memory no higher. Hosts that don't call it pay one atomic load per `require`.
- **A Markdown view's appends look at its blocks without allocating** (zgx-script): each update of `ui.markdown_view` made a string of every block's kind to find where it is kept; it now reads the kind as it lies and keys each block by plain numbers. An append into a reply with 256 paragraphs above it costs 19% fewer instructions (8% with a 6 KB paragraph streaming), and its keys hold no part of the blocks.
- **Only the observed nodes that moved are told so** (zgx): each frame that moved anything gathered every observed node's bounds and visibility (a link's or an icon's in each paragraph) into a list to publish; it now compares each with what was last told and gathers those that changed. An append into a reply with 256 paragraphs costs 7% fewer instructions.
- **A repaint draws what its clips show, not every node** (zgx): the paint walk passes by children whose ink falls wholly outside the clip they're drawn in (a scroll view's rows out of view), on a full repaint as on a damaged one. Resizing a 3,000-row file list visits 144 nodes a frame instead of 4,715, and its paint phase falls 36% (7.43M to 4.72M instructions).
- **A streamed paragraph is prepared from the words of the one it grew from** (zgx-gpu): each append to a paragraph prepared from its words (#1292) found, looked up and packed every word again. A paragraph that starts with a recent one's text, in the same runs, now keeps that one's words and packed advances up to its last word after a space following a letter or digit. Only the text after it is read, its words looked up, shaped and packed. One word appended to a streaming Markdown reply's 6 KiB paragraph costs 415 K instructions instead of 1.44 M, and 194 K instead of 342 K at 96 bytes. Memory is unchanged, and the peak is lower.
- **A wrapping text keeps its lines while a window is dragged a few points** (zgx, zgx-gpu): a resize measured every wrapping text again at the new width, breaking its lines anew, though most rows had no break move. Text measured from its words now also reports the widths it holds for. From its widest line's width to where the next word would join a line, its lines break the same, and layout serves any width between from the size it has, without asking. A host's measurer gives them with `Scene::set_font_text_reach_measurer`, which `TextServices` and the renderer install. On a 3,000-row file list dragged 3 points a frame (`reflow_bench`), layout costs 33.0 M instructions a frame instead of 69.0 M, and text 0.13 ms instead of 1.90 ms.
- **A style re-run with the same result writes no paint** (zgx): a reactive style that sets no paint and ran again to the same styles (a box whose colour a moved selection reads, but which did not change colour) re-laid and re-wrote its unchanged effects, transform, clip and mask each time; after one such run it writes nothing, as a box changing only its size already did, and leaves what else set them since. `update_bench` function-prop, where 198 of 200 boxes keep their colour each update, falls 13.6% of its updates (26.8 M instructions in 200).

### Fixed

- **A clickable chip span is one pill, not underlined unless asked** (zgx, zgx-script):
  - **Underline:** a span with `on_click` was underlined even with `underline = false`, since false was taken as not given. `underline = false` now leaves a link not underlined at rest (`TextSpan::no_underline`), though a hover's underline still shows. It wins over an underline's style, colour or thickness too.
  - **One wash:** a span with an icon and a background was washed in three pieces: the icon's room, its text and the padding after it. Their backgrounds now join into one rounded wash per line. Spans side by side keep their own.
- **Chinese text on macOS 26 draws in PingFang** (zgx-gpu, zgx-cosmic-text): glyphs whose shapes are only in Apple's `hvgl` table, as macOS 26's PingFang keeps them, are drawn through Core Text into the glyph atlas, at the weight and subpixel position they were shaped at, so Chinese draws in the system's own Chinese face (and at Regular for regular text) rather than falling back to Heiti.
- **A scroll view stretched past its edge always springs back** (zgx, macOS trackpads): every stretched view springs back as a gesture ends, not only the last one a gesture took, so a list released past its end while another view takes the next gesture no longer stays shifted with empty space below it; a view whose content fits shows no stretch and lets go of any as the gesture ends. `zgx inspect node` gives a scroll view's offset within its range and any stretch (`scroll`), and `Ui::scroll_report` the same.
- **A fade over stacked pictures holds textures the size of its bands, and none at rest** (zgx-gpu): where `fade_edges` crosses stacked items, the renderer draws them unfaded into a band texture and fades it once. One texture covered every band, so a clip faded at its top and bottom took one the size of the whole clip (Zeron's wallpaper, a plugin's layered scene: a 1888×1600 texture, 11.5 MiB, reported as a layer on a node that isn't one), and the pool of band textures was never let go. Bands apart now each take a texture of their own size (1.9 MiB there), and the textures go as the window goes idle (`GpuRenderer::trim`).
- **A flex item its content held wide shrinks again when that content gets smaller** (zgx): in a row or column too small for its children, an item held at its content's least size (CSS's `min-width: auto`) now shrinks as soon as that size drops: when a child starts clipping, leaves the flow (`absolute`) or gets narrower. Before, the item kept its old width until something else laid out its parent.
- **An inset title bar keeps its strip when going into full screen fails** (zgx-desktop, macOS): the inset title bar's toolbar is hidden as a window goes into full screen, and a transition that failed after it began (AppKit tells only the window's delegate) left it hidden on a window that wasn't in full screen, its traffic lights no longer centred. Two seconds after a window begins going into full screen, one that isn't gets its toolbar back.
- **An inset title bar leaves no bar over the content in full screen** (zgx-desktop, macOS): AppKit moves a window's toolbar into a window of its own above it in full screen, which the title bar's transparency doesn't reach, so `Titlebar::Inset`'s empty toolbar showed as an opaque 40-point bar over the content's top. It is now hidden as the window goes into full screen (the green button or `set_fullscreen`) and shown again once it is out, where it centres the traffic lights.
- **An app's shader can't take its process down on Windows** (zgx-gpu): FXC, which compiles shaders for Direct3D 12 when the app ships no DXC, has overflowed every stack on some shaders, ending the process from any thread. zgx's own shaders are compiled under a gate; a shader fill or layer shader an app writes is now compiled by FXC in a process of its own (the app's executable, started again as a compiler) and handed to Direct3D 12 as bytecode, kept on disk so it compiles so once a machine. One FXC can't survive draws nothing, and the app goes on. Where the executable can't be started again, isn't zgx's (zgx in a DLL), or wgpu offers no passthrough shaders, it compiles in the process as before; `ZGX_FXC_IN_CHILD=0` keeps it there.
- **A GIF in `ui.image` shows** (zgx-gpu): its first frame, through the `gif` crate, where it was a runtime error ("The image format Gif is not supported"): a picture's file decoded through the `image` crate, which reads PNG and JPEG alone. Any `.gif` someone attached in Zeron.
- **A `ui.image` with a `fallback` keeps its `fit`** (zgx-script): its picture shows inside a switch (the picture or its fallback), and the image's styles went on the switch, so `fit`, `object_position` and `image_rendering` reached no picture: it drew as `fill` (a 4:1 picture covering a 56 by 40 box drew 56 by 40, not 160 by 40). The picture takes them itself now.
- **A cached layer draws again once a picture in it has decoded** (zgx-gpu): a layer (a layer shader's, an isolated group's) drawn while a picture in it was still decoding was kept as it was drawn, without the picture, until something inside changed style: Zeron's wallpaper under an effect (scanlines, dither) came up blank as its picture changed, until a zoom drew it again. A layer drawn without something on its way (a picture decoding, a shader fill compiling) is drawn again rather than reused.
- **`math.huge` as a list's offset is its end** (zgx, zgx-script): every kind of `ui.list` (fixed or measured rows, positional or keyed) now takes an offset past its end as the end, and `-math.huge` or NaN as the top, as `ui.scroll` does. Before, a list took any non-finite offset as its top. Setting a bound number to NaN no longer hangs the app: the state and the widget passed it back and forth for ever, since NaN never equals itself.
- **Tab and Shift+Tab go on through a list's rows, built or not** (zgx): Tab only knew the rows a `ui.list` had built, so past them it left the list (or wrapped round to the composer) halfway down a transcript. Now, at the last built row that way, the next row is scrolled to, built, and its first control (its last, with Shift+Tab) focused with the same key, rows with nothing to focus passed over, as a page's Tab goes on down a list it holds whole. A list whose rows are one Tab stop (`keyboard = true`) is left by Tab as before.
- **Look Up in a text area shown a line at a time**: `EditorHandle::look_up` asked for the baseline of a text the editor doesn't lay out (its lines are nodes of their own), and returned false. It now takes it from the line node holding the selection's start, as `text_area` takes its pieces.
- **A picture in Markdown that isn't alone in its paragraph is still a picture** (zgx-script): `ui.markdown` made a picture a block only when nothing came before it in its paragraph. Words before it ("Here it is:" then the picture), or another picture on the line before, turned it into its alt text and lost its source. A picture on a line of its own now splits its paragraph there into the words before, the `img` block and the words after, and leaves no empty paragraph behind. A picture among words is a run with `img`, its source, beside its alt text.
- **A context menu keeps the selection it opened on drawn**: right-clicking inside an editor's selection opened the menu, which took the focus, and the highlight went (only the window going to the back kept it). While a context menu opened on the editor (its own, or the app's on it or a view holding it) shows, the selection stays drawn in the colour of a selection in the back, as AppKit's does, and is drawn as before once the menu closes.
- **A modal opening closes the popups open outside it** (zgx, all platforms): a dropdown, menu or submenu left open stayed drawn over a `modal` (`ui.modal`) opened after it, and, where it overflowed the window, in a popup window above everything in the window (a ⌘K palette under an open dropdown). A modal opening now closes every anchored popup open outside it, in the window or in a window of its own, as AppKit closes menus and popovers when a sheet opens. Popups opened from inside the modal stay, and a `layer` (an overlay, not a dialog) closes nothing.
- **Nothing a scroll leaves behind stays on screen**: scroll content that repaints rather than copies when it moves (a layer of its own, such as a faded or isolated view) repaints what goes or is laid out again in it after the move, where it was drawn before the move. A node removed in such a frame (a reply's hover lane under a still pointer, in a measured list flung past it) was damaged only where the content had moved it, and its pixels stayed where they were drawn until something painted there again.
- **`Ui::modifiers` follows the modifier keys while a web page or terminal has focus** (zgx-desktop, macOS): AppKit sends `flagsChanged:` to the key window's first responder, so while a page or a terminal held the keyboard winit heard no modifier change, and `ui.modifiers` stayed as it was (Command held for shortcut hints never showed, or a key stayed held after it was released). A local event monitor, installed with the native views, now passes each change zgx's view doesn't hear to `Ui::modifiers`.
- **`image_rendering = "pixelated"` draws an image view's picture pixelated** (zgx): the setting landed on the image view's box, while its picture is a node inside it, which the renderer asked. Every `ui.image` drew smooth (Zeron's lightbox at 300%: a 1 px checker ramped 46 → 202 → 46). `image_rendering` is now inherited, as CSS's `image-rendering` is: a node's own setting, else the nearest one around it.
- **`i18n-plural` asks each locale for CLDR's plural cases in every build** (zgx-script): without the `i18n` feature (the `zgx` command, test hosts) the lint asked every locale for English's `one`, so a Chinese, Japanese, Korean, Thai or Vietnamese plural with `other` alone, which is complete, was reported (50 times in one catalog). It now reads ICU4X's categories for each catalog's locale whatever the features.
- **A debug build's lint findings never cover the interface** (zgx-script): they showed as the "Script error" card, a modal that took the pointer and hid what lay under it. They now count in a strip in the window's lower corner, never as an error nor over a modal, and a test host (`testing::TestApp`, or `ScriptHost::lint_strip(false)`) shows none.
- **A web view's diagnostics report a popup window's backdrop** (zgx-desktop, macOS): `NativeDiagnostics::backdrops` lists AppKit's material behind each translucent popup shown in a window of its own (a menu past its window's edge) as an `NSVisualEffectView` at the popup's box, so `web_view --fixture`'s menu backdrop check passes again; it now runs on every macOS CI job.
- **A tooltip's glass fades with its card** (zgx-desktop, macOS): since a node tooltip's translucent card gets AppKit's material under it, the material stayed at full strength while the card faded out (100 ms) and showed at once while it faded in: an empty frosted pane the card's size lingered as a hover card went. The material now takes the panel's opacity through what holds it (the tooltip's fade), and goes as it reaches 0.
- **A node tooltip's card is its popup's panel** (zgx-desktop, macOS): a popup window took the panel to be what its root holds, which for a node tooltip is its clear layer. A card with its own shadow then got the window server's shadow too, outlining the whole reach of the card's (a dark rim round empty space below it), and a translucent card got no material behind it. Through boxes that paint nothing and hold one thing, the card is now the panel: its own shadow alone, and AppKit's material under its translucent fill, cut to its corners, as a menu's.
- **Popup windows open at their trigger in a zoomed window** (zgx-desktop, macOS, Windows, Wayland): in a window zoomed off 100% (`Ui::set_zoom`), a menu or popover shown in a window of its own opened away from its anchor and 1/zoom too large, as its frame was built from the scene's points with no zoom. On macOS and Wayland its window's frame, size, material and arrow, its renderer's scale and the input coming back from it now go through the window's zoom; on Windows the screens it may open on do too (its frame and input already did; on X11 everything already did). A zoom an app sets before its window exists now reaches that window's popups as well. *On Wayland, a popup with a window of its own needs the repository's winit ([getting started](docs/getting-started.md)).*
- **`ui.prefers`'s states type-check when read** (zgx-script): they were typed `Readable<boolean>`, whose `get` takes `self: never` so that any state fits a bound property, which made `ui.prefers.reduced_motion:get()` an error in a strict script. They are now `ReadOnlyState<boolean>` (`get` and `peek`), as `ui.modifiers` is.
- **A plugin replaces the root of a presence's content** (zgx-script): `ui.contribute { target, replace }` did nothing, without an error, when its target was the root a `ui.presence` builder returns, at one level or nested; a child inside the branch was replaced. The root is now replaced where the branch builds it, each entry as it enters, leaving with it, as `before` and `after` there already were.
- **A reload with a modal open keeps where closing it gives focus back** (zgx, zgx-script): reloading a script while a modal or command palette is open, then closing it (Escape), now gives focus back to what had it before the modal opened, found again in what the reload built, the same way the focused node itself is found (its list row, else its role and name, else its place). Before, focus went nowhere: the modal's scope remembered the node the reload replaced.
- **A reload keeps focus where it was** (zgx, zgx-script): reloading a script built the interface again, and focus fell to its first Tab stop, so a focused `ui.list` row, or any control but the first, lost the keyboard on every save. What was focused is now found again once the new interface settles: in a list's row, the same row by its index (scrolled to if the list hasn't built it) and the same place in it, so one of many alike buttons (a "Delete" per row) keeps the right one; elsewhere the one node of its role and accessible name if it is the only one, else the node at its place. Rust views get it with `compose::switch_keeping_focus`.
- **A remote picture that fails is asked for again by itself** (zgx-script): after a failed fetch (a dropped connection, a busy server's 5xx), a `ui.image` showing it fetches it again once the backoff passes (5 s, doubling up to 5 minutes), for as long as a node shows it. The backoff only held back a fetch something asked for again, and nothing did: the picture stayed blank, or on its `fallback`, for the life of the node.
- **A span's padding that changes measures its paragraph again**: an inline chip whose padding followed a state kept the width it was first measured at (a padding change was taken for a colour change), so its line wrapped or left a gap.
- **A list in a box easing shut lays out** (zgx): a `ui.list` (or scroll view) of a set height in a column, in a box that clips while its height eases (a fold), never settled once its rows held a percentage height (`h_full`): the column's floor counted the list as nothing, so it squeezed the list, which built no rows, and the next pass all of them. A list's or scroll view's set height now counts toward its column's min-content height, as CSS counts a scroll container's: the column keeps the list's height and the box clips it, with or without percentages inside. What moves: a list or scroll view with its own height, in a column with no height of its own that is shrunk and holds percentage heights, used to be squeezed below its height and now keeps it, clipped by what clips above it (as it already did without percentages inside).
- **A terminal moved to another window keeps naming what runs in it** (zgx-terminal): when an engine-drawn terminal's session showed in another window (a tab dragged out, a view mounted elsewhere), its output followed it to that window's tasks, but a check of the process in front already due, and the app resolver's polling, stayed with the first window's. Once that window closed they ended with it, their pending flags left set, and the terminal never named its process or status again. They now move with the session.
- **Terminals opened at once all start on macOS** (zgx-terminal): macOS's `openpty` isn't safe to call from two threads at once while ptys are in use, and an engine-drawn terminal could fail to start ("Terminal · couldn't start: Unknown error: -6"), about once in 4,000 concurrent opens. Ptys are now opened one at a time. `zgx-alacritty-terminal` is 0.26.1.
- **A terminal program's first clipboard request is answered** (zgx-terminal): an engine-drawn terminal was given the app's platform (its clipboard) only after its session had started and been read once, so on a busy machine a program that copied to the clipboard or asked to read it (OSC 52) as it started could have its copy dropped or its question answered empty. The platform is now the terminal's before its session starts.
- **A test's timers keep its time** (zgx-script): under `zgx test` and `TestApp`, `ui.after` and `ui.every` fire as frames advance the test's time (1/60 s each), in order and each at its own time, never on the wall clock between two steps. A deferred `ui.after` in an app no longer lands mid-test on a slow machine. A wait (`TestApp::wait_until`) delivers a frame when one is asked for, else moves the test's time on by the time that passes. `ui.after(0, fn)` runs `fn` before the next frame is drawn, as it does in a window: in a test, before the frame that made it ends, or as the next one starts.
- **A token radius or border takes its single corners or sides, as a number does** (zgx, zgx-script): `rounded = "$app.r", rounded_tl = 0, rounded_tr = 0` painted all four corners at the token's radius, and `border = "$w", border_sides = { … }` every side at its width: the token resolved after the refinements and wrote over them. The token now rounds only the corners no single corner sets (and follows theme changes there), `rounded_corners` and `border_edges` replace a token radius or width as they replace a number, and padding and margin, which already did, are pinned by tests.
- **A layout that never settles fails a test, without a panic** (zgx-script): `zgx test` and `TestApp` panicked when layout didn't settle in 64 passes (a list squeezed in a box easing shut, say), taking the whole run down. They now contain it as a window does: the last layout is kept, and the error naming what still moved is the app's (`app:error()`, `TestApp::error`, the inspector), so the test fails on it and the rest of the run goes on.
- **A runaway script is stopped on time in a backgrounded app** (zgx-script): the watchdog that stops a script past its time budget woke from its timed wait as the system's coalesced timers allowed, and in a process macOS had put in the background (App Nap, a busy CI runner) that came 117 to 197 ms late, the script running on meanwhile. Its waits now end on a strict dispatch timer, which is not coalesced (within 2 ms there), and its thread runs at the interactive QoS; on Linux it takes no timer slack, on Windows the highest thread priority.
- **A Windows program's last output shows after it exits** (zgx-terminal): ConPTY renders what a program last wrote after the program has gone, and an engine-drawn terminal stopped reading at the exit, so a program's last lines were often lost: ping's statistics after Ctrl+C, a build's final error. The terminal now closes the pseudoconsole and reads its output to the end (five seconds at most) before reporting the exit. `zgx-alacritty-terminal` is 0.26.1. The output pipe now ends as the pseudoconsole closes (zgx no longer keeps its own copies of the console's ends of the pipes), so an exit is reported as soon as the last output is read.

## 0.1.8 (2026-11-02)

A fix for 0.1.7, which drew every Chinese character on macOS 26 as blank space: a font face whose shapes zgx can't draw (macOS 26's PingFang keeps them only in Apple's `hvgl` table) is now passed by in fallback, and Chinese takes Heiti SC or TC. Rust and Luau code builds as before, but for a struct literal of zgx's `WebViewOptions` or `WebViewState` (each has a new field), each below with what to change. Every published 0.1.7 crate builds against this release. Forks published with it: `zgx-cosmic-text` 0.19.8 and `zgx-mlua` 0.12.7. Behaviours change too, each below with what to check.

### Changed: what to check in an existing app

- **`WebViewOptions` has a new field, `accelerated`** (zgx): a `WebViewOptions` written out field by field stops compiling. *Migrate:* end it with `..Default::default()`, or set `accelerated: true` (the default).
- **`WebViewState` has a new field, `full_screen`** (zgx): a `WebViewState` written out field by field stops compiling. *Migrate:* end it with `..Default::default()`, or set `full_screen: false`.
- **A second `mount` in a test is the next launch** (zgx-script): a test's apps in `zgx test` share a storage directory of the test's own, each saved before the next starts, so a second `mount()` finds what the first kept (`ui.persist`, folders kept with `ui.open_folder { remember }`), as a relaunch would. Each test starts with nothing kept. A headless app's `ui.persist` kept state in memory only.
- **A web page on Linux saves no downloads** (zgx-desktop's `webkitgtk`): a `download` link or an attachment is cancelled as it starts, as on macOS and Windows, where web views save nothing without a handler of the app's. WebKitGTK saved them into the user's downloads folder unasked.
- **A test names an item's window by its key** (zgx-script): `app:window(key)` in `zgx test`, and `TestApp::window`, take the key of an item of `ui.windows` (its `id` field, or `key(item)`), whose window's own id a test can't know. A `ui.window`'s id still names it, and a key two lists share fails, saying so.

### Added

- **Clear coats in 3D scenes** (zgx-3d): `World::set_clearcoat(id, Some(Clearcoat::new(1.).roughness(0.1)))` lacquers a mesh, as car paint, varnish or a glaze: a thin coat of glass reflects the lights and surroundings over it, smooth or rough, and what is under it (light through glass included) shows the less the more the coat reflects. glTF models bring theirs: `KHR_materials_clearcoat`'s factor and roughness, and its textures (`Map::Clearcoat`, `Map::ClearcoatRoughness`, `Map::ClearcoatNormal`: how much coat, how rough, which way it faces, the last its own and not the surface's). A mesh with one draws with a pipeline of its own; a world with none binds nothing new.
- **Container queries** (zgx, zgx-script): `container = true` on a box and `queries = { { min_width = 900, style = { … } } }` on what's inside give styles that follow the box's content size, as CSS's `@container` rules, in the frame layout changes it and without running the script; a resize that crosses no bound restyles nothing. As in CSS, a container's content doesn't size its width (a debug build reports one that's 0 wide). In Rust, `View::container` and `View::when_container(Query::new().min_w(900.), …)`, and `Scene::set_contained`.
- **Targets by class in tests and inspect** (zgx-script): `.tool`, `.tool.primary` and chains such as `#bar >> .tool` find elements by their `class` in `zgx test`, `zgx inspect` and MCP, as plugin targets do: one selector language.
- **Gradient masks** (zgx, zgx-script): `Styled::mask_gradient(Gradient)` (`Scene::set_mask_gradient`), Luau `mask_gradient` and `mask_radial` written as `gradient` and `radial` are: a node and what it holds show through a linear or radial gradient's alpha over its box, at whatever size it's laid out (CSS's `mask-image` with a gradient), for a list fading out at its bottom or a vignette. The mask's image is made from the gradient at the box's size when it's laid out, and again when the box resizes; it then draws as an image mask does.
- **Gradient text** (zgx, zgx-gpu, zgx-script): `Styled::text_gradient(Gradient)` (`Scene::set_text_gradient`), Luau `text_gradient` and `text_radial`: a text's glyphs filled with a linear or radial gradient over its box (CSS's `background-clip: text`). The text paints into a cached layer of its own, where its glyphs draw, the gradient is kept where they cover (a source-in blend) and its selection, marks and shadows draw behind them (destination-over); selected glyphs then take the selected-text colour. The same shader as every quad, two more blend states (wgpu and Direct3D 11). Glyphs rasterize at the layer's scale, and the gradient's colours are made again only when the box resizes, so a counter re-uploads nothing but glyphs. Text without a gradient is no layer.
- **Devtools reach every window** (zgx-script, zgx-run): `zgx inspect windows` lists the app's windows (the main one, `ui.window`'s and `ui.windows`'s), each with its id, size, title and popups open. Any command takes `window` (`--window` on the CLI, `window` on every MCP tool, `zgx_windows` to list) and answers for that window: an id, or an item's key in `ui.windows`. zgx-run registers each window it opens while devtools run (`inspect::register_window`), and forgets it when it closes.
- **Plugins add beside a branch's root** (zgx-script): `before` and `after` at the root of a `ui.show`'s or `ui.switch`'s branch, or of a component, which matched nothing, are siblings in the parent's flow that come and go with the branch and read what the root provides, live. At the app's root or a window's root, which can't have siblings, they are an error in `ScriptHost::contributions` and to the plugin.
- **What each plugin contribution does now** (zgx-script): `ScriptHost::contributions()` lists each `ui.contribute { target }` and `ui.filter` with its plugin, target, place, how many elements it applies at now (0 when its target matches nothing shown) and its latest error. `zgx inspect contributions` and the MCP tool `zgx_contributions` give the same, as text and JSON.
- **A plugin command's dropped shortcut says so** (zgx-script): when a plugin's command declares a shortcut the app's commands (or an earlier plugin's) hold, spelled the same or not, `ScriptHost::dropped_shortcuts` records the key, why ("taken by the app") and the command that holds it, and `ScriptHost::contributions` (`zgx inspect contributions`) lists it as place `shortcut`, for the app to tell the plugin's author.
- **Where a plugin can put something and what it can read there** (zgx-script): `ScriptHost::elements` lists the app's ids, classes and hooks grouped by each element that `provide`s context, with a selector that reaches each group and the context names it provides and inherits. A list's repeated rows are one group with a count, private elements show nothing inside, and names the source declares that aren't showing are listed `mounted: false`. `zgx inspect elements` and the MCP tool `zgx_elements` give it as text and JSON.
- **A host lends a package to plugins** (zgx-script): `Capability::Package("ds")` lends package `ds` to a plugin, and `ScriptHost::grant_all_plugins` grants a capability to every plugin, those loaded later too. zgx.json allows `"package:<name>"`. The plugin requires `@ds/…` into its own VM, with module state of its own. A lent module requires only inside lent packages, never `@app/…`, and an error names the missing grant. What it calls is gated by the plugin's grants. An edit to the package reloads its plugins, and `contributions()` lists what each plugin was lent. `zgx check` resolves a plugin's lent packages, and `zgx types` adds their aliases to the plugin's `.luaurc`.
- **Plugins add beside a presence's content** (zgx, zgx-script): what a plugin puts before or after a `ui.presence`'s content root enters with it, and as the content leaves it stays where it lay until the exit animation ends, then goes in the same frame. A contents node whose last child lays out in its place (`display_contents`) now lays out flat too, with what it holds before that child kept over its place (#1275).
- **Plugins add beside a list's rows** (zgx-script): `ui.contribute { target, before | after | replace }` and `ui.filter` at the root of a `ui.each` or `ui.list` row, which drew nothing, apply a row each: what is added lays out as the list's own items, keyed with its row (it follows a reorder and goes with a removal), and reads what the row's root provides, live.
- **Plugin targets by class and inside other elements** (zgx-script): `ui.contribute { target }` and `ui.filter` take `.class` (every element with it) and descendant chains, `#sidebar >> .session-row`, in the selector syntax tests use. A target matching several elements contributes at each, and a chain only where its ancestors hold, including inside a list's rows. `zgx lint` warns of an id, hook or class in a target, a chain's parts too, that no element of the app declares; under strict types an app's classes are targets (`HookTarget`) and a chain is any text (`HookChain`).
- **Text streams into an editor where it ends** (zgx, zgx-script):
  - `TextEditor::apply_external(range, text)` and `append_external(text)` take an edit made outside the editor (a model's reply, a collaborator) as `set_text` takes the document it makes, same undo and redo, comparing only the edit; the caret at the end follows the stream, and a selection keeps what it selected.
  - An editor bound to a long text follows the edits the text keeps (`zgx::text::Text` remembers its last 16), applying each where it is.
  - A `\r` and a `\n` streamed in different tokens make one line break, not two.
  - Luau's `state:append(text)` (and `append(path, text)`) adds to a long string in place, keeping the edit for a bound editor, and a string growing past 64 KiB becomes one; `zgx lint` flags `state:set(state:get() .. token)` (`append-concat`).
- **Text says the language it is in with `lang`** (zgx, zgx-desktop, zgx-script): HTML's attribute, as `Styled::lang`, `TextSpan::lang`, and Luau's `lang = "zh-TW"` on any element or span. It is inherited by what the element holds. Its Han characters take that language's fonts, ahead of the app's locale and of what a line's kana or hangul say, so a Chinese quote in a Japanese app is drawn in a Chinese face. Screen readers are given it as the text's language. The fork's fallback search keeps simplified, traditional and Hong Kong Chinese lists whatever the locale.
- **`View::not_contents`** (zgx): undoes `contents`, so a region built by code that set it can be put in a flow of the host's choosing (with `display_contents`, its children in its parent's).
- **`WebViewOptions::accelerated`** (Luau `accelerated`; Linux only, macOS and Windows ignore it): on by default. Off, WebKitGTK paints the page on the CPU and zgx copies and uploads only what each frame changed, as up to four rectangles, instead of the whole page WebKit's compositor reports on every frame. A 40 × 40 spinner on a 1076 × 700 page uploads about 3,300 pixels a frame instead of 753,200, and WebKit's web process drops from 40–52% of a core to 3–9%. Painted pages draw no `backdrop-filter` and flatten 3D transforms.
- **A web page's full screen** (zgx `WebViewState::full_screen`, `WebView::exit_full_screen`; Luau `web.full_screen`, `web:exit_full_screen()`): a page's `requestFullscreen` is told to the app, tracked like the page's other state, on every platform. On macOS it now works, in WebKit's own window over the screen (WKWebView's element full screen was off, so pages' requests failed); on Windows and Linux the element fills the page's node, which an app can grow, or make its window full screen, to show it so.
- **A web page's context menu on Linux** (zgx-desktop's `webkitgtk`): a right click shows WebKit's menu for what's under the pointer, a link (open, open in a new window, download, copy its address), an image, media, a selection or field (copy, cut, paste, select all), the page (back, forward, reload, Inspect Element), as a zgx menu where the click was. It showed none: GTK's own would open off the page. Spelling, font and media-control items are left out.
- **Drags out of a web page on Linux** (zgx-desktop's `webkitgtk`): a page's link, image or selected text drags as zgx's own drag. It drops on zgx's targets as `DraggedText` (the selection, or the address), and out of the window on other apps over XDND or Wayland's data device: the address, the text, and an image's bytes from WebKit's own copy, so a page behind a login drags its images too. A selection goes as plain text.
- **`WebView::print`** (zgx): the platform's print dialog for a web page, as the page's own `window.print()` shows it. On macOS both are now the print panel as a sheet on the window (the page's `window.print()` did nothing); on Windows WebView2's print preview; on Linux GTK's print dialog, now transient for the app's window on X11 (it opened wherever the window manager put it, owned by no window it knew).

### Performance

- **The core benchmarks are cheaper than in 0.1.7**, all but the lab's stream (below) (exact instruction counts at the release on Linux arm64): `micro-state` −36.5%, the lab's scroll −25.9%, heavy scrolling −16.6%, the other micro benchmarks −17 to −31%, the heavy streams −2.7%, animation −4.1%, mount −1.6% and `3d-mixed` −2.2%. Updating a script's state costs 3.98% less, measured with the same benchmark on both sides (the gate's −27.4% counts the benchmark's own setup at 0.1.7 only, since #1321). Script layout, `anime`, `style`, `list-state` and `spans-follow` are flat (−0.11% to +0.02%).
- **Every example app's interactions cost less**, from −0.12% (`app-mail`) to −3.79% (`app-code`).
- **The lab's stream costs 0.37% more**: Linux's system font cache (#1307) makes startup 9.3 M instructions cheaper, but the thread that checks it makes glibc lock its allocator in a process that otherwise has one thread, as this headless benchmark does (about +12 M). Apps with a window already have threads. The next release fixes it.
- **3D shadows find their pipelines by one byte** (zgx-3d): a frame drawing shadows looked up each run's shadow pipeline by a tuple of flags through the standard library's SipHash; it now uses one byte under the variants' own hasher. `3d-mixed`, which draws shadows, falls 2.2% (257 K instructions a frame).
- **`display = "contents"` in a column or row costs what a box does** (zgx): its parent lays its children out as its own with zgx's own column and row layout, not taffy, unless they need taffy as its own children would. 500 rows in a column: 6.0 M instructions to mount and 0.54 M each relayout, against 9.2 M and 1.2 M before (a box: 5.9 M and 0.30 M).
- **A long text keeps its edits only for a reader that follows them** (zgx): every edit of a long text (a keystroke in an editor on a long document) recorded itself in the text's log, read or not; a text now records edits only once a reader that follows it by them asks (`Text::keep_edits`: an editor bound to it, when the value changes from outside, and `ui.markdown_view`), and otherwise only takes a new id. A stream into a bound editor takes its first token whole, then each one as it is.
- **A streaming Markdown paragraph is mended from where it got to** (zgx-script): while a block streams, the hanging-marker repair (`**bold` shown closed) scanned the whole block into a vector of chars on every append; it now keeps its scan and reads only the bytes appended. An append into a 6 KB paragraph costs 17–20% fewer instructions (13% with 256 paragraphs above it) and its peak heap is up to 23% lower; the repairs are the same, checked against a fresh scan of every prefix.
- **Linux reads its system fonts from a cache** (zgx-gpu), as macOS and Windows do. At every launch fontdb parsed fontconfig's configuration and opened every installed font. With 113 fonts that cost 11.7 M instructions; from the cache it costs 0.46 M (-96%). The cache lives in `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/zgx/` (or `~/.cache/zgx/`). It is used while the configuration files fontconfig read, the font directories they name and the environment fontdb reads (`HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `FONTCONFIG_FILE`) are unchanged, and it keeps the generic families fontconfig's aliases chose. A launch that finds them changed loads the fonts as before and writes the cache again, once. The cache file's format is now 4 on every platform, so each rebuilds its cache once.
- **Variable-height lists pay for keeping their place only when they load earlier pages** (zgx): the key of the row a list holds in view, kept so rows added above it leave the view where it was (`on_start`), is no longer carried by every list's scroll and layout pass.
- **A Markdown view follows a long reply by its appends** (zgx, zgx-script): `ui.markdown_view { "{reply}" }` reads the state's text where it lies, rather than copying it into a string each update; a long reply streamed in with `reply:append(token)` (Rust: the new `Shared::append`) is parsed from the inserts its text keeps (the new `Text::id` and `Text::inserts_since`), with no copy and no comparison of the document. With 256 paragraphs, an update costs 2.4% less when the reply is `set` whole (at the same or lower peak heap) and 6.7% less when it is appended to (the long text keeps its last edits, about 4 KB). Any other edit reads the text whole, as before.
- **A parent's new percentage bases read its children's cached bits** (zgx): when a taffy-laid-out parent's bases change, it reads each child's percentage flags from the bits cached with what the child reads (`OWN_PERCENT_*`) instead of working them out from its styles each time. x86: widgets −0.08%, mount −0.06%, micro-state and the scroll scenes −0.05%, app-chat −0.04%, every cell ≤0. arm64: mount −178K instructions, micro-vscroll-variable −78K, heavy-stream −40K, app-chat −29K, app-mail −20K (#1276).
- **A hot reload compiles only the files that changed, and a save that changes nothing reloads nothing** (zgx-script): every module was compiled again on each reload. A hot-reloading host now keeps each file's bytecode by what the file held, for the files of the last load alone, so a long session's edits don't grow it; every module still runs again, as before. A watcher woken for files whose bytes are what is loaded (an editor saving the same text, a checkout touching times) no longer reloads, so the app keeps its state. In an app of 170 modules (466 KB of Luau) a reload after one module changed takes 6.5 ms where it took 14 ms, and an unchanged save 0.15 ms where it reloaded in 14 ms. A host that loads once keeps nothing. Compiled files are shared by every host and VM on a thread, by their bytes, so the same text compiles once wherever it loads.
- **A row built again allocates and copies less** (zgx, zgx-script): a view's children are given exactly as many slots as there are (a growing vector reserved four, 2.5 KB, for a container of one); a script element's static styles, its children's flow and its wrappers no longer move the 632-byte view in and out on the way; a `ui.each` row's identity is one allocation and its key is compared as it is written; shared state republished builds each changed list or record once, not as a vector copied. `update_bench` rows, whose changed rows are built again, fall 2.1% of their updates (9.6 M instructions in 200), rows updated in place 0.9%.
- **A script element's style keys are found by their atoms** (zgx-script): each property key a Luau table names (`w`, `bg`, `text_size`) is now known by the atom Luau keeps in the key string, given once per string, instead of through a map keyed by the string's heap address. A lookup costs the same whatever the heap's layout (an unrelated change no longer moves it) and less: `fill_from` −20%, micro-vscroll-variable-luau −0.48%, script-update −0.14%.
- **A table a script returns reaches Rust without a copy of its type check** (zgx-mlua 0.12.7, zgx-script): a code editor's highlighter result, a row builder's and every other table result is referenced as it lies on the stack. app-code and app-code-search run about 150K fewer instructions, script-update 562K.
- **Script state finds and compares table keys without calling libc** (zgx-script): a table of a few fields is scanned for an equal key, and keys and short strings compare with a few word loads. A republished list matched by `id` costs 0.7% less an update (script-update), and its cost no longer moves with where memcmp's bytes land from build to build (#1273).
- **A terminal reads streaming output once a refresh** (zgx-terminal): an engine-drawn terminal read its session and worked its rows out again for every chunk of output, about a thousand a second where Windows' ConPTY hands output over in small chunks, though no more than one frame a refresh shows. Output arriving faster than the display refreshes is now read at the next refresh, all of it at once; output after a pause (a key's echo) is still read as it arrives. `NodeHandle::refresh_interval` gives the display's refresh period to code like it (zgx).
- **A text area shapes only the line an edit changed, at any length** (zgx): a text area under 64 KiB was laid out whole on every change. Its caret layout shaped every line again, so a token streamed into a 16 KB reply cost 20.5 M instructions (1.16 ms) in a shaped window. A text area of more than one line is now laid out a line at a time, as long documents were (one line is shaped whole either way). A change's text is derived from the last one by a one-chunk edit, and a bound text's line-ending check reads it a block at a time. A token now costs 96 K instructions at 16 KB and 82 K at 1 KB (3.7 M before), in line with 73 K at 128 KB. Shown a line at a time, a text area keeps its placeholder, its ranges for input methods (`EditorHandle::text_area`) and the geometry assistive technology reads of each character (`Semantics::set_text_lines`). A line height under a point is kept as it is, no longer rounded up to one.
- **A text that changes to one measuring the same is repainted, not laid out again** (zgx): a counter or a clock (a text that doesn't wrap, as long as before) is measured as it changes, and if it measures as before nothing around it is laid out again. arm64: micro-state −20.1M instructions, script-update −16.6M, micro-state-luau −8.3M, app-dashboard −2.2M; i18n_bench's frame 87 → 34 µs (#1288).

### Fixed

- **Chinese text draws on macOS 26 again** (zgx-cosmic-text, zgx-gpu): a font face with no outlines zgx can draw, such as macOS 26's PingFang (its shapes only in Apple's `hvgl` table), is passed by in fallback, and Chinese takes Heiti SC or TC instead. In 0.1.7 every Chinese character on macOS 26 drew as blank space.
- **An editor's undo and redo don't break after an app answers an undo** (zgx): a document loaded (`load_next`) as an answer (`answer_next`) to an undo kept the step that redid the text before it, and an answer as long as the text kept the steps before the undone one. Undoing or redoing them then broke the text, or panicked out of range or inside a character. A load now leaves nothing to undo or redo, and an answer to an undo nothing older to undo. Controlled fields that answer what is typed, as Zeron's do, reach these paths.
- **Apps on different zgx versions keep a system font cache each** (zgx-gpu, macOS and Windows). The cache was one file, so two apps whose zgx read different formats each rewrote what the other had written, and both loaded every font at every launch. The file is now named for its format (`system-fonts-v3`). Another format's file, or one left half-written, is removed once it has gone unchanged for 30 days; an app keeps its own file marked as in use.
- **Text measured without a renderer no longer keeps every word it shaped** (zgx-gpu, zgx-cosmic-text 0.19.8). A renderer trims cosmic-text's word cache as it draws. Headless hosts, tests and servers measure through `TextServices`, and nothing trimmed theirs, so it grew without bound (about 2.7 KB a frame for 30 churning labels). The text cache now sweeps the word cache as it grows, letting go of words unused lately, so it levels off (30 labels churned through 450,000 values: about 49,000 words, where untrimmed it reaches 105,000 and climbs). `TextServices::release_unused` empties prepared text and words at once, for a host returning to its baseline after a batch. `TextServices::text_cache` returns its cache, as a renderer's does.
- **A long text streamed into gives back its edits once the stream stops** (zgx, zgx-script): the last edits a long text keeps for its readers (an editor or `ui.markdown_view` following a reply by its appends) stayed for as long as it did; they now go as the host goes idle (`Text::forget_edits`), so a reply streamed in holds what the same text set whole does. Appending to a short string copies it once rather than twice, so `state:append` and `Shared::append` never cost more than setting the joined text.
- **A keystroke in a long bound document no longer reads all of it** (zgx). Whether the bound text needed its line breaks normalized was found by scanning every chunk, on every keystroke. A text now counts its carriage returns where it is made and in the chunks an edit makes again, so the check is constant time: a keystroke on a 100,000-line file costs 1.8 M instructions, down from 5.3 M. A short bound text now takes each edit where it is, rather than a new copy of the whole text, and the editor doesn't compare the value it has just written.
- **A new paragraph is prepared from its words** (zgx-gpu). A paragraph that cosmic-text shapes as one left-to-right span, plain or rich, is now measured and laid out from its words. Each word is shaped once and shared by every text that holds it. Before, every new text built a cosmic-text line around its cached words, then copied their glyphs and laid them out again. Glyphs, positions and sizes are cosmic-text's, bit for bit. text_bench (prepared) measures 1,000 new paragraphs in 5 ms instead of 19, and streams one in 8.5 ms instead of 32. A label whose text changes every frame costs 13.5 K instructions to measure, not 23.7 K; wrapped, 38 K, not 89 K. Lines with tabs, right-to-left text, hyphenation, letter spacing, fallback families or a word across runs take the old path.
- **A Japanese or Korean line's min-content is measured in its language** (zgx-gpu). Measured from its one-word-a-line copy, as some lines are, a word of kanji alone lost the line's kana and took the system language's Chinese face: 11.23 px where the line gives 11.0. The copy now takes the line's language.
- **A touchpad scroll comes to rest on a whole device pixel on Linux** (zgx-desktop): a two-finger scroll and the coast after the fingers lift could leave what they moved resting between pixels (a canvas view at x 86.502), its text and hairlines blurred, where the wheel's scroll eases in whole pixels. Its last step now brings the scroll's whole motion to a whole number of device pixels, at the window's scale and zoom, by under half of one: no visible jump, and fractions still while it moves.
- **A drag let go as an earlier drop finishes drops on Wayland** (zgx-desktop): once a drop is performed, the window's own drag ends there, taken, and its next drag goes to the compositor at once, while the earlier drop's target is still finishing with it; that drop's end, coming later, is no longer taken for the newer drag's. A tab grabbed straight after a drop moves to another window or tears off as one grabbed later does ([#1259](https://github.com/zeronsh/zgx/issues/1259)).
- **A web page's file input opens the window's file dialog on macOS and Linux** (zgx-desktop): `<input type=file>` asks through `WebView::choose_files`, the window's own file dialogs (a sheet on macOS; the desktop portal's, or zenity's, on Linux), and what's chosen goes to the page, several files where the input takes several and a folder for `webkitdirectory` on macOS. On macOS it opened nothing; on Linux GTK's chooser opened as a window of its own, apart from the app's.
- **A web page's cursor on Linux is the one it sets, as it sets it** (zgx-desktop's `webkitgtk`): the cursor was read just after each pointer event, before WebKit's web process had placed the pointer, so it showed the previous one until the next move (a link's hand, after stopping on it, stayed an arrow); and an image cursor (`cursor: url(…)`) showed as the arrow. The window now follows the cursor WebKit sets as it sets it, and shows a page's image cursor itself.
- **A web page's `alert`, `confirm` and `prompt` show on macOS** (zgx-desktop): each is an alert sheet on the page's window, with the page's origin under its message and a field for `prompt`, and the page's script waits for its answer. They returned at once before: `alert` showed nothing, `confirm` was false and `prompt` null.
- **A web page's copy reaches other apps whole on Wayland** (zgx-desktop's `webkitgtk`): a selection the page copies goes out with its HTML, so it pastes formatted elsewhere, and an image it copies (its clipboard API, a canvas) as a PNG. It went out as text alone, and an image not at all. The clipboard is now also read after any key, not only one with Control or Shift, as a page's own key handler can copy.
- **A script on Windows isn't stopped short of its time budget** (zgx-script): Windows counts a thread's CPU time a scheduler tick (15.6 ms) at a time, so a call that used 25 ms could read as two ticks, 31.25 ms, and be stopped under a 30 ms budget it hadn't spent. A budget's CPU time now counts as spent only a tick beyond it there; a runaway script stops up to that much later.

## 0.1.7 (2026-11-01)

A patch: Rust and Luau code builds as before, but for a struct literal of `PaintExtras` or of zgx-desktop's `WindowOptions` (each has new fields) and code naming `switches::ALL`'s length, each below with what to change. Every published 0.1.6 crate builds against this release. `zgx-terminal` is new: terminals on every platform, through zgx-desktop's `terminal` feature. Forks published with it: `zgx-alacritty-terminal` 0.26.0 and `zgx-zune-jpeg` 0.5.15 (new), `zgx-cosmic-text` 0.19.7 and `zgx-mlua` 0.12.6. Behaviours change too, each below with what to check.

### Changed: what to check in an existing app

- **A covered window stops drawing on Windows** (zgx-desktop): as on macOS and X11, a window nothing of which can be seen, or on another virtual desktop, does no drawing, and `ui.window.shown` is false there; on the way its menus and popovers close, as they do when it is minimized. A window that may show through (translucent, click-through, acrylic) never counts as covering it. Automated tests that need a covered window to go on drawing set `ZGX_RENDER_WHEN_COVERED=1`. *Needs the repository's winit ([getting started](docs/getting-started.md)).*
- **A selection is one rounded shape** (zgx): text selections, in selectable text and editors, draw lines that touch as one continuous shape, with rounded outer corners and curved inner ones, even in a translucent colour and at fractional scales. `$selection.radius` is now 3, not 0; set it to 0 for plain rectangles. An empty line in a selection shows as a sliver. An editor's selection is one node carrying a highlight, not a rectangle a line, so code that walked an editor's nodes for them finds it through `Scene::text_highlight`.
- **`zgx inspect` and `zgx test` act only on what shows** (zgx-script): a closed popover's, menu's or modal's content stays in the scene, unseen, and `click`, `hover`, `right-click`, `drag`, `scroll`, `snapshot` and `wait` took it as if it showed, landing on whatever was under it. They now fail with `doesn't show … (open what holds it first)`; `find` and `tree` still list it, marked `hidden`. A test that clicked something closed now fails where it used to pass.
- **zgx-terminal's screen model is zgx's fork, `zgx-alacritty-terminal`** (zgx-terminal): `alacritty_terminal` 0.26.0 without its comparisons of `usize` and `i32` with its grid indices from the primitive's side. Those impls reach every crate linking it, where an inferred comparison (`range.contains(i)` with `i: &&usize`) stopped compiling: an app turning on zgx-desktop's `terminal` feature would have found code of its own broken. Its default `serde` feature is off.
- **A value the app sets on a `ui.input` or `ui.textarea` leaves nothing to undo** (zgx-script), as setting an HTML field's value does: ⌘Z after showing another note could write the old note over it, and `on_change` would save that. The field's own edits still undo. A value answering one keeps the history: with `value = function`, the function's next change after `on_change` or `on_commit`, however late; with `value = state`, a different value within 100 ms of the edit. Rust's bindings keep their history (`TextEditor::load_next` asks for this).
- **An effect's run owns what it makes** (zgx, zgx-script): a `ui.on_cleanup`, `ui.after` or `ui.every` made in a `ui.effect` now goes before the effect runs again, and with the effect, as the docs said and a React effect's cleanup does. Before, a run's cleanups and timers went to whatever was being built at the time, or to the script until it reloaded: content a `ui.presence` swapped out kept its effects' cleanups, and an effect's cleanups didn't run between its runs. A `ui.computed` run owns what it makes until its next run (start timers in an effect or a handler). `Runtime::own` and `Runtime::running` let a host give a resource to the running effect.
- **Edge fades ease in** (zgx-gpu): `fade` / `fade_sides` (`Styled::fade_edges`) bands follow smoothstep rather than `t²`. `t²` reached full opacity still rising, and the band's inner end showed as a line; smoothstep is level at both ends. Every fade looks a little different: content stays nearer full opacity further into the band and falls more steeply in its middle. Before and after: `docs/results/fade-smoothstep-2026-10-31/`.
- **The renderer going on with less stays out of release banners** (zgx, zgx-desktop, zgx-script): a layer drawn coarser, an image softer, a glyph left out, a frame skipped after a GPU error or a layout that didn't settle showed "Something went wrong", sometimes in another window's banner. They are logged once as `degraded:`, in their own window's banner in debug builds only. `zgx::issues::IssueKind`, `on_issue_kind` and `report_kind` tell them apart; `ScriptHost::on_issue` and `issues()` give apps and `zgx inspect errors` the kind. An app's `on_error` still hears them.
- **Pictures keep their files, not their pixels** (zgx, zgx-gpu, zgx-script): a script's `src` image is read for its size alone, decoded off the main thread when drawn, shrunk to the size it shows, and its pixels given back once uploaded (1.5 s on). A page of eight large photos: footprint 662 → 294 MB. A picture over 256 by 256 pixels shows a frame later than before; one up to that size (an icon) decodes as it is first drawn, in that frame, and `decode = "sync"` (HTML's `decoding`, `ImageData::set_decoding`) makes any picture wait rather than pop in. The image cache's budget counts files' bytes now; `ImageData::pixels` still gives all the pixels (decoded once and kept), and `pixels_at`, `keeps_file`, `bytes`, `set_decoding`, `zgx::image::Decoding` and `zgx::image::release_decoded` are new.
- **`monospace` is Menlo on macOS** (zgx-gpu): every app's monospaced text now has a face of its own on macOS, as on Windows (Cascadia Mono or Consolas) and Linux. cosmic-text named Noto Sans Mono, which macOS lacks, so each word took the fixed-width face with most of its characters; Latin text already came out in Menlo. A word mixing Latin with characters Menlo lacks (Chinese, Japanese, Hebrew) now takes its Latin letters from Menlo and the rest from another face, where it could take all of them from one.
- **`zgx_3d::POINT_LIGHTS` is 8** (zgx-3d, #1123), the point lights a frame shades (the nearest the camera's target); it was 4. Its spelling is the same, but an array sized by it now holds eight, and a scene with five to eight point lights now shades them all. *Migrate:* size by the constant, not by 4.
- **`zgx_script::Window` and `driver::Line` keep 0.1.6's fields** (zgx-script): code building them with a struct literal compiles as before. A window's `under_pointer`, `visible` and own `state` are read with `ScriptHost::window_extras(id)` (a `WindowExtras`, `non_exhaustive` with `Default`, beside `window_size_limits`), and whether a tree line's node shows with `driver::tree_lines`, whose `TreeLine` holds the `Line` and its `hidden` flag; `tree` and `tree_text` are as in 0.1.6.
- **`TabsOptions` keeps the fields 0.1.6 published** (zgx-components): `reorderable` and `on_close`, as published zgx-script 0.1.6 builds it by literal. What tabs gained since (`keep`, `group`, `on_move`, `on_tear_off`) is in `TabsMore`, made from `Default` and its fields set, and passed with `tabs_with(label, items, selected, options, more, panel)`. `tabs` is as it was.
- **A tab can stay home when torn off** (zgx-components, zgx-script): `on_tear_off` returns whether it takes the tab. `false` keeps it in `items`, its preview sliding home, as an app's "All" tab needs; Luau's returning nothing still tears it off. `TabsMore::on_tear_off`'s `TearOffTab` now returns a `bool`.
- **A terminal is a document named Terminal to assistive technology** (zgx-terminal): its rows were children of an unnamed container that AT-SPI, UIA and AX drop, so they read as the window's own lines. They now sit in a document named Terminal (AT-SPI `document frame`, macOS `AXDocument`).
- **Screen readers hear a terminal's new output, not each keystroke's line** (zgx-terminal): an engine-drawn terminal's rows are text labels (they were images), and new output is a live region of its own saying the lines output brought in or what a line gained; before, the whole terminal was the live region and a reader re-read the prompt's line with every character the shell echoed. Checked with Orca on Linux and the AX tree on macOS.
- **A test's clicks are counted as a person's** (zgx-script): `zgx test`'s and `TestApp`'s presses come with the keys held, as a window's do, so `on_press`'s `clicks` and a double click count in a test as they do in a window; separate `app:click` calls in a row still count as separate clicks (`InputDispatcher::forget_clicks`).
- **A test or devtools action says what is in its way** (zgx-script): `click`, `double_click`, `type`, `fill`, `hover` and `drag` in `zgx test`, and `zgx inspect`'s actions, fail on a target outside its scroll's clip naming the clip ("scroll it into view first"), and on one under another node naming that node and where, rather than acting on whatever was there. Input's `InputDispatcher::pointer_target` is public: what a press at a point lands on.
- **A field with nothing to undo hands ⌘Z on** (zgx), and ⌘⇧Z or Ctrl+Y with nothing to redo, to the app's commands, as AppKit's responder chain does: a `ui.command { key = "cmd+z" }` reaches the app's own undo from inside a field without `reserved = true`. A field with a step still takes the key.
- **`WindowOptions` has new fields, `max_size` and `visible`** (zgx-desktop): a `WindowOptions` written out field by field stops compiling. *Migrate:* end it with `..Default::default()`, or set `max_size: None` and `visible: true`.
- **Wheels and drags in a zoomed box move it as far as the hand** (zgx, zgx-script): a scroll view's offset, `on_wheel`'s `dx` and `dy`, and `anime.createDraggable`'s position took the window's points, so under `zoom = 0.5` content moved half as far as the wheel or pointer did. They are in the box's own points now. A drag's `grab_x`, `grab_y` are from where the source shows. An app that scaled these for its own zoom now scales twice. docs/reference/events.md says which points each field is in.
- **`ui.span` reads the options its table holds** (zgx-script): one walk over them, rather than a lookup for each of twenty names, makes a plain span's script cost about 40% less. A span's table is read as a plain table, as an element's is: options behind a metatable's `__index` are no longer seen.
- **Backspace follows the platform's text fields** (zgx): it took a whole grapheme everywhere, so a mistyped tone mark in Thai or vowel sign in Devanagari took the syllable with it. In Hebrew, Arabic, Indic and Southeast Asian scripts it now takes one code point, as every browser and `NSTextView` do. An emoji, a flag, a keycap and a CRLF go whole. Where platforms differ it follows the native field: on macOS an accent, a dakuten or Hangul jamo goes with its letter, as in `NSTextView` and Safari; elsewhere one code point goes, as in Chromium. The caret and forward delete still move by grapheme.
- **`ui.select`, `ui.combobox` and `ui.date_picker` are the box they are given** (zgx-components): `id` and sizes reached a wrapper the trigger sat in at its own width, or (combobox, date picker) the closed popup, so `#id` found nothing and `w` sized the panel. Now `id` finds the control, `w` and `grow` size it, and in a `ui.column` it fills across as an `input` does. `w` or the parent's `items = "start"` narrows one that used to hug its text. Rust's `select`, `combobox` and `date_picker` too.
- **A script's root fills its parent across** (zgx-script), as a block does: `return ui.column { … }` was as wide as what it held. Its `bg` now reaches the edge, and `items` and `justify` work across the whole width. Height is unchanged: it takes what it holds, and `h_full` asks for the rest. A root with a `w` keeps it, and embedded in a Rust view it fills the box it is given. Scripts updating under such a root cost about 6% less: their rows are no longer measured twice.
- **A `ui.list`'s rows fill its width** (zgx): a row's root shrank to what it held, where a block fills the row. Its `bg` and borders now reach the list's edge, and what `grow` or `justify` spread goes across the whole row. A row with a width of its own (`w`) keeps it.
- **A scroll view or list under `ui.show`, `ui.switch` or `ui.presence` fills its parent** (zgx): it stayed at its default 320 points (240 tall for a `ui.scroll`), where on its own it stretches, and what lay past that was clipped and took no click or drop. Check a view that looked right at 320: give it a `w`, or a `max_w`, to keep that width.
- **`ui.each` rows stretch as any child does** (zgx-script): a row of `each` shrank to what it held, where the same node on its own fills its parent (columns' and rows' `items` defaults to `"stretch"`). A row's `bg` now reaches the edge, and what `grow` spreads goes across. In a `ui.row`, a child of `each` likewise takes the row's height, as a plain child does. A child with a `w` or `h`, or a parent with `items`, keeps what it said; one that relied on shrinking wants `self = "start"`.
- **`PaintExtras` has a new field, `outer_mask`** (zgx), for a fade inside a fade: a `PaintExtras` written out field by field stops compiling. *Migrate:* end the literal with `..Default::default()`, or set `outer_mask: None`.
- **`zgx::switches::ALL` is `[&Switch; 4]`** (zgx), with `BAKED_LOOPS`; it was `[&Switch; 3]`, so code naming that length stops compiling. *Migrate:* iterate it, or take a slice (`&zgx::switches::ALL[..]`), and don't name its length.
- **`local_x` and `local_y` read through zooms and transforms** (zgx-script, zgx), the node's own included, as CSS's `offsetX`: they were the window's point less the laid-out corner, so anything zoomed or moved by a transform read wrong. A script that corrected for its own zoom now corrects twice, and one that reads them on a transformed node gets the point in its untransformed box. `ContextMenuEvent::local_x` and `local_y` are the same.
- **A bare `perspective` on a node with none holds from the first frame** (zgx-script): `anime` tweened it up from 0, past the eye. `perspective = { from, to }` still tweens.
- **Frames past a budget no longer fail** (zgx-gpu): `GpuRenderer::render` returns `Ok` where it returned an error for layers, images, native surfaces, glyphs and shadows past their limits; it draws what it can, and `GpuRenderer::take_issues` says what it went without. A host that drew through `render` itself should hand those on.

### Added

- **`selection_radius`** (zgx, zgx-script): a selectable region's highlight corners, beside `selection_color`, over the `$selection.radius` token; a state or function follows.
- **glTF lights and cameras; spot lights, and lights that follow objects, in 3D scenes**: a model's `KHR_lights_punctual` lights (suns, bulbs and spots, glTF's units as zgx-3d's) are added with it, each following its node, and `Placed::lights()` gives them; `Placed::cameras(&world)` gives its cameras where its nodes are now, for `World::set_camera`. `World::set_light_parent(light, Some(id))` places any light in an object's frame, and `World::set_spot(light, Some(Spot::new(direction, inner, outer)))` shines a point light as a cone. A frame now shades with the eight point lights nearest the camera's target, where it took four. A world using none of these binds nothing new.
- **Widths from what an element holds** (zgx, zgx-script): `w`, `min_w` and `max_w` take `"min-content"`, `"max-content"`, `"fit-content"` and `"fit-content(320px)"` (`.w_calc("max-content".parse()?)`), as CSS's: a badge as wide as its text in a column, with no `on_resize`.
- **A host can ask a capability of every plugin contribution** (zgx-script): `ScriptHost::require_contribution_capability(capability)` makes adding into a slot (`ui.contribute(name, node)`) or before or after an element (by `#id` or a hook) take that capability, so an app can lock plugins out of its interface. A target or slot the host opens with `expose_hook(target, None)` (`"slot:name"` for a slot) stays open. Without it, contributions are open, as before.
- **`display = "contents"`** (zgx, zgx-script): an element's children lay out in its place, as its parent's own (CSS `display: contents`), and `ui.each(items, row, { display = "contents" })` makes its rows a grid's cells or a row's items rather than one box. Focus order and assistive technology keep the element where it is, with a role or label it has. In Rust, `.display_contents()` and `Scene::set_display_contents`.
- **Dotted borders, and dashed and dotted outlines** (zgx, zgx-gpu, zgx-script): `BorderStyle::DOTTED` (a `Dashed` of length 0) draws round dots as wide as the border, as far apart, spread evenly round the box and its rounded corners; Luau `border_style = "dotted"`. `Styled::outline_style` (and `Scene::set_outline_style`) draws an outline dashed or dotted, as CSS's `outline-style`; Luau `outline_style = "dotted"` and `outline_dash = { length, gap }`. A solid outline is the shader's ring as before; a broken one is rasterized once and kept. A canvas stroke's dash may now be 0 long (a dot, with round caps), as an HTML canvas's `setLineDash` takes.
- **`on_double_click`** (zgx-script), as the DOM's `dblclick`, and `app:double_click(target)` in `zgx test` (`TestApp::double_click`).
- **Direct3D 12 compiles with DXC where the app ships it** (zgx-gpu): with Microsoft's `dxcompiler.dll` beside the executable (14.3 MB; `dxil.dll` isn't needed), Windows shaders compile with DXC instead of FXC, and with FXC if that fails. A cold window's first text came sooner (437 ms against 776 on an RTX 4090), and DXC's output is kept in the shader cache as FXC's is. Nothing changes for an app that doesn't ship the DLLs ([shipping](docs/windows.md#shipping-a-zgx-app-on-windows)).
- **A `ui.textarea` scrolls by line and says where it is** (zgx, zgx-script): `ui.editor.scroll_to_line(el, n, { align })` puts a line at the top, middle or bottom of the view, and `on_top_line` hears the first line in view: an outline can jump to a heading and follow the text (`EditorHandle::scroll_to_line`, `View::on_top_line`).
- **A field's undo and text position from a script** (zgx, zgx-script): `ui.editor.undo(el)`, `redo`, `can_undo` and `can_redo`, so an app can make one undo stack across panes, and `ui.editor.offset_at(el, x, y)`, the byte under a point, so a drop goes where the pointer is (`EditorHandle::undo`, `redo`, `offset_at`).
- **A folder's files can be shown and handed on** (zgx-script): `folder:file(path)`, and `folder:list(path, { files = true })`'s entries, give a `File` that `ui.image`, `ui.read_file` and `drag_export` take as they do a dialog's, so a browser shows thumbnails and drags files out to Finder. A plugin with the grant can show the files it was given.
- **A folder can be kept past the run** (zgx-script, zgx, zgx-desktop): `ui.open_folder { remember = "library" }` asks the host to keep the grant under that key, and `ui.remembered_folder("library")` settles with the handle on a later launch if the folder still resolves, or nil. `folder:forget()` drops it. The host keeps it where no script reads, in its storage's `.folders`: on macOS a security-scoped bookmark, which works sandboxed or not and follows the folder moved or renamed, elsewhere its path. Plugins keep none, and `ScriptHost::refuse_remembered_folders()` keeps none. `Platform` gains `bookmark_folder`, `resolve_folder` and `release_folder`, with defaults that keep a path.
- **Glass in 3D scenes** (zgx-3d): `World::set_transmission(id, Some(Transmission::new(1.).ior(1.5).volume(thickness, color, distance)))` lets light through a mesh. What is behind it shows through, bent by its index of refraction, blurred by its roughness, tinted by its colour, and in a volume dimmed by how far the light goes. glTF models bring theirs: `KHR_materials_transmission`, `KHR_materials_ior` and `KHR_materials_volume`, their factors and their transmission and thickness textures (`Map::Transmission`, `Map::Thickness`). A world with glass draws its other meshes a second time a frame into a picture of what is behind; a world with none draws nothing more, binds nothing new, and draws its frames slightly faster than before (`3d-textured` −0.5%, `3d-mixed` −0.8%).
- **Grid areas, named lines and dense flow** (zgx, zgx-script): `areas = { "head head", "side main" }` names a grid's cells and `area = "main"` places an item in one (CSS `grid-template-areas`, `grid-area`). `[name]` in `cols` and `rows` names a line, `grid_column` and `grid_row` take CSS's `"side / main"` and `"2 / span 3"`, and `flow = "dense"` fills holes. In Rust: `grid_template_areas`, `grid_area`, `grid_column`, `grid_row`, `grid_flow`.
- **Windows made hidden, shown fully drawn** (zgx-desktop, zgx-script, zgx-run): `WindowOptions { visible: false, .. }`, `ui.window { visible = false }` (or a state) and zgx.json's `"visible": false` make a window hidden, its content laid out, its script running and its first frame drawn meanwhile; hidden, it draws nothing more. `WindowHandle::set_visible(true)`, or `ui.window.visible:set(true)` for zgx-run's window, draws what changed since before it appears, so it shows whole at once, with no blank frame. On Wayland, where a window appears as it first draws, its first frame is drawn as it shows; on X11 a window keeps no picture while unmapped, so its theme's background (not black) shows for the few milliseconds between its mapping and its first frame.
- **Clicks land where an animation is going** (zgx, zgx-script): `hit = "target"` hits an element, and what it holds, where its `ui.tween`, `ui.spring`, `anime` or layout animation is taking it, not where it shows: clicking fast on a stack that re-fans after each removal removes the photo under the pointer each time. Assistive technology reads it at its goal too. In Rust, `Scene::set_hit_target` gives any node a `HitTarget`; `Scene::hit_bounds` is where it takes hits. Also: a node removed with size math's content widths no longer leaves them behind.
- **Hyphenation** (zgx, zgx-gpu, zgx-script): words break at their hyphenation points, a hyphen ending the line, as CSS's `hyphens: auto`, in the system's language, from typst's `hypher` (48 languages). In 0.1 it's a font feature, inherited as features are: `FontFeatures::with_hyphens(true)` in Rust, `font_features = "hyphens"` in Luau; `hyphens = "auto"` of its own waits for 0.2.
