It's behind a `hyphens` feature (zgx-desktop's), off by default, about 1.2 MB, read in place; without it, Luau's `"hyphens"` is an error where scripts are linted (a debug build, `zgx lint`). The text is unchanged: selection, carets and copying see the word whole. zgx-cosmic-text is 0.19.5.
- **Pixelated images** (zgx, zgx-gpu, zgx-script): `Styled::image_rendering(ImageRendering::Pixelated)` (`Scene::set_image_rendering`), Luau `image_rendering = "pixelated"`, as CSS's `image-rendering`: an image, or a background image, drawn larger or smaller takes each point's nearest pixel, its edges hard, for pixel art, a magnified screenshot or a QR code. Its draw samples with a nearest sampler of its own (wgpu a bind group, Metal pipelines, Direct3D 11 a sampler state, each made with the first): the shaders are as they were, and an image not pixelated draws exactly as before, in as many draws.
- **An app's main window reopens where it was left** (zgx-run): `"frame_autosave": true` in `zgx.json` saves the window's frame under the app's `app_id`, so each app zgx-run runs keeps its own, and a string saves it under that name, as `ui.window { frame_autosave }` does for an app's other windows. `zgx.json` rejected the key.
- **`TextEditor::move_visually_at`** (zgx) moves left or right as the text shows it from a caret place (`TextPosition`, with its affinity), stopping on each side where bidirectional runs meet, and returns the place it went to draw the caret there. `move_visually`, 0.1.6's form, keeps its signature and moves from the caret's byte.
- **`ui.open_folder`** (zgx, zgx-script): a folder a person picks, as a handle only the dialog makes, which lists, reads and writes inside that folder and nowhere else: `..`, absolute paths and links out of it are refused, it works off the main thread, and a write replaces its file whole. A plugin needs the `folders` grant, and a Rust host can refuse it to every script (`ScriptHost::refuse_folders`, `Capability::Folders`, `Platform::open_folder`).
- **The primary selection through `Platform`** (zgx, zgx-desktop): `primary_text` and `set_primary_text` read and set X11's and Wayland's primary selection (what a middle click pastes; none elsewhere). An engine-drawn terminal's selection becomes it as the drag ends, and a middle click there pastes it, as Linux terminals do.
- **Find's marks and an editor's decorations are rounded as a selection is** (zgx): each match is one shape over the lines it runs on, rounded by the new `$mark.radius` token, which follows `$selection.radius` unless a theme sets it. `Scene::set_text_mark_shapes` sets marks so; `set_text_marks` keeps its look. An editor's decorations are marks on one node of their own, not a rectangle node each.
- **Lint `list-row-get`** (zgx-script): `state:get()` straight in a positional `ui.list`'s `row` builder, which is read once per index and goes stale as the list changes; `data` with a `key` makes each row follow its item.
- **Any element can be a slider to screen readers** (zgx, zgx-desktop, zgx-script): Luau's `role = "slider"` on a column, row, div or overlay, with `value`, `min`, `max`, `step` and `value_text` (all followed where they can change), `on_change(value)`, and `keyboard = true`. In Rust: `View::role(Role::Slider)`, `numeric_when` and `value_when`, plus the new `View::adjustable(step, on_change)` and `adjustable_keys`. VoiceOver, Narrator and Orca read the value, its range, its step and its value text ("High", not "3"), and increment, decrement and set it through `on_change`. With `keyboard`, the arrows, Page Up and Down, Home and End move it as `ui.slider`'s do, and it becomes focusable. `ui.slider` now tells screen readers its step and takes `value_text` too. On Linux, the value text is AT-SPI 2.46's `Value.Text` in builds from this repository, which patch in accesskit_unix. A build from crates.io, with upstream accesskit_unix, gives Orca the number, range and step without the text.
- **Autocorrect and smart punctuation, opt-in per field** (zgx, zgx-desktop, zgx-script): `View::autocorrect` (Luau `autocorrect = true`) corrects a misspelled word as it ends and expands the person's text replacements ("omw" to "On my way!"); `View::smart_punctuation` (`smart_punctuation = true`) makes straight quotes curly and `--` a dash as they are typed. Both go through the system's own services (macOS's `NSSpellChecker`, in the forms the person chose), only where the person has them on in System Settings, and never in a password field. Each substitution is an undo step of its own: undo puts back what was typed, and it stays. Off by default; other platforms substitute nothing yet (`Platform::substitutions`, `Platform::correction`).
- **Tabs move between strips** (zgx-components, zgx-script): `ui.tabs { group = "panes", reorderable = true }`, a tab dragged over another strip or panel of the group goes there, as in an editor's split panes; `on_move` hears it (`TabsMore::group`, `on_move`, through `tabs_with`).
- **Tabs tear off into windows** (zgx-components, zgx-script): with `TabsMore::on_tear_off` (Luau `ui.tabs { on_tear_off }`), a reorderable tab dragged off its strip is carried as a drag, its chip under the pointer. Dropped on a strip of its group in any of the app's windows, it goes there and `on_move` is told. Dropped back on its own strip, it stays. Released outside every window, it leaves `items` and `on_tear_off` is told its id and where it was grabbed, to open it in a window of its own (`ui.windows`, `under_pointer`).
- **An app answers a terminal program's request to read the clipboard** (zgx, zgx-terminal, zgx-desktop, zgx-script): `Terminal::on_clipboard_read` (Luau: `ui.term`'s `on_clipboard_read`) gets each OSC 52 read as a `ClipboardRead` to `allow()` or `deny()`, at once or after a prompt the app shows; a request let go is denied. Without one, Ghostty still asks with its own dialog and the engine-drawn terminal denies, now with an empty answer the program can read rather than none.
- **Terminals on Windows and Linux** (zgx-desktop's `terminal` feature, on in zgx-run): `zgx::terminal::terminal` and Luau's `ui.terminal` draw a session with zgx itself on every platform: a pseudoterminal (openpty, ConPTY) and its screen, in the window's own text, each row drawn again only when it changes. Colours to true colour, wide and emoji cells, cursor shapes, the alternate screen, scrollback, selection and copy, bracketed paste, mouse reporting, OSC 8 links, input methods at the cursor, and the session's title, directory, progress, bells, exit and foreground process (an app's resolver included) as with Ghostty. An idle terminal does no work. On macOS, `ghostty` still draws them when it is on. What it can't do yet (images) is in docs/native-surfaces.md.
- **The kitty keyboard protocol in engine-drawn terminals** (zgx-terminal): a program that asks (`CSI > flags u`: Neovim, Claude Code and other agents) gets its keys as the protocol encodes them: Ctrl+C as `CSI 99;5u` rather than ^C, Escape as `CSI 27u`, repeats and releases, shifted and base keys, every key as an escape with its text, and its flags answer `CSI ? u`.
- **Terminal sessions on every platform** (zgx-terminal, new): a shell or any command on a pseudoterminal (openpty on Linux and macOS, ConPTY on Windows) and the screen it draws, kept by `alacritty_terminal`: colours and true colour, wide characters, scrollback, the alternate screen, synchronized output. `Session` does its I/O on a thread of its own that sleeps until the program or the app writes, and wakes the app once until it reads, so a flood of output costs one update per read and an idle shell nothing. Titles, the working directory (OSC 7), bells, progress (OSC 9;4), finished commands (OSC 133), notifications (OSC 9/777), clipboard requests (OSC 52), the foreground process and exit codes come back as events. The engine-drawn terminal view builds on it.
- **`app:copy_file(from, name)` in `zgx test`** (zgx-script): a fixture from the app's directory, an image or a folder of them, copied into the test's own files for a dialog to answer with; `write_file` takes text only.
- **A test acts in the app's other windows** (zgx-script): `app:window(id)` in `zgx test` is a window the app declares (`ui.window`, or one of `ui.windows`) with an app's methods, nil while it is closed. It shares the app's state, tasks and frames as a desktop app's windows do, and `close()` closes it as a person does. In Rust, `TestApp::window(id)` mounts one as a `TestApp` of its own.
- **Balanced text: `wrap = "balance"`, `Styled::text_balance`** (zgx, zgx-script): wrapped text breaks at the narrowest width that keeps as many lines, so a heading's last line isn't left short, as CSS's `text-wrap: balance` does, for up to six lines. Inherited, as CSS's is; boxes and line counts are as they were. `Scene::set_text_balance` sets it, and `Scene::text_wrap` is what renderers read.
- **Han characters take the app's language's fonts** (zgx, zgx-gpu, zgx-desktop, zgx-script): a script with `ui.i18n` sets the windows' text language to its locale, as CSS's `lang` on a document does, so a Japanese locale's kanji draw in a Japanese face and `zh-TW`'s in a traditional Chinese one, whatever the system's language. Rust apps call `WindowFactory::set_text_language`; `Platform::set_text_language` carries it, and `FontSystem::set_language` in the fork applies it. A Chinese, Japanese or Korean language set takes precedence over a line's own kana or hangul. A system locale such as `ja-JP` now gives Japanese faces too (the fallback lists named only `ja`).
- **Text shadows** (zgx, zgx-script): `Styled::text_shadow(x, y, blur, color)` and `text_shadows`, Luau `text_shadow = { x, y, blur, color }` or a list, as CSS's `text-shadow`: the glyphs again under them, moved and tinted, inherited by the text inside. `Scene::set_text_shadows` for scenes built directly. `blur` blurs it (a Gaussian's sigma in points): each glyph's coverage is blurred once and kept in the glyph atlas, so a blurred shadow costs a frame what a sharp one does, on every backend. Shadows paint outside the text's box, as CSS's do (a text itself stays clipped to its box).
- **A window an item, from Luau** (zgx-script, zgx-run): `ui.windows(list, build, { key, on_close })` opens a window as an item appears in a list and closes it as the item goes, as `ui.each` does rows: a detail window per photo, a tab torn off into a window. A person closing one calls `on_close(item)`. `under_pointer` opens a window under the grab, on `ui.window` too.
- **Screen readers read web pages on Linux** (zgx-desktop's `webkitgtk`): a page's accessible tree, which WebKit serves from its own process, is the window's too, under the page's box (an internal frame), with the box for its parent, so Orca follows focus into the page and reads it: its focused field, its headings by its browse mode's heading keys. GTK's own window for the page says nothing to a reader, and GTK here names WebKitGTK as its toolkit, so Orca keeps its web script (and its browse mode) for the page. `NodeHandle::touch_semantics` (and `Semantics::touch`) has assistive technology read a node anew when what a host adds to it changes; a `gpu_view` with an empty label is none of assistive technology's.
- **Tab into a web page on Linux focuses its first field** (zgx-desktop's `webkitgtk`): Tab or Shift+Tab reaching a page's box focuses the page's first or last focusable element, as tabbing into a browser's frame does, and a screen reader names it ("Probe field entry.") rather than reading what it holds.
- **Web views on Linux** (zgx-desktop's `webkitgtk` feature, which zgx-run turns on): `zgx::web_view` and `ui.webview` show WebKitGTK 4.1 pages on X11 and Wayland, drawn offscreen and shown as images, so they clip, round, fade and take zgx content and glass over them as any node does. Pointer, keys (reserved shortcuts first, keys the page leaves alone falling through to the app's), input methods, `<select>` popups (as zgx menus), the clipboard (bridged through zgx's on Wayland), navigation, scripts, messages, app schemes, profiles and the inspector work as on macOS. WebKitGTK is opened at run time (`libwebkit2gtk-4.1-0`): building needs none of it, and without it pages stay placeholders. Two CPU copies a frame, and no drags out of a page yet: see docs/linux.md.
- **A window's largest size** (zgx-desktop, zgx-script, zgx-run): `WindowOptions::max_size` and `WindowHandle::set_max_inner_size`, beside `min_size`; zgx.json's `max_width` and `max_height`; and `ui.window { min_width, min_height, max_width, max_height }`. The same minimum and maximum make a window of fixed size that stays resizable to the system, so window managers such as Raycast can move it on macOS, which they refuse for one that isn't resizable.
- **`ui.window_rect(el)`** (zgx-script): where an element shows in the window, through every zoom and transform around it, as `{ x, y, width, height }` in window points (CSS's `getBoundingClientRect`). A ref's `x`, `y`, `width` and `height` stay as laid out.
- **A window reads its own state** (zgx-script, zgx-run): `ui.windows`' `build(item, win)`, and `ui.window` content given as `function(win)`, get `win.active`, `win.shown` and `win.size`, that window's, where `ui.window.active` is the main window's: a command can act on the window in front.
- **Size math** (zgx, zgx-script): `w`, `h` and their limits take `calc(100% - 24px)`, `min(…)`, `max(…)` and `clamp(…)` (`.w_calc("…".parse()?)` in Rust), worked out from the containing block as CSS's are, with no `on_resize` writing state.
- **A `ui.list` row taller than `height` is a layout warning** (zgx): it ran over the next row silently.
- **Spans follow state, and take any font** (zgx-script): a span's `color`, `bg`, `text_size`, `weight`, `bold`, `italic`, `underline`, `strike` and `font` take a state or a function, as its text does, so a run restyles without rebuilding its paragraph's node or losing a selection in it, and `font` takes what `ui.text`'s does ([text](docs/reference/text.md)).
- **Sticky elements in scroll views** (zgx, zgx-script): `.sticky_top(n)` and `sticky_top = n` hold an element `n` below the top of the `ui.scroll` around it as it scrolls past, inside its parent, as CSS's `position: sticky` with `top`: section headers, with no script running as it scrolls. Vertical scroll views only.
- **Vertex colours in 3D scenes**: `Geometry::with_colors(colors)` gives each vertex a colour, multiplying its material's and its base colour map's across each triangle. glTF models bring theirs (`COLOR_0`: red, green and blue, with alpha or not, in floats or normalized integers), and a `BLEND` material whose alpha is only in its vertices draws translucent. A geometry without them binds nothing new.
- **Where a map sits on a 3D mesh**: `World::set_map_placement(id, Map::BaseColour, Some(MapPlacement::new().scaled(4., 4.).wrap(Wrap::Mirror, Wrap::Repeat)))` moves, scales and turns each of a mesh's maps on its own, says how it repeats, and with `.uv_set(1)` reads the coordinates `Geometry::with_second_uvs` gave it. glTF models bring theirs (`KHR_texture_transform`, samplers' `wrapS` and `wrapT`, `texCoord` and `TEXCOORD_1`). A mesh's maps share one sampler, its first map's wrap. A mesh using none binds nothing new; a wrap alone only takes another sampler.
- **OpenType features in Luau** (zgx-script): `font_features = "ss01 cv02=3 liga=0"`, as CSS's `font-feature-settings`, on any text or what holds it, followed from a state or function ([properties](docs/reference/properties.md)).
- **`z`** (zgx, zgx-script): `.z(n)` and `z = n` paint and hit a node over its siblings with a lower `z`, for a raised card or a badge. Among siblings only, unlike CSS's `z-index`: there are no stacking contexts. Layout, Tab order and accessibility keep document order, as in CSS ([stacking](docs/reference/properties.md#stacking)).
- **Double-sided and cut-out meshes in 3D scenes**: `World::set_double_sided(id, true)` draws a mesh's back faces too, and `World::set_alpha_cutoff(id, Some(0.5))` cuts it out by its base colour texture's alpha, in its shadow as well (leaves, hair, fences). glTF models bring theirs (`doubleSided`, `alphaMode: MASK` and `alphaCutoff`). A `BLEND` material whose alpha is only in its texture is now translucent, where it drew solid. A mesh using neither binds nothing new.
- **`zgx test` reads more** (zgx-script): `app:text` gives a field's value, `app:selection` its selection, `app:tree` takes a target, and `~"part"` targets what a label or text holds, anywhere in it (in `zgx inspect` too). `ui.combobox`'s `items` may be a plain list.
- **`ui.tabs { keep = true }`** (zgx-components, zgx-script): a tab's panel stays mounted once chosen, hidden, until its tab closes, so an editor keeps its undo, caret and scroll across a tab switch (`TabsMore::keep`, through `tabs_with`).
- **Grid tracks of any size** (zgx, zgx-script): `cols` and `rows` take a list as CSS's `grid-template-columns` writes it, besides a number of equal tracks: `cols = "220 1fr auto"`, `minmax()`, `fit-content()`, `repeat()`, and `cols = "repeat(auto-fill, minmax(240, 1fr))"`, which reflows cards with the window and runs no script. In Rust, `.grid_template_columns(tracks)` and `.grid_template_rows(tracks)` with `zgx::grid_tracks::GridTracks`. What CSS rejects is an error saying why ([grid tracks](docs/reference/properties.md#grid-tracks)).

- **Morph targets in 3D scenes**: `Geometry::with_morph_targets(vec![MorphTarget::new(positions).with_normals(normals)])` gives a mesh shapes to blend toward, and `World::set_morph_weights(id, &weights)` (or `set_morph_weight`, one target) how much of each, on the GPU, before any skin; its shadow and picking follow. glTF models bring their targets (positions and normals), their mesh's or node's weights, and clips moving the weights, so `ui.model { animation = … }` plays them. A target in a sparse accessor isn't read yet: that mesh shows unmorphed. A scene without targets, or with every weight 0, binds nothing new.
- **glTF sparse accessors**: any of a model's accessors may be sparse: its positions, normals, texture coordinates, skins, indices and animation keys, over a base or over zeros. Blender exports its morph targets this way, so those models morph now.
- **Morph targets in 3D scenes**: `Geometry::with_morph_targets(vec![MorphTarget::new(positions).with_normals(normals)])` gives a mesh shapes to blend toward, and `World::set_morph_weights(id, &weights)` (or `set_morph_weight`, one target) how much of each, on the GPU, before any skin; its shadow and picking follow. glTF models bring their targets (positions and normals), their mesh's or node's weights, and clips moving the weights, so `ui.model { animation = … }` plays them. A scene without targets, or with every weight 0, binds nothing new.
- **Editors hear a paste before it goes in** (zgx, zgx-script): `ui.textarea { on_paste = function(e) … end }` (`View::on_paste`), as HTML's `paste` event, from a key, the Edit menu, the context menu or a middle click. `e.text` is the clipboard's, `nil` for an image, `e.files` the files copied in Finder, Explorer or a file manager (`Platform::clipboard_files`), readable as `ui.open_files`'s are, and returning `true` takes the paste: a composer can make a pasted picture, a file or a long text an attachment. Hosts paste through `Ui::paste_into` ([controls](docs/reference/controls.md)).
- **`zgx new --template plugins`** (zgx-script): tasks made to be extended. Its `zgx.json` exposes shared state, an action the script defines with `ui.actions`, and a list to filter. `plugins/focus` is allowed them, adds beside the app's hook, filters the list and calls the action. Its test mounts the app with its plugin, as `zgx run` does.

- **Plugins in a Luau app, from zgx.json** (zgx-script, zgx-run): `expose` is what the app offers plugins (shared state read only or written, actions, targets to replace or filter), and each plugin's `allow` is what it's granted (`shared:<key>`, `action:<name>`, `override:<target>`, `fetch` and the rest). A grant beyond what's exposed is an error naming the plugin and the grant. `zgx run`, `zgx test` and `zgx audit` load them alike (`zgx_script::app_config`). `ui.actions { name = function(arg) … end }` defines the app's own actions, called as a Rust host's are (`ui.invoke`, `ui.action`). A plugin's `ui.shared` default now gives way to the app's, since plugins load first ([plugins](docs/plugins.md#plugins-in-a-luau-app-zgxjson)).

- **3D camera controls as a model viewer's**: `zgx_3d::OrbitControls` answers a view's input: drag to turn, right- or middle- or Shift-drag (and Shift-scroll) to pan, scroll or pinch to zoom, and, focused, arrow keys to turn (pan with Shift), `+` and `-` to zoom and Home to come back; a flick coasts to a stop (`coast`, a frame at a time while it coasts), not with reduced motion. `Orbit::pan` moves its target. `ui.scene3d { orbit = true }` uses it (`orbit = { pan = false }` turns panning off), focusable, in Rust with no script per frame; with reduced motion on, flicks stop where let go and `auto_rotate` holds still. A `camera.target` from a state now moves the orbit's target only as it changes, so a pan stays.
- **3D anti-aliasing you can turn off, and smoother highlights**: `World::set_samples(1 | 4)` (in Luau `ui.scene3d { antialias = false }`): 4 samples a pixel by default as before, 1 for hard edges at a quarter of the colour and depth memory. Multisampled scenes now also widen a surface's roughness where its normal turns faster than the pixels show (geometric specular anti-aliasing, Tokuyoshi and Kaplanyan), so highlights on fine curves blur instead of flickering: about 1% more GPU work (scene3d demo 42.7–43.1 → 42.8–43.3 Mcycles/s), none at one sample.
- **Editors' highlight colours follow a theme switch, and a highlighter's state is any value** (zgx-script): a `highlight` span's `"$token"` colour is resolved again when the theme's tokens change (`ThemeScope::revision`, now public), at no cost while they don't. What a line leaves the next can be any Luau value (a string, a boolean, a table), not only a number: the same string or number is the same state, a table the same one only as itself ([controls](docs/reference/controls.md)).
- **`read_only` on inputs and text areas** (zgx-script): `ui.textarea { read_only = locked }` (a boolean, a state or a function, as `View::read_only_when`) keeps the field selectable and focusable and refuses the person's edits; `value` still sets it ([controls](docs/reference/controls.md)).
- **`ui.window.size`** (zgx-script): the window's size in points, read-only and followed (`ScriptHost::window_size`, which `zgx run` calls), for decisions layout can't make, such as a sidebar collapsing to icons. A new lint, `window-size-layout`, reports an element's size computed from it, which layout does without the script ([native](docs/reference/native.md#size)).
- **`ui.each` takes a plain table, and dates and times can be controlled** (zgx-script): a list that never changes needs no state; `ui.date_picker`, `ui.calendar` and `ui.time_field` take `value = function` with `on_change`, as `ui.select` and `ui.checkbox` do.
- **Text on a canvas** (zgx, zgx-script): `c:text(text, x, y, { size, color, weight, align, baseline, tabular, font })` and `c:measure_text(text, opts)`, `Canvas::text` and `zgx::canvas::measure_text` in Rust. A chart's labels are shaped and drawn as any text is, sharp at any zoom and with fallback fonts, over the canvas's shapes. A canvas without text pays nothing for it. Assistive technology reads the canvas's `label`, not its text.
- **Tone mapping and exposure in 3D scenes**: `World::set_tone_mapping(ToneMapping::Neutral | Aces | Linear)` and `World::set_exposure(stops)`. Khronos's PBR Neutral stays the default (colours read true, only highlights roll off); `Aces` is the filmic curve (Stephen Hill's fit, exposed by 1/0.6 as three.js does, so mid grey stays about mid); `Linear` clamps. In Luau, `ui.scene3d { tone_mapping = "aces", exposure = 1 }` (`exposure` reactive). Output stays sRGB-correct either way (linear 0.18 shows as 118 of 255).
- **`app:hover(target)` in `zgx test`, `TestApp::hover` in Rust; a `ui.progress` takes a number or a function** (zgx-script): a tooltip can be tested, and a bar in a list row needs no state. `zgx test` no longer warns that `ui.persist` has no storage, which it never has.
- **`zgx new <name>`** (zgx-script) writes a Luau app that runs at once (`zgx run`), from one of four templates: `basic` (tasks), `router` (notes with routes and a modal route), `list` (a long list paged as it scrolls, searched as you type, reorderable favourites) and `form` (a profile form). Each has a component, a theme of the app's own tokens (read as `ui.tokens.app.*`), a test, `zgx.json`, `.luaurc`, its editor types, a README and a GitHub Actions workflow running `zgx check`, `zgx lint`, `zgx audit` and `zgx test`, all four clean. `--i18n` writes its text as `ui.t` messages with `locales/en.json` ([getting started](docs/getting-started.md#in-luau)).
- **Image-based light in 3D scenes**: `World::set_environment(Some(Environment::studio()))` lights every surface from all around, its reflections sharp in polished metal and blurred in rough (split-sum: a cube prefiltered at five roughnesses, Karis's fit for its second factor, nine spherical harmonics for its diffuse light). `Environment::decode_hdr` reads a Radiance `.hdr` image (equirectangular), `from_equirect` and `from_cube` take linear RGB; `intensity` and `rotated` brighten and turn one. In Luau, `ui.scene3d { environment = "studio" | "none" | "sky.hdr" | { src, intensity, rotation } }`, and a scene showing a `ui.model` has the studio unless it says otherwise. Prefiltering takes 11–16 ms off the main thread (the studio once an app); its cube is 0.5 MB, written in place on Apple GPUs (`zgx_gpu::viewport::cube_with_levels`): no more driver blocks (Box 20, DamagedHelmet 23 with or without). A scene without one binds nothing new.
- **`zgx run <app>`** (zgx-script) opens a Luau app in a window as `zgx-run` does, by the zgx-run beside `zgx` or on PATH (saying how to install it when neither is there), so one command runs, checks, lints, audits and tests an app. `zgx audit` finds it the same way.
- **Tests in Luau** (zgx-script): `zgx test` runs an app's `*.test.luau` files, each test against the app mounted headless. A file gets three globals: `test(name, body)`, `mount()`, and `expect(v)` with `to_be`, `to_equal`, `to_contain`, `to_be_truthy`, `to_be_nil` and their `never` forms. The app is driven by devtools' targets (`#id`, `"label"`, `role:button`, `#list row 3`): click, type, fill, key, scroll, command, text, has, count, wait, state and set_state. A failure names its line, and `zgx check` knows the globals' types in test files alone. `TestApp::try_new` mounts or answers the script's error ([testing an app](docs/scripting.md#testing-an-app)).
- **Skinned meshes in 3D scenes**: `Geometry::with_skin(joints, weights)` gives a mesh's vertices up to four joints each, and `World::set_skin(id, Some(skin))` binds an object showing it to a `Skin::new(joints, inverse_binds)` (objects as joints, up to `MAX_JOINTS`, 256): the GPU places each vertex by its joints' matrices, so a clip moving the joints bends the mesh. glTF models bring their skins (`JOINTS_0`, `WEIGHTS_0`); its shadow and picking follow the pose. A scene without skins binds nothing new and costs what it did (the scene3d demo level, 23 driver blocks); CesiumMan walking with its texture: 25. Morph targets are not read yet.
- **`zgx test` drags** (zgx-script): `app:drag(from, to)` drags one target onto another, as `TestApp::drag` does in Rust.
- **glTF animations**: `Model::clips` (and `clip(name)`) reads a model's animations: channels moving its nodes' translation, rotation and scale, by STEP, LINEAR (rotations along the shorter arc) or CUBICSPLINE keys. `World::place_model` adds a model as `add_model` does and keeps its nodes' objects; `Placed::pose(world, clip, seconds)` poses a clip at any time. In Luau, `ui.model { animation = "Walk" }` (or its place, from 1) plays one round and round, posed in Rust a frame at a time. The `model` example plays a file's first clip.
- **A trackpad's swipe between pages goes back and forward** (macOS): two fingers across (or three, as the system's Trackpad setting has it), as Safari does, over a view with `on_navigate` (zgx-script) or `View::on_navigate` (Rust), which hears `"back"` or `"forward"`. A two-finger swipe navigates only where it starts where nothing under the fingers scrolls that way, so a carousel mid-content still scrolls; AppKit tracks it from there. With nothing that navigates under it, it scrolls and stretches as before ([events](docs/reference/events.md)). *Needs the repository's winit ([getting started](docs/getting-started.md)).*
- **Live connections** (zgx-script): `ui.socket(url, { json, reconnect, on_message, on_open, on_close, max_buffered })`, as React's `useWebSocket`, keeps a connection open through the host's `ScriptHost::socket_connector`. It has `status` (`"connecting"`, `"open"`, `"reconnecting"`, `"closed"`), `attempt`, `retry_in` and `buffered` as states, `error` as a `UiError`, and `send(v)` (text, or a table as JSON), `send_bytes(s)`, `close()` and `reconnect()`. What is sent before it opens waits and goes in order. It opens again after a growing wait when its connection ends, and again at once when a state its URL function reads changes. `ui.sleep(ms)` is a promise to await in a task ([state](docs/reference/state.md#live-connections)).
- **Files dropped from outside the window, and uploads** (zgx-script, zgx-run): `on_file_drop = function(files, e)` and `on_file_hover = function(over)` hear files dragged in from Finder, Explorer or a file manager (react-dropzone's `onDrop` and `isDragActive`). A `File` now has `size` and `type` (its media type) as the web's does. `ui.fetch(url, { file = f })` sends a file as the body, `multipart = { … }` sends a form's text and files as `multipart/form-data`, both read off the window's thread, and `on_progress(sent, total)` hears the body go out. zgx-run's fetcher tells it as it writes, and a host's streaming fetcher does with `Body::sent` ([state](docs/reference/state.md#the-clipboard-and-files), [events](docs/reference/events.md)).
- **Suggestions at the caret** (zgx, zgx-script): `ui.popover { at_caret = true, anchor = ui.textarea { … } }` (`View::at_caret`) places a popover at the caret of the field in its anchor, as a mention's or a slash command's suggestions are. It follows the caret as text is typed, a line wraps, the field scrolls and an input method composes. It takes no focus, so the field's keys move through the suggestions, and `active_descendant = id` (`View::active_descendant`, HTML's `aria-activedescendant`) tells assistive technology the one it is on ([overlays](docs/reference/overlays.md#suggestions-at-the-caret)).
- **Scripts read whether their window is active and shown, post system notifications and badge the app's icon** (zgx, zgx-script, zgx-desktop): `ui.window.active` and `ui.window.shown` (`Ui::observe_window_active`, `Ui::observe_shown`, published by `ScriptHost::window_state`), `ui.notify { title, body, tag }` (`Platform::notify`: macOS's Notification Center for a bundled app, a Windows toast for the app's identity, a Linux desktop's notification server), and `ui.badge(n)` (`Platform::set_badge`: the Dock's, a Linux dock's). A `UiError` gains the kinds `"unsupported"` and `"denied"` ([native](docs/reference/native.md#notifications-and-badges)).
- **A component's `id` says when it replaces one inside** (zgx-script): `Panel { id = 5 }` names the component's root, so an `id` set on that root goes unseen. A warning as it mounts names both ids and where, and `zgx lint`'s `component-id` finds it within a file.
- **A popover can anchor to an editor's caret, and an editor can name its active descendant** (zgx, zgx-script): `ui.popover { at_caret = true, anchor = ui.textarea { … } }` (`View::at_caret`) places a popover at the caret of the field in its anchor, as a mention's or a slash command's suggestions are. It follows the caret as text is typed, a line wraps, the field scrolls and an input method composes. It takes no focus, so the field's keys move through the suggestions, and `active_descendant = id` (`View::active_descendant`, HTML's `aria-activedescendant`) tells assistive technology the one it is on ([overlays](docs/reference/overlays.md#suggestions-at-the-caret)).
- **Lists report reaching their start and resting at their end, and keep their place when rows are added above** (zgx, zgx-script): `ui.list { on_start = fn, start_distance = 200 }` (`View::on_start`) is called as the list's start comes in reach, as `on_end` is at its end, and `at_end = state` (`View::on_at_end`) follows whether it rests at its end. A list with `on_start` given rows above the ones in view (`measured_rows`, or `ui.list` with `measured` and `key`) finds the row it showed by its key and keeps it where it was, rather than letting the new rows push it down; other lists track nothing for it ([scrolling](docs/reference/scrolling.md)).
- [The channels example](crates/zgx-script/examples/channels): a team's chat in Luau against a server in memory, built on this release's primitives. It has a WebSocket that reconnects on its own with a banner while it does, messages shown at once and confirmed or refused (and sent again), unread counts and presence, a list that stays at its end and loads earlier pages at its start without jumping, mentions in a popover at the caret, an emoji picker, files dropped or attached and uploaded with their progress, and a system notification and the icon's badge for what arrives in the background. Run it with `cargo run --release -p zgx-script --example channels`.
- **A notification's click reaches the script, and Windows badges the taskbar button** (zgx, zgx-script, zgx-desktop): `ui.notify { on_click = fn }` (`Platform::next_notification_click`) runs when the person clicks the notification, the app's window brought forward first: through macOS's notification center delegate, a Windows toast's `Activated`, a Linux server's `ActionInvoked`. A notification without a `tag` gets one of its own. On Windows `ui.badge(n)` is an overlay on the taskbar button, the count drawn and described to screen readers ([native](docs/reference/native.md#notifications-and-badges)).

### Performance

- **Scripts that update and scroll run faster than in 0.1.6** (exact instruction counts at the release on Linux arm64): updating a script's state costs 6.4% less and the lab's scroll 4.7% less; every other core benchmark is faster by 0.01% to 0.48% (heavy scrolling −0.48%, `micro-state` −0.34%, widgets −0.25%, animation −0.20%, mount −0.15%), or flat (script layout and `list-state`, +0.01%).
- **Textured 3D scenes draw in 63% fewer instructions a frame** (8.81 → 3.25 M; the whole benchmark −56%); loading one costs about 5% more, for its larger shaders.
- **The example apps' interactions cost 0.06% to 0.40% more** (`app-sketch` +0.40%, `app-chat` +0.29%, `app-code-search` +0.15%, `app-mail` +0.11%, the dashboard +0.06 to +0.09%, `app-code` +0.08%), zgx's own share 28 to 97 K instructions an interaction, the rest Luau (its garbage collector's pacing) and libc. Their causes are named, for 0.1.8: `z` ordering's bookkeeping (#1070), even in apps that set no `z`; percentage bases (#1116) in mail and chat; and the code editor's highlight conversion (#1020).
- **3D frames set each pipeline, binding and buffer once a run of meshes needs it** (zgx-3d): every run set them all again, the same or not. A frame of 2,000 varied meshes costs 6% fewer instructions.
- **3D frames allocate almost nothing of their own** (zgx-3d): every skinned or morphing mesh's pose went into a new list each frame, and a frame's working lists grew from empty. Poses now share one list apiece and the lists are kept between frames, so a frame of 2,000 skinned, morphing and textured meshes makes 180 allocations where it made 1,511, the rest wgpu's. Frames cost 1–4% fewer instructions and no longer change cost when other code shifts the heap. `World::set_morph_weights` copies into the list a mesh has.
- **3D pipelines cost less to make** (zgx-3d): a mesh variant's shader is written in one pass, copying only the lines it keeps, instead of being copied three times (prefixes, anti-aliasing, comments). Setup of the `3d-textured` cell falls by 0.29 M instructions and of `3d-mixed` by 1.18 M.
- **A canvas's filled areas draw on the GPU on Windows when the app ships DXC** (zgx-gpu): with `dxcompiler.dll` beside the executable, an area chart draws as one quad again, where with FXC alone, which can't compile its shader, it rasterizes every redraw.
- **A field reads its code options with less work** (zgx-script): `highlight`, `decorations`, `underlines`, `tab`, `on_caret`, `on_top_line` and `find` are read straight from its table, rather than by a protected lookup each, which every `ui.input` and `ui.textarea` paid.
- **A JPEG drawn small never decodes at full size** (zgx, zgx-gpu): it decodes at 1/2, 1/4 or 1/8 of its size, the smallest that covers the size drawn, with the DCT scaled (zgx's fork of zune-jpeg, published as `zgx-zune-jpeg`; `ImageData::kept_scaled` and `zgx::image::DecodeScaled` are new). A kept image's decode no longer copies its pixels to premultiply them. A page of eight 4000 by 3000 photos as 300 by 420 cards: each decode's peak 91.6 → 18.6 MiB and time 47 → 22 ms; the process's peak about 490 → 330 MB. JPEGs decode through the fork directly, not `image`, which no longer carries a JPEG decoder of its own; their pixels are unchanged at full size.
- **Left-to-right text skips the bidi analysis** (zgx-gpu): a line with characters beyond ASCII (accents, `—`, `…`, `×`, emoji) but nothing right to left, no bidi control and no paragraph separator is laid out as one left-to-right run, as ASCII already was, without `unicode-bidi`'s analysis. One look at its bytes decides; a test checks every code point the analysis treats otherwise is caught. The gate's `lab-scroll` (such text, scrolled) runs 4.4% fewer instructions.
- **New monospaced text no longer searches every installed font** (zgx-gpu, zgx-cosmic-text): each new word of monospaced text (a terminal's every line, a log, a code view's edits) weighed every fixed-width face on the system before shaping. Its family's face is now kept, as other families' are, and the search runs only for a character the face lacks. Shaping a terminal's 24 new lines takes 32 µs instead of 135 µs, and an engine-drawn terminal printing 20,000 lines a second spends a quarter less CPU on macOS. A text's fallback families are looked up once rather than whenever it is laid out anew.
- **A scrolling terminal draws only its new lines** (zgx-terminal): when output scrolled the screen, every row of an engine-drawn terminal was worked out and drawn again. The rows still on screen now move up with it, without layout, and only the new lines draw, so ordinary output (a line a frame) costs about a third less on the UI thread and 15% less in all, and a flood of output costs the same. Rows stay in reading order for screen readers.

### Fixed

- **A canvas stroke no longer crashes a Windows app** (zgx-gpu): Direct3D 12 compiling the canvas shader overflowed the stack inside FXC, Windows' shader compiler, so an app's first chart line, sparkline or stroke crashed it on a machine that hadn't cached the shader yet. Filled areas, whose shader FXC can't compile, now rasterize on Windows; strokes still draw on the GPU.
- **The arrows reach every place in mixed-direction text** (zgx): where a right-to-left run meets a number or another run, a boundary has a place on each side, and Left and Right now stop at both, so the caret can sit after a number ending a Hebrew or Arabic run (to type more digits), not only after the run. At a line's visual edge the arrows no longer bounce back when its last run goes the other way (`(אבג) [123] xyz`); they stay, or go on to the next line.
- **A hot-reloading host's type check no longer piles up** (zgx-script). In a debug build, every host with `watch` ran luau-lsp over the whole app after each reload on a thread of its own, and never stopped the previous run. With several debug apps open and agents saving files, hundreds of luau-lsp processes ran at once (531 on one Mac, which ran out of memory). Now a machine runs one check at a time, however many hosts and debug processes it has. A process's checks take turns on one thread, and processes take turns on an advisory lock file in the temporary directory, held only while luau-lsp runs. Each check starts once its host's saves have stopped for 300 ms. A newer reload replaces the host's check, killing its luau-lsp if it is running, and a dropped host kills its own. When nothing is asked for, the checker's thread waits, doing nothing.
- **A dialog asked for as a script loads opens, or says why not** (zgx-script): `ui.open_files`, `ui.save_file`, `ui.open_folder` and the clipboard's images, called from a task at a script's top level, failed silently. They looked for the window's platform at once, before the view had mounted, and the load's success then cleared the error. They now look for it as their promise's work first runs, when the window is there, and an error reported while a script loads (a refused dialog, a task's failure) stays shown.
- **Colour emoji keep their colours** (zgx-gpu): a colour glyph (an emoji) was multiplied by its text's colour, so grey text dimmed its emoji, black text drew silhouettes and red text tinted them red. Colour glyphs now draw in their own colours with the text's alpha (opacity and fades still apply), whatever the text's colour or selected-text colour, and their shadows are their shape in the shadow's colour. The software renderer already drew them so.
- **An emoji asking for emoji presentation draws in color** (zgx-gpu): a cluster with U+FE0F, or a keycap's U+20E3, now comes from the color emoji font even where the text font has its characters. On Windows, Segoe UI has `1` and the keycap mark, so `1️⃣` drew as a black outline. Text-default symbols without U+FE0F stay in the text font.
- **What leaves takes no clicks** (zgx-script): a `ui.each` row removed from its list, and what a `ui.presence` lets go of, play their `exit` where they were but the pointer passes through them to what's under, so a fast second click acts on what's still there, never on data already gone. A focused row leaving gives focus to the next row (else the one before, else the nearest focusable around it), Enter and Space never activate it, and assistive technology no longer offers it.
- **Escape gives focus back as browsers and native toolkits do** (zgx). A popup closing returns focus to what had it before it opened. If that's gone, it returns to the popup's trigger, but only if the popup opened from the keyboard. Otherwise nothing is focused. Before, a popup opened with nothing focused gave focus to the window's first focusable control (Zeron's "New chat"), so the next Enter pressed it. Escape no longer turns focus rings on either, as `:focus-visible` has it: a menu opened by the mouse and closed with Escape gives focus back without a ring, and one moved through with the arrows keeps it. Tab, the arrows, Home/End, Page Up/Down and typing still turn rings on, and shortcuts with Cmd, Ctrl or Alt don't. A context click on a focusable view focuses it before its menu opens, as a press does in a browser, so the menu gives focus back to it.
- **A broken Ghostty checkout in zgx-ghostty-sys's cache is fetched again**, not a failed build: one left without its files (as a cache keeping only part of a directory leaves it) is deleted and fetched anew, with a `cargo:warning` saying so. Only a checkout the build script made, marked by its `.zgx-ghostty-checkout` file, is ever deleted; an unmarked one stops the build with what to do.
- **A Japanese line's kanji draw in a Japanese font** (zgx-gpu): Han characters took the fallback font of the system's language, so on an English system a Japanese line's kanji came from a different font than its kana: Arial Unicode MS on macOS, Microsoft YaHei UI's Chinese forms on Windows. A line's own kana (or hangul, for Korean) now says which language its Han characters are in, and the run cache keeps them apart. Lines with neither keep the system language's font, and ASCII text is never looked through.
- **Large images draw, at the size they show** (zgx-gpu): an image's texture held its whole file (a 4000 by 3000 photo was 48 MB however small it showed), so two large photos passed the 64 MiB budget and the second was left out, or, before 0.1.7, ended the app. Textures now hold an image at its drawn size, resampled as mip levels are, uploaded again as it is drawn larger. The budget is four of the window's frames (at least 64 MiB), and past it images are drawn softer, not left out.
- **An empty image `src` shows nothing** (zgx-script): `ui.image`, `ui.svg` and `bg_image` with `src = ""` (a picture not chosen yet, often a state's first value) show nothing and report no error, where they read the script's directory and failed with "Is a directory". A missing image's error names the script line asking for it, never a line of `.zgx/types.d.luau` or another declaration or hidden file that happened to hold the same text.
- **A layer as large as the window draws at its scale** (zgx-gpu): each isolated layer had 16 MiB of its own, so one window-sized layer at 2x (2880 by 1800, 20.7 MB) rasterized coarser, softer, and warned of the 64 MiB budget with far less resident. A layer now takes what the budget leaves; the budget is four of the window's frames, at least 64 MiB, counted against what the textures allocate. The warning names the layer and what was resident, allocated and live, once per layer. `zgx inspect memory` lists each layer (`gpu layer`: node, pixels, scale, live and allocated bytes, transformed or parked) and `layers live` beside `layers`.
- **Monospaced text is upright on Linux** (zgx-gpu): cosmic-text's monospace family, Noto Sans Mono, is seldom installed, and its stand-in was whichever fixed-width face came first, on a system with DejaVu its Oblique: every code block and terminal slanted. A family with an upright face is taken now (DejaVu Sans Mono, Liberation Mono, Ubuntu Mono and the like, else any).
- **Chinese text on macOS draws in PingFang** (zgx-gpu, the cosmic-text fork): macOS 26 keeps PingFang, its Chinese face, in a private framework, outside the font directories fontdb reads. The fallback lists named only PingFang, so Chinese Han drew in Arial Unicode MS, and traditional and simplified Chinese looked alike. The fork now loads PingFang from where Core Text finds it, if no PingFang is already loaded. Heiti SC and TC, in every macOS, follow it in the lists in case it moves. The system font cache's format is bumped, so the cache is rebuilt once with PingFang in it: 32 more faces, about 0.01 ms more to read it at launch.
- **CJK in monospaced text shows** (zgx-gpu, macOS): a monospaced line with Chinese, Japanese or Korean characters fell back to a bitmap-only system font that has no units per em, took infinite width and showed nothing past the first such character. That font is passed by now.
- **A declared window's own state follows it** (zgx-desktop, zgx-run, zgx-script): `win.active`, `win.shown` and `win.size` stopped following a declared window for two reasons. `WindowContext::on_closed` replaced any earlier handler, so `zgx run`'s second handler for a window dropped the first, and with it the effect keeping the state current. And a reload (a hot one, or devtools starting to record) built the window's content with a new state that no host set. Each `on_closed` handler now runs, in the order given, and a window keeps its own state across loads while it is published.
- **A remembered folder survives an upgrade outside the sandbox** (zgx-desktop): on macOS, a folder kept with `ui.open_folder { remember }` is now a plain bookmark unless the app is sandboxed. A security-scoped bookmark resolves only under the code-signing identifier that made it, and an unbundled app's identifier changes with its build (zgx-run's is cargo's metadata hash), so every remembered folder was forgotten at an upgrade. A sandboxed app still makes security-scoped ones. A grant of the other kind still resolves, and is renewed as the kind the app makes.
- **A plugin's node reads what the app provides above it** (zgx-script): `ui.use_context` in what `ui.contribute` adds no longer crashes with "Lua instance passed Value created from a different main Lua state". A plain value arrives converted into the plugin's script, a state as the same state (read only, followed live), and a function is an error naming it. Adding `before` or `after` the root of a `ui.each` or `ui.list` row, which drew nothing, is now an error saying to target an element inside the row.
- **A menu opened by a press stays open when released over its trigger** (zgx-desktop, Wayland): when a compositor cut a tall menu and slid it over its trigger, the popup's pointer events (on zgx's own queue) could be handled before the window's release that came first. The release then counted as a drag away, and the menu closed. A window's own buttons and wheel are now heard where the window last saw the pointer. *Needs the repository's winit ([getting started](docs/getting-started.md)): from crates.io, a Wayland menu opens in its window.*
- **A menu's trigger no longer looks pressed after the menu closes** (zgx). A menu opens on the press, so the release went to the menu, and the trigger kept its pressed look while the menu showed and after it closed. A press that opens a popup now ends for what it pressed (it hears a pointer cancel), as the release goes to the popup.
- **Tabs and horizontal scrolls no longer warn of an unresolved height** (zgx): with devtools recording, every `ui.tabs` (and any `scroll_x` with no height of its own in a row sized by its content) reported "height 100% has nothing to resolve against" for its viewport and content. Those take a definite height or their content's by design, so layout warnings now pass over a scroll's own viewport and content.
- **A scroll view's fade leaves its scrollbar whole** (zgx): `fade_edges` on a scroll view or list with a scrollbar (Luau's `fade = { 64, 64 }` with `scrollbar = true`) faded the scrollbar's thumb with the rows, as it passes the fade to all its children. The scrollbar now paints outside its own view's fade; a fade around the view still fades both.
- **Coarse curved meshes no longer shadow themselves in speckles** (zgx-3d): where a curve turns from the sun, its flat faces fall behind its smooth normals and caught their own shadow as acne; the shadow lookup now moves off the surface further the more it turns away. A shadow still starts at the base of what casts it, however low the sun. The `model` example's floor sits just below a model's base, where a model's own floor fought it in speckles.
- **Rounded span backgrounds round on their own corners** (zgx-gpu): a span's rounded background (`TextSpan::radius`, Luau `ui.span { bg, padding, radius }`, an inline code chip) rounded only the corners that lay on its text's own edges, so a chip mid-line was square, as was each piece of a wrapped one. Each piece now rounds on its own box: a chip wrapped across lines rounds each line's piece on its own ends, as CSS's `box-decoration-break: clone`.
- **Ctrl+C reaches a terminal's program on Windows whatever started the app** (zgx-terminal): Windows passes a parent's choice to ignore Ctrl+C on to its children, so an app started by one (an agent's shell, a service, a launcher) started shells where Ctrl+C never interrupted anything. A session restores Ctrl+C handling before it starts its program.
- **Screen readers hear all of a terminal's new output, and not its echo** (zgx-terminal): an engine-drawn terminal told readers of new output by setting its live region at each read, and output read in two parts before the frame's accessibility update (a command's output, then the prompt after it) was heard as its last part alone: Orca said `$` and never the output. It now says everything read since its last update with the next frame's. And the shell's echo of each typed character was read out, once a read with nothing new had dropped what was typed: what was typed now waits for its echo.
- **A terminal names the program in front after it execs another** (zgx-terminal): an engine-drawn terminal kept the name it first read for a foreground process as long as the process's pid stayed in front, but a process keeps its pid when it runs another program in its place. So the shell, read in the moment between its session forking it and its running, could be named after the app for the whole of a quiet command (`sleep 30`), and `exec less` went on showing the shell. The name is now read again at each check, and the check runs once output settles (a moment after the latest output, not the first of a burst), so a command that goes quiet at once, like `sleep 30` typed at a prompt, is named too. Checks still follow output alone: nothing polls.
- **⌘Z no longer sticks in a field the app normalises** (zgx, zgx-script): undo went back to what was typed, the app normalised it again, and that became a new step. What the app makes of an edit now joins that edit, so undo goes past both (`TextEditor::answer_next`).
- **A drag begun as the previous drop finishes isn't lost on Wayland** (zgx-desktop): the compositor gives the pointer back at a drop, before the drop's target finishes with it. A drag started then (a tab, say) was ended by that earlier drop's end, with a button release it never had. It now goes on, and goes to the compositor once the earlier drop is done. *Known issue:* until then it can't leave its window, so let go within that moment (a few milliseconds, longer for a slow target), it drops nothing ([#1259](https://github.com/zeronsh/zgx/issues/1259)).
- **A web page's tree reaches screen readers on Linux on a busy machine too** (zgx-desktop's `webkitgtk`): the page's box embedded the page's accessible tree only if WebKit had named it by the time the page loaded; now it does whenever WebKit names it.
- **A shortcut a web page leaves alone reaches the app on a busy machine** (zgx-desktop's `webkitgtk`, Linux): the key came back from WebKit's web process with the modifiers held when it answered, not when it was pressed, so Ctrl+K let go of quickly reached the app as K and no shortcut took it.
- **An input method's candidates show under a web page's caret on a busy machine** (zgx-desktop's `webkitgtk`, Linux): IBus over XIM forgets the spot as a composition begins, and a page's caret comes from WebKit's web process, sometimes after the candidates had shown, which then stayed at the window's bottom left. The window gives the spot again as a page's composition begins, and tells the input method each caret the page reports as it comes, not at its next frame.
- **Web Inspector opens in a window of its own on Linux, however large the page** (zgx-desktop's `webkitgtk`): WebKitGTK docked the inspector inside a large enough page, where it drew in the page's frames but heard none of its input. It is now detached as WebKit docks it, as its own undock button would, before the page lays out around it.
- **Video and audio play in a web page on Linux** (zgx-desktop's `webkitgtk`): WebKitGTK took a page in a `GtkOffscreenWindow` for one in no window and started no media there, so no `<video>` or `<audio>` ever loaded and an `AudioContext` stayed suspended. A page's window is now zgx's own offscreen window, which WebKitGTK counts as a window; WebM and Ogg play with the GStreamer plugins WebKitGTK depends on, H.264 with `gstreamer1.0-libav`.
- **A window's content fills it** (zgx-script): the content of a `ui.window` or an item's window of `ui.windows` took its own size, so a detail window's column sat in its top-left corner and a `ui.tabs` in it fell back to a scroll view's default 320 by 240. It now fills its window as the app's own window's root does: across, as a block fills its parent, and down with `h_full`.
- **A `ui.tabs` panel can fill the tabs** (zgx-components): `grow` and `h_full` on a panel's root had nothing to fill, so an editor in a tab stayed at its default size, with layout warnings.
- **A glTF file requiring Draco or meshopt compression says so**: its geometry can't be read yet, and it loaded as an empty model with no error.
- **`zgx test` drives the desktop and the window's size** (zgx-script): `mount()` has a clipboard and file dialogs that answer as the test says (`save_to`, `pick`, `write_file`, `read_file`, `clipboard`, `copy`), so an Export or Import button can be tested instead of failing with "no platform here", and `app:resize(width, height)` lays out at a new size, `ui.window.size` following. `TestApp::try_with_desktop` for Rust tests.
- **The first undo no longer empties a controlled field** (zgx-script): a `ui.input` or `ui.textarea` with `value = function` took its text just after mounting, as an edit, so ⌘Z blanked it and `on_change` told the app so.
- **A capture of a window's frame shows its loops and caret** (zgx-gpu; macOS). `zgx inspect` captures and `Ui::snapshot` read the frame Metal drew, so what Core Animation plays over it (a baked loop, the blinking caret) was missing: an agent saw a page with its spinner gone. They now draw it in, at its current frame.
- **A small looping animation no longer keeps a window drawing** (zgx-gpu, zgx-script; macOS). A spinner or pulsing dot declared with `anime.animate { loop = true, frameRate = … }` on `opacity` or `shaderTime` drew a frame each tick, and any drawn frame keeps about 176 MB of Metal driver memory for 8–13 s on M5 Macs, so one spinner held it for as long as it spun. Such a loop is now drawn once into a small sheet and played by Core Animation: no frames, no wakes. A gallery page with a spinner went from ~340 to ~170 MiB, and app + WindowServer CPU fell 1.7–1.9 points at 60 and 120 Hz. Nothing changes in an app: a loop that a layer couldn't show exactly (cut by a clip or a fade's band, painted over, under a layer, in a popup, large, or watched by a script) draws as it did, and in a `trace` build `zgx inspect switch baked_loops off` turns it off.

- **Pages of flex containers peak lower** (zgx): each container on the advanced layout path gets a layout tree the size of its children rather than taffy's 16 slots, about 4 MB less at the peak on a 21-page gallery, 0.48 → 0.12 MB allocated per page switch at that site.
- **`zgx check` reports unknown members of `ui`** (zgx-script): `ui.window`'s type was a function intersected with a table, which Luau reads as `never`, and that quieted every lookup on `ui`: a misspelled `ui.nope` passed strict mode and failed as `attempt to call a nil value` when it ran. A misspelled option in a `ui.window { … }` table is `zgx lint`'s `window-option`.
- **A list being shrunk no longer flips its height for ever** (zgx): a size within a 1024th of a point of the last one told is rounding, not movement, and isn't told. Sums at a column's scale could land on neighbouring floats, a reader of the size dirtied layout, and it landed on the other: layout never settled. It took a list in a tight column (a zoomed sidebar) with a fractional neighbour (a fold animating).
- **A reload frees the script it replaces** (zgx-script): an `anime` animation, timeline, draggable or scroll link held a strong handle to the script's own Luau state from inside it, so the state never closed and each hot reload leaked all of it. On a gallery with 15 MB of script heap, 100 reloads took the heap from 54 MB to 2.7 GB; now they stay between 60 and 95 MB, every old state closed.
- **A panic no longer ends hot reload** (zgx, zgx-script): a panic while reloading shows as a script error does and the next save reloads, where the watching task went on failing with `` `async fn` resumed after panicking `` until the app was relaunched. A task that panics in `LocalExecutor::tick` goes, and the tasks after it stay ready.
- **A `ui.list` with sticky rows takes `key`, `scrollbar` and its edge callbacks** (zgx-script): `key` with `sticky` was refused, and `scrollbar`, `overscroll`, `on_end` and `on_start` panicked or failed on the wrapper around the list. A grouped list with keyed rows and sticky headers works: a sticky row is built as its row is, and follows its key as the list changes.
- **A `ui.list` of `data` takes `heights`** (zgx-script): a grouped list, with `sticky` if it likes, shows its items as they change: an edited item's row builds again. Rows given `heights` by `key` alone are built once a key, so an edit didn't show.
- **An app left alone gives memory back** (zgx-desktop, zgx-script): as the window goes idle (1.5 s after its last frame), a script host collects its scripts' garbage, drops the decoded pictures no node shows (kept, up to `ScriptHost::image_cache`'s budget, while the app is in use), and the allocator returns what was freed (`mi_collect` with the `mimalloc` feature, `malloc_zone_pressure_relief` on macOS, `malloc_trim` on Linux). Zeron's gallery, 200 pages browsed and left 15 s: 237–265 MB → 207–209 MB. `ScriptHost::collect_garbage` collects now; `zgx::idle::on_idle` registers what else a host should give back.
- **Luau's free pages leave memory as an app goes idle** (zgx-mlua, zgx-script): Luau frees a 16 KB page only when its last object goes, and the program's allocator kept such pages in larger runs it returns only whole. With the `mimalloc` feature they now come from a pool of their own, and each free page goes back to the system as a host goes idle (`mlua::release_free_pages`). Zeron's gallery, 200 pages browsed and left 15 s: 197–204 MB → 173–177 MB.
- **An animation started while only unseen loops played starts on the next frame** (zgx-script): the engine, then looking a few times a second, started it up to 250 ms late and in one jump (a sidebar's width animating 286 → 542 in one frame). A clip's first frame now advances it one frame at most.
- **Layout that never settles no longer ends the app** (zgx, zgx-desktop, zgx-script): a window keeps the layout the last of its 64 passes left, tells nothing more of its geometry, and goes on, taking input. It reports once, naming the nodes that still moved (`@12`, as `zgx inspect` does), on stderr, in the window's banner, and to the new `zgx::issues::on_issue` and `ScriptHost::on_error`. Whenever the layout changes it tries again in at most 16 passes. `Ui::try_prepare_frame` still returns `LayoutFeedbackError` unless a host calls `Ui::contain_layout_feedback`, as window hosts do ([what an app keeps running through](docs/application.md#when-something-fails)).
- **`ui.each` in a `scroll_x` lays out across** (zgx-script): its rows stacked in a column there.
- macOS: with zgx-desktop's `mimalloc` feature, the program's heap shows in vmmap, footprint and Activity Monitor as "Memory Tag 240", not "IOAccelerator". mimalloc's default tag, 100, is the one macOS names IOAccelerator, so an app's heap read as GPU memory: in one app 107 MB of "IOAccelerator" was its heap. `MIMALLOC_OS_TAG` still overrides it.
- **`ui.split` works without `size`**, a state of its own at 280 points; **`zgx lint`'s `unknown-token` names what a token's group holds** when nothing is close (`$text.muted`: there is `$text.primary`, `$text.secondary`…) (zgx-script).
- **An input bound to a state calls `on_commit`** (zgx-script): `ui.input { value = draft, on_commit = add }` heard nothing on Enter, as only a controlled input (`value = function`) told it. It now hears the text on Enter, and on leaving the input changed since it was focused, as a controlled input does.
- **A scroll view, list or text area can keep a gesture at its edges** (zgx, zgx-script): `overscroll = "contain"` (`View::overscroll_contain`, CSS's `overscroll-behavior: contain`) on `ui.scroll`, `ui.list`, `ui.input` and `ui.textarea`. A wheel or trackpad gesture that starts over the view stays with it at its edges, momentum included, so the scroll views around it neither move nor stretch (a transcript inside a page). `"auto"`, the default, hands what it can't scroll on as before ([scrolling](docs/reference/scrolling.md)).

## 0.1.6 (2026-10-29)

A patch: Rust and Luau code builds as before (every API change since 0.1.5 is an addition, and zgx-desktop and zgx-gpu 0.1.5 build against this zgx). zgx-gpu and zgx-desktop now need `zgx-cosmic-text` 0.19.3, published with it. One behaviour changes, below with what to check: Windows input methods go through the Text Services Framework by default.

### Changed: what to check in an existing app

- **Windows: input methods go through the Text Services Framework by default**, not IMM32. Editors give input methods and text services (the emoji panel, dictation, the touch keyboard) a text store: they read the text around the caret, place candidates at the clause they convert, reconvert committed text in place and follow the editor's input mode, and each clause is underlined. To keep IMM32, an app calls `Application::text_services(false)`, and a user sets `ZGX_TSF=0` (`ZGX_TSF=1` forces the framework on); a window where the framework does not start uses IMM32 ([windows](docs/windows.md#the-text-services-framework)).

### Added

- **An inbox example** (zgx-script): `examples/inbox`, a React-style app on `ui.router` (a modal route, deep links), `ui.paged` with a list's `on_end`, `ui.debounced` search, a reorderable `ui.each` and context, in the accessibility audit.
- **Emission and occlusion maps in 3D scenes**: `Map::Emission` colours the light a mesh gives off (multiplying its material's emission), `Map::Occlusion` dims the ambient light reaching each point (glTF's occlusion, not the sun's or a bulb's). glTF models bring theirs, and `KHR_materials_emissive_strength`. A scene without textures still binds none and costs what it did; DamagedHelmet with all five maps: 25 driver blocks, as with three.
- **Routing** (zgx-script): `ui.router { routes, scheme }` keeps where an app is: named routes with `:params` (`navigate` by name, `open` by path), back and forward with the mouse's buttons and Alt+←/→ (⌘[ and ⌘] on macOS), deep links by scheme, and `router.view` showing each route. A modal, popover or menu can be controlled (`open = function`, `on_close`), so a modal route opens with its route and goes back as it closes ([routing](docs/reference/state.md#routing)).
- **Reorderable lists** (zgx-script): `ui.each(list, row, { key, reorder = function(from, to) end })` makes rows the person drags into a new order (above or below a row, a line showing where) or moves with Alt+↑/↓, each a Tab stop told how to move and a keyboard move heard ("Moved Inbox to position 2 of 5"); it works with `animate`. `TestApp::drag` and `TestApp::scroll` drive drags and scrolling in tests ([dynamic](docs/reference/dynamic.md)).
- **Lists that load as they scroll, and debounced states** (zgx, zgx-script): `ui.list { on_end = fn, end_distance = 200 }` (`View::on_end` in Rust) calls `fn` as the list's end comes in reach, once each time and again once its rows grow, filling a short view by itself, with no script running while it scrolls; `ui.paged(load(cursor) -> { items, next })` gives `items`, `loading`, `status`, `done`, `error` (a `UiError`), `more()` and `reload()`, keyed like `ui.resource` and cancelling a load superseded; `ui.debounced(state, ms)` follows `state` once it has settled, on one timer ([state](docs/reference/state.md#pages-and-debouncing)). A script node is also 16 bytes smaller (a list's rarely set options are boxed).
- **Handlers that await, and optimistic writes** (zgx-script): `ui.async(f)` is a handler running `f` as a task each call (a save on a click, a toast's Undo), cancelled as its owner goes, an uncaught error reported once. `ui.optimistic(state, next, save)` writes `next` now, keeps it if `save` succeeds and puts back what it replaced if not, returning the error; overlapping calls never revert over a newer write ([state](docs/reference/state.md#host-calls-with-results)).
- **A 3D texture decoded from a file keeps the file, not its pixels**: `Texture::decode` holds the PNG or JPEG and its decoded pixels only until a renderer has uploaded them, decoding again only if another upload needs them (a lost device, another window's renderer). Uploads share the decoder's pixels and build mipmaps without copying the full-size level. DamagedHelmet's live heap: 64.8 → 17.2 MB.
- **A failure is a typed error, not only text** (zgx-script): `ui.try`'s error, a promise's `error`, `:after`'s `on_error` and what `:await()` raises are a `UiError` with `message`, `kind` (`"raised"`, `"action"`, `"network"`, `"cancelled"`, `"http"`, `"io"`), an HTTP failure's `status` and `body`, and a raised value kept as `value`. `ui.fetch(url, { raise = true })` rejects a status outside 2xx as an `"http"` error. It is still text: `tostring`, `..`, `{err}`, string methods, a state set to it, a toast's `title`/`body` and props read its message. Code comparing it with `==` to a string, or checking `type(err) == "string"`, should read `err.message` or `err.kind`; under strict types, a state of strings is set to `err.message` ([state](docs/reference/state.md)).
- **Forms filled from what is loaded** (zgx-script): `ui.form { values = state }` fills a form from a loaded record and follows it, a field the person has edited keeping what they typed; a form and its fields have `dirty`; `submit` and `on_success` are handed the form. Checkboxes, sliders, steppers, segmented controls and selects can be controlled as React's are (`value = function`, `on_change`). `response.json` is typed `any` and `form.reset` takes any record. `TestApp::fill(id, text)` replaces a field's text. [The settings example](crates/zgx-script/examples/settings) shows them together ([forms](docs/reference/controls.md#forms)).
- **Decoding a PNG or JPEG holds about the image once, not twice**: `zgx_gpu::assets::decode_image` decodes into the image's final buffer, widening RGB and grey to RGBA in place, rather than into a buffer the image was then copied out of. A decode's peak: PNG 2.00 → 1.09 images, JPEG 2.00 → 1.36 (zune-jpeg's own colour planes). For `ui.image` and 3D textures alike; under the system allocator, fewer freed large regions kept (a 3D model's three 2048 textures: 35.6 → 23.6 MB).
- **Normal and metal-roughness maps in 3D scenes**: `World::set_map(id, Map::Normal | Map::MetalRoughness, texture)` (zgx-3d; `Map::BaseColour` is `set_texture`'s). A normal map tilts a surface by its texels (glTF's convention: +x along u, +y up the texture), its frame taken from the screen's derivatives, so meshes need no tangents. A metal-roughness map scales the material's factors (blue metal, green roughness). glTF models bring theirs, in Rust and in `ui.model`. A scene without textures still binds none and costs what it did.
- **Textures in 3D scenes**: `Texture` (zgx-3d), made from RGBA8 pixels or a PNG or JPEG, and `World::set_texture(id, texture)` colour a mesh across its texture coordinates, mipmapped and filtered. glTF models bring their base colour textures, in Rust and in `ui.model`. A scene without textures binds none and costs what it did. GPU-view painters get `zgx_gpu::viewport::texture_with_levels`: a texture written by the CPU in place on a Mac with unified memory, never copied. A copy into a texture would keep some 160 MB of Apple's driver memory while copies come ([3D scenes](docs/reference/3d.md#model)).
- **Localized messages** (`zgx::i18n`): ICU MessageFormat messages from JSON locale files, the locale negotiated from what the app asks or the system's preferred languages, and each message followed, so a locale switch or an edited file updates only the texts that changed. Plurals and numbers come behind the `i18n` feature, dates behind `i18n-dates`, both off by default (on in zgx-run). In scripts, `ui.i18n { dir = "locales", source = "en" }` loads a directory of locale files (a bundle ships them), `ui.t(key, args)` is a message as text or a label, and `ui.locale` reads and sets the locale. Every widget's label takes a message or a state and follows it: tabs, selects, modals, menus and the other components name their parts in the current locale.
- **A window's glass and light or dark change while it shows**: `WindowHandle::set_glass(Some(glass))` replaces its glass (macOS's effect view behind the content, Windows 11's system backdrop) and `set_glass(None)` removes it. `WindowHandle::set_theme(Some(appearance))` shows it light or dark whatever the system shows, title bar included, and `None` follows the system again. *On Windows, holding through a change of the system's needs the repository's winit ([getting started](docs/getting-started.md)).* Both were fixed when the window was made.
- **Theme tokens as typed fields** in scripts: `ui.tokens.notes.wash` is `"$notes.wash"`, built from the applied theme, so an editor completes an app's tokens and luau-lsp checks them. `zgx lint`'s `unknown-token` rule names a token nothing declares (a misspelled `"$app.crad"` read as transparent before).
- **A key binding can name a physical key**: `KeyBinding::code("Numpad1", modifiers, action)` matches the key with that physical code (the DOM's `KeyboardEvent.code`) whatever the layout makes it, so the keypad's 1 is apart from the digit row's.
- **A select's options follow a state** (`ui.select { options = state }`, Rust `select_live`): relabelled in place in a new locale, added and removed by their value.
- **glTF models in Luau: `ui.model { src, fit }`** shows a `.glb` or `.gltf` in a 3D scene. It's read off the main thread and shows once read, `fit` scales it to that many units across, and it hears clicks and hovers as a group does. A changed file reloads the script, as an image does ([3D scenes](docs/reference/3d.md#model)). It's zgx-script's `gltf` feature, on in zgx-run (48.5 KiB of a build for shipping).
- **Read-only shared state for plugins** (zgx-script): `ScriptHost::expose_shared_read(key, capability)` lets plugins read and follow a shared key without writing it. A plugin's `set`, `update`, `push` and the rest on it, or binding it where it is written, is an error naming the key; the app's scripts write it as before ([plugins](docs/plugins.md#what-plugins-see-of-the-app)).
- **glTF models in zgx-3d** (its `gltf` feature): `Model::from_bytes` reads a `.glb`, or a `.gltf` whose buffers are inline; `Model::from_bytes_with` reads one whose buffers sit beside it. A model brings its default scene's node tree, its meshes' triangles (smooth normals made where a mesh has none) and its materials' metallic-roughness factors, blending and unlit. `World::add_model` adds it as one group, with its base colour, metal-roughness and normal textures. Emission and occlusion textures, animations and skins are not read yet. `cargo run -p zgx-3d --features gltf --example model -- FILE` shows one.
- **A plugins example and guide** (zgx-script): `examples/plugins`, a task list two plugins extend with before/after, a replacement, a filter, an `@app` module override and a shared package, its host deciding what they see; [plugins.md](docs/plugins.md) covers making an app hookable, `hook = false`, the trust model of `Override`, and `ui.shared`/`expose_action`. A `ui.show`'s or `ui.switch`'s element can now be replaced or filtered, and `zgx check` resolves `@app/…` and `@app-original/…` against the app's directory.
- **Shadows in 3D scenes**: `World::set_shadows(true)` (zgx-3d), or `shadows = true` on `ui.scene3d`, makes the sun (the first directional light) cast shadows from opaque meshes, filtered soft at their edges. Off by default, it costs nothing. On, it costs a 2048 by 2048 depth map (16 MB) and a depth pass that's drawn again only when meshes or the sun move, not the camera. In the Luau demo, orbiting: +2% GPU and +0.7% CPU ([3D scenes](docs/reference/3d.md#scene3d)).
- **Clicking and hovering 3D objects**: `World::pick(point, size)` (zgx-3d) says which mesh a point of the view's box shows, where on it and how far from the camera (`Hit`). It's found on the CPU from the meshes' triangles, so a world never picked costs nothing. In Luau, `ui.mesh` and `ui.group` take `on_click(hit)` and `on_hover(over)`; a group hears its meshes', and a drag (orbiting) clicks nothing ([3D scenes](docs/reference/3d.md#mesh)).
- **Input modes, as HTML's `inputmode`**: `View::input_mode(InputMode::Email)` (`Text`, `Decimal`, `Numeric`, `Tel`, `Search`, `Email`, `Url`, `None`), `EditorHandle::set_input_mode`, and Luau's `inputmode = "email"`, say what kind of text a field takes. It is a hint, never a check. On Windows it is the field's input scope (through the Text Services Framework, the default; not with `ZGX_TSF=0` or `text_services(false)`), which the touch keyboard and input methods follow, and `None` shows no touch keyboard. Windows input methods also reconvert committed text in place (select it, press Convert or Hanja).
- **macOS: an opaque GPU view gets a layer of its own** (`GpuView::set_opaque(true)`): where its box shows it whole and alone, the painter draws straight into a `CAMetalLayer`, and zgx no longer copies it into the window's frame. On an M5 Max the system's GPU power about halves, level with wgpu's own, for about 1–2 points more app CPU, about 4 more of the window server's (as wgpu's) and about 20 MB. It never makes the main thread wait for a drawable: a thread of the plane's own waits for its drawables. It shows every refresh at 120 Hz on an M5 Max and an M1 Max, 19 and 27 ms from a frame's start. A box moved, clipped, faded or drawn over goes back into the window's frame in the same transaction, so no frame shows the view nowhere. The extra CPU is macOS's own for presenting a `CAMetalLayer`, so it stays opt-in: views not marked opaque, and zgx-3d's, composite as before ([GPU views](docs/gpu-views.md#a-layer-of-its-own-macos)).
- **Plugin hooks** (zgx-script): a plugin adds before or after any element of the app's, or in its place, without a slot: `ui.contribute { target = "#save", after = node }`. A target is an `id`, a declared `hook = "name"`, or a role and a name as the app writes them; `private = true` takes an element out of reach. Replacing needs `Capability::Override`, `ScriptHost::expose_hook` makes a target ask a capability, and what a plugin adds is described to assistive technology as the plugin's ([plugin hooks](docs/design/plugin-hooks.md)).
- **Plugin filters** (zgx-script): `ui.filter { target = "#title", text = function(text) … end }` passes an element's text, label, hidden, disabled, tooltip, a `ui.each`'s or `ui.list`'s items or its children's order through a plugin's function, again when what it reads changes; it needs `Capability::Override` ([plugin hooks](docs/design/plugin-hooks.md#filtering)).
- **Module overrides** (zgx-script): a plugin trusted with `Capability::Override` replaces one of the modules the app requires as `@app/…`: `ui.override { module = "@app/components/card", with = "./card" }`, the replacement reaching the original as `@app-original/…`. The replacement runs as the app's own code, so the grant is a trust grant.
- **Packages** (zgx-script, zgx-run): `ScriptHost::package("kit", dir)`, or zgx.json's `"packages"`, lets an app require modules from a folder outside its directory, as `@kit/…` or through a `.luaurc` alias into it; read only, and plugins reach none. `build::bundle` brings the packages its zgx.json lists into the bundle.
